# Audio profiles

What every Techguns sound is like, so that original GregGuns replacements can fill the same role.
Generated by [`tools/audio-describe`](../../tools/audio-describe/README.md). Each sound was measured (loudness, envelope, spectrum, stereo), rendered as a spectrogram and a waveform, and described by Claude (`claude-sonnet-5-5`) from that evidence.

> [!NOTE]
> Claude can't take audio input. The descriptions are read from the spectrogram, the waveform and the measurements, not heard. The measured columns are exact; the prose is an informed reading, with a confidence level for each sound. **Peak** and **RMS** are dBFS; **LUFS** is EBU R128 integrated loudness. The *volume the code passes* is in each area's `sounds.md`.

Coverage: 319 of 319 sounds described.

## 01-core

| Sound | Event | Sounds like | Peak | RMS | LUFS | Length | Attack | Decay to −20 dB |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sounds/test.ogg` | test | A loud, low-heavy noisy explosion blast that hits almost instantly and rumbles away over about 1.2 seconds. | 2.5 | -10.9 | -11.2 | 1.23 s | 0.03 s | 0.69 s |

### `sounds/test.ogg`

**A loud, low-heavy noisy explosion blast that hits almost instantly and rumbles away over about 1.2 seconds.** *(explosive, noisy, boomy, rumble, distorted, impact, low-heavy; confidence high)*

- **Measured:** 1.23 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 2.5 dBFS, RMS -10.9 dBFS, -11.2 LUFS, true peak 2.6 dBTP; centroid 1259 Hz, flatness 0.044; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** very loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned and attenuated by the game engine, which suits a spatial point-source explosion with distance falloff.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Attack about 0.03 s, peak at 0.03 s. Holds high to about 0.3 s, then steps down around 0.3 s. Falls to -20 dB by about 0.69 s and -40 dB by about 1.16 s. Short tail ends about 0.04 s of silence before the file ends.
- **Frequency profile:** Bass-heavy broadband noise: about 12% sub, 33% bass and 28% low-mid, with 16% mid. Presence and air are small (about 10% combined). Centroid is about 1.26 kHz and 85% rolloff about 2.6 kHz. Flatness is 0.044, so the spectrum is tonally dense despite the noise texture, with a dominant low-end rumble. The upper noise decays quickly and a lowpass cut sits at about 10-12 kHz.
- **Role:** Explosion sound, such as TNT or a blast. The file is named test, so it may be a placeholder or test sound, but the evidence points to an explosion effect.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Near-instant blast attack, a broadband burst that peaks at 0.03 s and clips slightly.; 0.05–0.45 s Dense, loud low-frequency boom and noise body, strongest below about 250 Hz with a bright noisy upper layer.; 0.45–1.19 s Rumbling decay tail; highs fade first and the sound narrows toward bass and low-mid noise until it vanishes.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer a sharp noise-burst transient (about 30 ms) with a deep sub boom around 40-100 Hz and a mid-heavy noise body lowpassed to about 3-5 kHz. Add a rumbling tail with a 1.0-1.2 s exponential decay. Use mild saturation for grit. Keep it mono. Target about -11 LUFS with the peak at or below -1 dBFS; the original clips, which should be avoided.

### `sounds/guns/ak47_reload.ogg`

**A mechanical AK-47 reload made of clicky metallic handling sounds: magazine release and rattle, a heavy thunk around 1.1s, a pause, then a second cluster of clacks ending in a loud bolt/charging slap near 1.87s with a ringing tail.** *(transient, metallic, mechanical, clicky, noisy, bright, foley; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.78 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -2.8 dBFS, RMS -26.4 dBFS, -21.0 LUFS, true peak -2.4 dBTP; centroid 5935 Hz, flatness 0.0; 15 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be spatialised by the engine as a point source at the player, so it should stay compact and dry.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Sparse and transient-driven. Several sharp attacks with decays of about 0.06s to -20 dB and about 0.4s to -40 dB. There is a quiet bed from 0.65 to 1.0s, a big impact at about 1.1s, a silent gap from about 1.45 to 1.62s, then a final peak at 1.87s. A decaying tail with small bumps runs to about 2.65s, then 0.15s of silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Bright and mid-forward. The centroid is about 6 kHz, with 85% rolloff near 9.5 kHz. Energy is mostly presence (39%), air (27%) and mid (26%), with almost no sub or bass content in the measured balance. The clicks have a 1 kHz body and strong 2-5 kHz content. The late tail has a horizontal tonal line near 2.3-3 kHz, and the low pulses below 100 Hz in the tail are faint. Very low flatness reading is likely a measurement quirk; the sound is noisy but impact-based.
- **Role:** Player AK-47 reload sequence in a gun mod: magazine out, magazine in, then bolt/charging handle. The Qwen notes about gunshots at 10s of intervals conflict with the 2.8s duration and the measurements, so they are disregarded; the metallic character and lack of pitch movement agree.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Tiny initial tick as the reload begins; 0.09–0.30 s Two or three sharp metallic clicks, probably the magazine release and mag drop, with a bright 1 kHz and 2-5 kHz body; 0.38–0.65 s Isolated light click at 0.38s followed by quiet low-level rustling and handling noise; 0.65–1.00 s Very quiet sustained friction or rattle bed with low rumble; 1.06–1.40 s Loudest impact of the first half, a broadband metallic thunk or magazine seating with a bright noisy burst and short decay; 1.45–1.62 s Near-silent gap; 1.63–1.82 s Rapid cluster of 3-4 small metallic clacks, perhaps a rod or bolt being pulled back, with some low thump; 1.82–1.95 s Loudest hit of the file (peak 1.87s), a heavy bolt slam or charging-handle release, broadband and bright; 1.95–2.65 s Ringing metallic tail with a sustained tone near 2.3-3 kHz and periodic low pulses, followed by faint late clicks at 2.03-2.55s
- **Recreation notes:** Make about 2.8s total. Layer a magazine release click, a mag rattle, a heavy metal-on-metal mag insertion thunk at about 1.1s, and a gap of about 0.15s. Then add 3-4 light clacks, a loud bolt slam at about 1.87s with a short ringing metallic tail, and small late clicks. Keep the sound mono and dry, with a peak near -3 dBFS and about -21 LUFS integrated. Avoid gunshot-like booms and keep the low end minimal.

### `sounds/guns/ak47_shot_a.ogg`

**A single heavy, low-weighted rifle shot with a sharp instant attack, a boomy noisy body and a long reverberant decay tail fading by about 1.2 s.** *(transient, boomy, noisy, gunshot, reverberant, low-heavy, punchy, mono; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.50 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -2.8 dBFS, RMS -18.1 dBFS, -18.4 LUFS, true peak -2.7 dBTP; centroid 837 Hz, flatness 0.0; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned and attenuated by the game engine, so it localizes cleanly as a point source and is cheap to spatialize. Any width would need to come from engine reverb.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Near-instant attack (about 0.01 s), no sustain, then a steady exponential decay: -20 dB by about 0.56 s and -40 dB by about 1.0 s. The tail is inaudible after about 1.2 s, with about 0.45 s of trailing silence in the file.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated below about 1.4 kHz (85% rolloff), with a centroid near 840 Hz. Sub (about 19%), bass (about 20%) and low-mid (about 18%) dominate, giving a thumpy, boomy body. Highs are only present in the first 0.2 s as a brief crack and then fall away. The spectrum is broadband and noise-like, with no steady tonal lines, and the tail is progressively darker.
- **Role:** Player-fired AK-47 rifle shot (guns.ak47Fire). It is one variant ('a') of several, so it is played at random for a varied fire sound. The Qwen notes about a splash are not supported by the spectrogram, which shows only a single decaying blast and tail.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Sharp muzzle-blast crack with broadband burst reaching up to the highs, peak at 0.01 s; 0.05–0.40 s Dense, boomy body with strong sub and bass energy and a noisy mid-range that darkens quickly; 0.40–1.20 s Reverberant or echoing decay tail, low rumble gradually fading into silence; no distinct second event
- **Recreation notes:** Build it from three layers: (1) a short, sharp noise or crack transient of about 20-50 ms for the muzzle snap, (2) a low thump or boom body with sub content around 40-150 Hz and a fast pitch-down, lasting about 0.3 s, and (3) a medium-sized room or outdoor reverb tail of about 0.8-1.0 s that is darker than the body. Total length is about 1.2-1.5 s with a quick fade at the end. Peak around -3 dBFS, integrated loudness around -18 LUFS. Make 2-3 slightly varied versions for repeated fire. Keep it mono.

### `sounds/guns/ak47_shot_b.ogg`

**A single close, bass-heavy rifle shot with a sharp noisy crack that decays into a rumbling, reverberant tail over roughly one second.** *(transient, noisy, boomy, gunshot, low-end, reverberant, percussive; confidence high)*

- **Measured:** 1.50 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -3.2 dBFS, RMS -18.6 dBFS, -18.6 LUFS, true peak -2.4 dBTP; centroid 900 Hz, flatness 0.0; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. This suits a positioned 3D in-game source and avoids phase problems, and the engine can pan it by the shooter's location.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Very fast attack of about 0.01 s to a peak of -3.2 dBFS. Rapid initial decay, reaching -20 dB at about 0.55 s and -40 dB at about 1.02 s. Smooth tail fading to silence by about 1.1 s, with about 0.4 s of trailing silence in the file.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the sub, bass and low-mid ranges (about 66% combined below roughly 500 Hz), with the centroid around 900 Hz and 85% rolloff near 1.5 kHz. The texture is noise-like and broadband in the first 0.3 s, with bright energy up to about 5-10 kHz that fades quickly. The centroid falls from about 1050 Hz to about 440 Hz over the file, so the sound darkens as it decays. The faint peaks at 10-16 kHz are likely encoding artifacts or very quiet hiss.
- **Role:** Player-fired AK-47 rifle shot, one variant ('b') of several fire sounds used to avoid repetition.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Instant broadband crack and muzzle blast with the peak at about 0.01 s, strongest in the bass and low-mids and extending bright up to a few kHz.; 0.05–0.40 s Dense noisy body with a booming low-frequency thump (sub and bass) and a fading mid/high noise wash.; 0.40–1.10 s Reverberant rumble tail that darkens and fades, with only low-frequency energy remaining.; 1.10–1.50 s Near-silence, with only faint residual decay and then trailing silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer a sharp noise-burst crack (about 20-50 ms, bright up to 5 kHz) over a low thump or sub-boom (about 50-120 Hz, 150-300 ms) and a short filtered-noise body. Add a darkened, short room-style reverb tail so the total is about 1.0-1.2 s with a smooth fade. Keep it mono with a peak around -3 dBFS and about -18 to -19 LUFS integrated. Make several slightly varied versions to avoid repetition, and avoid any tonal pitch or sweep.

### `sounds/guns/ak47_shot_c.ogg`

**A single heavy rifle shot with a loud, bass-heavy noisy crack that decays into a short reverberant tail over about 1 second.** *(gunshot, transient, noisy, boomy, reverberant, low-end, explosive; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.50 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -3.7 dBFS, RMS -18.9 dBFS, -19.4 LUFS, true peak -3.7 dBTP; centroid 817 Hz, flatness 0.0; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. This suits a positional 3D weapon sound, where the game engine handles panning and distance attenuation.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Near-instant attack, peaking at about 0.08 s. Steady decay: -20 dB by about 0.4 s and -40 dB by about 0.93 s. The tail fades out by about 1.1 s, followed by roughly 0.4 s of silence. The Qwen notes mention two shots, but the measurements show no further onsets, so this is one shot.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the sub and bass range (20-300 Hz), with a thick low-mid body and moderate mids. The spectrogram shows broadband noise reaching up to 10 kHz in the first 0.3 s, then the top end falls away. Centroid is about 817 Hz and 85% rolloff is about 1.3 kHz. Presence and air are weak (under 5%). The 'flatness 0.0' reading is probably a measurement quirk, because the spectrogram looks noise-like, not tonal. The isolated peaks near 10-16 kHz are faint artifacts, not musical tones.
- **Role:** Player-fired AK-47 rifle shot (guns.ak47Fire), one variant (c) of several used for variation.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.25 s Main muzzle blast: dense broadband noise with strong sub and bass weight, peaking at about 0.08 s.; 0.25–0.90 s Decaying body and reverb tail: mid and low energy fading steadily, with a fluttery, grainy texture and a darkening top end.; 0.90–1.08 s Very faint residual rumble dropping toward silence.; 1.08–1.50 s Silence (trailing, about 0.42 s).
- **Recreation notes:** Build it from about 1.0-1.1 s of audio. Layers: (1) a sharp noise-burst crack of 20-50 ms, (2) a low-frequency boom or thump at about 50-150 Hz with a fast pitch drop, (3) a mid-band noisy body for about 0.3 s, and (4) a short, dark room or reverb tail fading out by about 1 s. Roll off the highs above about 8 kHz over time. Target a peak around -3 to -4 dBFS, RMS around -19 dBFS and about -19 LUFS. Keep it mono, and make a few pitch or layer variations to match the sibling variants.

### `sounds/guns/alienblaster_reload.ogg`

**A roughly 3-second sci-fi weapon reload: low rumbling mechanical handling with a few sharp clicks early on, a louder clack near 1.4s, then a fading mid-range electronic whirr or charge-up and a quiet tail.** *(mechanical, electronic, rumbly, clicky, transient, noisy, sci-fi, layered; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.97 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -4.5 dBFS, RMS -25.7 dBFS, -25.4 LUFS, true peak -4.3 dBTP; centroid 1974 Hz, flatness 0.002; 10 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. Plays as a centred, positionless sound, so in game it needs engine panning and attenuation to sit at the player's weapon.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** No single attack. The first 0.1s fades in from near silence, then there are bursts of activity with small peaks at about 0.2-0.6s. The main peak is at 0.58s (-4.5 dBFS), and a second strong transient comes near 1.4s. A mid-level whirr sustains from 1.5 to 2.2s, then drops off and fades out by about 2.9s. The decay to -40 dB takes about 2.3s.
- **Frequency profile:** Dominated by sub and bass (about 60% of energy, below roughly 250Hz) with a thumpy, rumbly body. Mid energy at 500Hz-3kHz comes from the clicks and the whirr, which has a granular texture. Some very high content around 15-17kHz appears in the click strokes. Spectral flatness is very low, but the spectrogram looks mostly broadband and noisy, so treat this as a measurement quirk from the strong low end. The centroid swings between about 240Hz and 2kHz depending on whether low rumble or clicks dominate.
- **Role:** Reload sound for the alien blaster gun, played for the player: handling and clip release, a lock-in clack, then an energy charge or recharge whirr.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.30 s Low rumbling handling noise with soft onset as the weapon is moved or opened.; 0.34–0.66 s Two sharp clicks at about 0.34s and 0.6s, with bright vertical strokes reaching high frequencies, over a low thumpy body (latch or clip release).; 0.66–1.30 s Continued low rumbling and scuffing with a few small bumps, with energy dropping toward 1.2s (cartridge or part moving).; 1.36–1.55 s Loudest, brightest impact, a clack or snap at about 1.4-1.45s, probably the part seating or locking in.; 1.55–2.20 s Sustained mid-range, slightly warbling electronic whirr or charge, with bass around 150-250Hz and a granular texture around 1-3kHz that cuts off near 2.2s.; 2.20–2.97 s Quiet fading tail with little energy left, only faint low rumble and hiss.
- **Recreation notes:** Build in about 3s with three layers: (1) low-mid mechanical handling thumps and rattles, with a body around 80-200Hz; (2) a few sharp clicks at about 0.35s and 0.6s, plus a bigger clack at about 1.4s, with a metallic or plastic snap and some high-end sparkle; (3) a rising or steady electronic charge whirr from 1.5s to 2.2s, centred at 300Hz-2kHz with slight modulation, then a quick fade. Aim for an integrated loudness of about -25 LUFS and a peak near -4 to -5 dBFS. Keep it mono and leave the tail quiet.

### `sounds/guns/alienblaster_shot_a.ogg`

**A heavy, noisy sci-fi blaster shot with a strong low-end thump and a quick downward zap sweep on top, followed by a long, rumbling reverb-like tail.** *(blast, noisy, boomy, electronic, sci-fi, sweep, long tail, weapon; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.65 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -4.1 dBFS, RMS -21.7 dBFS, -20.9 LUFS, true peak -3.8 dBTP; centroid 1605 Hz, flatness 0.002; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono file. It will sit at the center or be positioned by the game engine, with no built-in width. This suits a player-fired weapon sound that is spatialised in game. The Qwen description of left-to-right movement conflicts with the mono file and is disregarded.. **Pitch:** falling. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack, peaking at about 0.11 s with no leading silence. Level drops about 8 dB over the first 0.3 s, reaches -20 dB at about 0.75 s and -40 dB at about 1.73 s. Long smooth tail, with the last 0.76 s silent.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is weighted to the lows: sub 21% and bass 16%, with low-mid and mid about 15% each, presence 10% and air 5%. Centroid is about 1.6 kHz, dipping to about 870 Hz in the second quarter as the tail darkens, then rising to about 2.6 kHz in the last quarter. The spectrum is dense and noisy with a bright descending sweep in the first 0.5 s. The low flatness value (0.002) is probably skewed by the strong low-frequency content. The perceived texture is noisy and gritty.
- **Role:** Player-fired alien blaster shot, the weapon fire sound for the guns.alienblasterFire event.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.30 s Impact burst: broadband noisy blast with heavy sub and bass thump and a bright diagonal zap sweeping downward from the top of the spectrum to about 1 kHz.; 0.30–1.20 s Body and decay: low rumble with fluttering mid texture and faint, weak tonal streaks around 1-2 kHz and 8 kHz, fading steadily.; 1.20–2.00 s Tail: dull, low-frequency rumble with pulsing, reverb-like decay while the highs fall away.; 2.00–2.65 s Near-silent trailing fade with only faint sub energy, then silence for the last 0.76 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a one-shot of about 2.0-2.6 s. Layer (1) a sub/bass boom or thump of 40-150 Hz with fast attack and about 0.5 s decay, (2) a noisy burst with a short downward laser or zap sweep from about 10 kHz to 1 kHz over 0.3-0.5 s, and (3) a tail of filtered noise or reverb that fades to silence by about 2 s. Target a peak of about -4 dBFS and about -21 LUFS integrated, and keep it mono.

### `sounds/guns/alienblaster_shot_b.ogg`

**A heavy, boomy sci-fi blaster shot with a fast descending laser-like zap at the start, a thick low-frequency body and a long noisy, reverb-like tail fading over about 2 seconds.** *(boomy, noisy, electronic, sci-fi, impact, distorted, long tail, weapon; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.76 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -4.4 dBFS, RMS -21.9 dBFS, -21.5 LUFS, true peak -4.4 dBTP; centroid 1376 Hz, flatness 0.0; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono file. It will be positioned by the game engine and has no built-in width. The Qwen description of left-to-right movement and stereo width conflicts with the mono measurement and is disregarded. It works as a centred player weapon sound, and any perceived space comes from the baked-in tail.. **Pitch:** falling. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Very fast attack (peak at about 0.03 s), then a slow, mostly steady decay: about -18 dB over the first 0.2 s, falling to about -33 dB by 0.6 s. It reaches -20 dB at about 1.5 s and -40 dB at about 2.0 s, with silence after about 2.07 s. Long, smooth tail with no distinct re-hits.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is weighted to the low end: sub 16% and bass 35%, with a centroid of about 1.4 kHz and 85% rolloff near 3.4 kHz. The spectrogram shows a bright low-frequency wash below 300 Hz, a diagonal falling zap line at the start, and a weak mid band around 450-1000 Hz. The top end is noisy and fades quickly. The 15-17 kHz peaks are likely encoder artefacts or faint noise, not a real tone. The texture is gritty and distorted, with low tonality.
- **Role:** Firing sound for the player's alien blaster weapon (guns.alienblasterFire), a variant of the shot sound, used for each trigger pull.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.40 s Initial blast: dense sub and bass thump with a bright, fast downward-sweeping zap line (several thousand Hz falling to roughly 400 Hz) and broadband noise.; 0.40–1.50 s Sustained body: rumbling low end with a faint band of mid resonances around 450-1000 Hz, gradually weakening and getting more grainy.; 1.50–2.10 s Decay tail: low rumble and hiss fade steadily, with the high end dying off first.; 2.10–2.76 s Near-silence; the last of the tail drops below audibility and the file ends with about 0.69 s of silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Aim for about 2-2.5 s total. Layer (1) a short 50-150 ms sub/bass thump with a punchy transient, (2) a fast descending sci-fi zap or pitch-swept saw/square from about 5 kHz down to about 400 Hz over 0.3-0.4 s, (3) a mid-heavy noisy or granular rumble that decays over about 1.5 s, and (4) a dark reverb-like tail. Add mild saturation for grit. Peak around -4 dBFS and about -21 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono or near-mono, and make a few pitch or tail variations so repeated shots feel less identical.

### `sounds/guns/alienblaster_shot_c.ogg`

**A heavy, boomy sci-fi gun blast with a bright descending zap at the start and a long, noisy, reverberant decay.** *(boomy, noisy, electronic, transient, blast, reverberant, sci-fi, weapon; confidence medium)*

The mid-highs are noisy and carry the descending zap streaks early on. The top end is sparse, and the high-frequency peaks listed (15-17 kHz) are probably codec artefacts. Dense, noise-like broadband texture overall. The brightness of the centroid rises in the last quarter only because the very quiet tail is mostly hiss.

Layer: (1) a punchy sub/low thump with a short attack at 40-120 Hz, (2) a fast downward laser sweep of roughly 8 kHz to 400 Hz lasting 0.2-0.3 s, (3) a filtered noise burst with a bit of grit or distortion for body, and (4) a short, dark reverb tail fading by about 1.8 s. Aim for a peak near -2 to -3 dBFS and about -20 LUFS integrated. Trim the silence at the end. Qwen's rise-then-fall pitch and 'rhythmic' claims are not supported by the spectrogram; the visible motion is mostly a fall.

### `sounds/guns/ar1_reload.ogg`

**A mono assault rifle reload made of several separate mechanical handling sounds (clicks, a rattly slide, a thunk and a snap) with gaps of silence between them.** *(mechanical, metallic, transient, noisy, clicky, reload, dry; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.09 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -3.0 dBFS, RMS -25.2 dBFS, -22.3 LUFS, true peak -3.0 dBTP; centroid 5155 Hz, flatness 0.004; 11 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono (single channel). This suits a positional first-person or 3D weapon sound, because it can be panned and attenuated by the engine without phase issues.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Multi-event, not a single envelope. The file opens almost immediately (0.01s leading silence) with soft clicks, then has silent gaps at about 0.27-0.36s and 1.15-1.49s. Each event has a sharp attack and a very fast decay (about 0.06s to -20 dB, 0.13s to -40 dB). The loudest hit is the magazine seat at about 0.95s. The tail is short, with 0.08s of trailing silence and no reverb.
- **Frequency profile:** Broadband and bright. The centroid is about 5 kHz, with the 85% rolloff near 10 kHz. Most energy is in the mids (about 41%), then presence (26%) and air (24%). There is almost no sub or bass content, apart from some low-mid thump on the insertion and rattle events. The spectrogram shows vertical strokes with speckled harmonics at about 1-4 kHz, typical of metal-on-metal and plastic impacts. Flatness is very low (0.004), which suggests tonal or resonant clacks rather than pure noise. Faint HF lines above 14 kHz are likely codec or artifact detail.
- **Role:** Player assault rifle reload sequence (guns.assaultRifleReload): mag out, mag in, then bolt or charging handle action. The listening notes about gunshots, key and tempo are not supported by the measurements and are probably invented. The match to a reload is supported by the file name, the event and the spacing of the clicks.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.27 s Magazine release or removal: a soft click at about 0.05s, then a stronger metallic clack around 0.13s with a short rattly decay.; 0.36–0.72 s Low-mid rattling scrape, likely the magazine sliding out or a hand fumbling. It is diffuse and noisy, with energy from about 200 to 1000 Hz and weaker clicks on top.; 0.82–1.15 s Magazine insertion: a soft pre-click at about 0.82s, then a loud, punchy seating clack at about 0.91-0.95s. This is the peak of the file, followed by a quick metallic decay.; 1.49–1.65 s Cluster of sharp clicks (about 1.49 and 1.57s) with a brief clacky tail, probably the bolt or charging handle being pulled.; 1.68–1.76 s Small, quiet, isolated tick or rattle.; 1.80–2.05 s Bolt release or slide forward: a louder, broad clatter at about 1.82-1.98s with a final small tick near 2.03s.
- **Recreation notes:** Build a roughly 2.0-2.1s one-shot from separate layers: (1) a mag-release click with a plastic snap at about 0.05-0.15s; (2) a soft low-mid slide or scrape at about 0.4-0.7s; (3) the main mag-seat impact at about 0.9s, with a metal clack plus a short low-mid thump, which should be the loudest point; (4) a bolt pull with two quick ratchet clicks at about 1.5-1.6s; (5) a bolt-forward clatter at about 1.8-2.0s. Leave silent gaps at about 0.27-0.36s and 1.15-1.49s to keep the rhythm. Keep it dry, with a very short decay on each hit. Aim for a peak of about -3 dBFS and about -22 LUFS integrated, with the mag seat as the loudest event and the bolt pull and release a few dB lower.

### `sounds/guns/ar1_shot_a.ogg`

**A single heavy, noisy assault-rifle shot with a loud broadband crack and thick low-end punch, followed by a long, dense, fading tail of rumble and reverb.** *(gunshot, transient, noisy, boomy, broadband, punchy, reverberant, decaying; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.23 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -0.9 dBFS, RMS -17.1 dBFS, -17.2 LUFS, true peak -0.9 dBTP; centroid 1800 Hz, flatness 0.0; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it is centered and easy to position in 3D. It will pan and attenuate with distance in game, and a stereo tail could be added by the engine or reverb.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Attack is about 10 ms, with the peak at 0.01 s. The level stays high for roughly 0.3 s (about -10 to -13 dB per tenth-second), then steps down. It reaches -20 dB at about 0.75 s and -40 dB at about 1.18 s, with a near-silent end at about 1.19 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is bass-heavy: sub 10%, bass 29%, low-mid 25%, mid 18%, presence 10%, air 6%. The centroid is about 1.8 kHz and falls from about 1.5 kHz to 1.2 kHz across the quarters, so the tail gets darker. The spectrum is broadband and noise-like with strong energy below 500 Hz and a rolloff near 4.4 kHz. The two faint peaks near 15-16 kHz are likely codec artifacts. The flatness reading of 0.0 is odd for such a noisy sound and probably a measurement quirk.
- **Role:** Player-fired assault rifle shot (guns.assaultRifleFire), a single round in a repeated fire sequence.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Sharp muzzle crack: near-instant attack with broadband noise reaching up to about 10 kHz and a strong low-frequency thump.; 0.05–0.40 s Body of the shot: dense bass and low-mid energy (roughly 20-500 Hz) with a fluttering, rough texture, and the level only slowly dropping.; 0.40–1.20 s Tail: rumbling reverb or room decay that darkens over time as the highs die away, and fades to silence by about 1.2 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer about 1.2 s total: (1) a short, bright noise crack of 10-30 ms with a fast attack; (2) a low thump or punch at 50-150 Hz lasting about 150-300 ms; (3) a mid-heavy noisy body lasting about 300 ms; (4) a dark reverb or room tail of about 0.8 s fading smoothly. Aim for a peak near -1 dBFS, RMS around -17 dBFS and about -17 LUFS. Make a few variations, since this file is the 'a' variant, and keep the tail short enough to avoid mush during full-auto fire. The Qwen notes mention explosions and shouting, which conflict with the measurements and look invented. The 'gritty, electronic' and 'no pitch movement' remarks are consistent.

### `sounds/guns/ar1_shot_b.ogg`

There is a hard lowpass near 10-11 kHz, with only faint content above it. The highs die away quickly while the lows linger. The texture is noise-like and dense with no steady tones, though the very low spectral flatness figure is likely a measurement quirk. The centroid is about 1.6 kHz and the 85% rolloff about 3.7 kHz.

- **Recreation notes:** Build it from 3-4 layers, about 1.2-1.4 s in total. (1) A sharp noise-burst crack of 10-30 ms with highs up to about 10 kHz. (2) A low thump or body layer, a pitched-down kick or sub boom at 50-150 Hz lasting about 150-300 ms. (3) A mid noise blast of 100-200 ms for the powerful report. (4) A reverb or room tail of about 1 s that rolls off the highs, fading out by about 1.2 s. Aim for a peak near -3 dBFS and about -17 to -18 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono, and make 2-3 slightly different variants to avoid repetition. Note that Qwen's 'rising pitch' claim is not supported by the spectrogram.

### `sounds/guns/as50_reload.ogg`

Silent gaps sit at about 3.4-4.1s, and the file ends with about 0.04s of trailing silence.

The hits also carry a strong sub-bass thump below 100 Hz, visible in the spectrogram. Flatness reads 0, which probably reflects the measurement more than a truly tonal sound; the texture is noisy and metallic. A brief tonal ring near 1 kHz appears at about 3.0-3.3s and 4.7s.
- **Role:** Player reload sound for the AS50 sniper rifle: handling, magazine out and in, and bolt or charge. The context and the multi-stage structure support this. Qwen's description of footsteps, gunshots and a clock is unreliable, though it does agree the sound is mechanical and percussive.
- **Elements:** 0.00–1.00 s Quiet handling noises: soft rustles, small clicks and low thumps (about 0.2-0.9s) as the weapon is moved and a part is released. The sub-bass pulses are gentle and rhythmic.; 1.20–1.70 s First big mechanical event: a bright clack or latch with a low thunky body, such as a magazine being pulled or a bolt being thrown. It has a noisy, wideband burst and a short tail.; 1.70–2.60 s Sparse small ticks and faint rustles at low level, a gap while the player handles the gun.; 2.60–3.30 s Second mechanical event: a rattly, clacky insertion or bolt movement building to a heavy thump near 2.9-3.1s, followed by a faint tonal ring around 1 kHz that fades by about 3.4s.; 3.40–4.10 s Near silence, with only a tiny click around 3.7s.; 4.15–4.95 s Final two loud metallic slams (about 4.2s and 4.6s): bright, noisy, with heavy low-end thump and a short ringing tail around 1 kHz. They probably represent the magazine seating and the bolt closing or charging handle.
- **Recreation notes:** Build about 5s of sequenced foley and layer each stage separately. Layers: soft cloth and gear rustle with small clicks; a heavy metal magazine release clack at about 1.3s; a sliding or insertion rattle with a low thud at about 2.9s; a hard magazine seat slam at about 4.2s; and a bolt close at about 4.6s with a short ringing metallic tail. Add sub-thump (40-100 Hz) under the main hits. Keep gaps of near silence between stages. Target peak around -3 dBFS, integrated loudness around -19 to -20 LUFS, mono. Keep the tails short, under 0.5s, and leave out reverb so it stays close and dry.

- **Envelope:** Near-instant attack (about 10 ms), peaking at -2.9 dBFS. Fast initial decay, about -14 dB within the first tenth of the file and -20 dB by 0.4 s. Long smooth tail reaching -40 dB at about 1.2 s, then digital silence for the last ~1.16 s. No secondary onsets.
- **Frequency profile:** Broadband noise burst. Bass (about 100-250 Hz) holds the most energy at 34%, with substantial low-mid, mid and presence content. The initial crack extends up to 10 kHz and above. Centroid falls from about 2 kHz in the first quarter to about 570 Hz in the last quarter as the highs die quickly and the tail turns to dull rumble. Spectral flatness is reported as 0.0 but the spectrogram looks noise-like, so treat that figure with caution. The peaks reported near 15-17 kHz are likely codec artifacts.

- **Recreation notes:** Build in three layers: (1) a sharp 10-30 ms noise or click transient for the crack, (2) a pitched-down body thump or boom around 80-200 Hz lasting 200-400 ms, (3) a smooth reverb or room tail of filtered noise fading over about 1 s. Keep the total length about 1.3-1.5 s, with no long silent tail. Target a peak around -3 dBFS and about -23 LUFS integrated. Make several variants with slight pitch and EQ changes. Keep it mono. The Qwen notes about a tunnel and a moving source are not supported by the measurements; it is a single shot with a natural decay.

### `sounds/guns/beamgun_start_c.ogg`

**A loud, rumbling low-frequency burst of gritty noise that surges for about a second and then fades into a long, dark tail, like a heavy energy weapon spinning up.** *(boomy, rumbling, gritty, noisy, low-end, energy, sustained, dark; confidence medium)*

It is centred on the player and stays consistent in-game, but it will sound narrow and may need a separate layer or spatialisation if width is wanted.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.

It falls 20 dB by about 0.58 s after the peak and 40 dB by about 1.03 s, with a gradual tail and about 0.33 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the sub (38%) and bass (40%) bands, with a centroid around 500 Hz and an 85% rolloff near 540 Hz. Low-mid, mid and presence content is thin but gives a gritty, hissy haze that is visible in the spectrogram up to about 10 kHz early on and fades later. Flatness is low (0.018), but there are no stable harmonic peaks, so it reads as a low rumble with a pitched or tonal weight rather than clear notes.
- **Role:** Start or charge-up sound when the player begins firing the beam gun, a heavy energy weapon spin-up or ignition.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.10 s Quick onset at moderate level with a first onset near 0.05 s, rising into the main body.; 0.10–0.70 s Dense, loud sub and bass rumble with a fluttering, granular texture and some upper-mid grit. The strongest swell lands near 0.44 s.; 0.70–1.25 s Rumble loses energy and brightness as it decays, leaving flickering low-mid modulation.; 1.25–1.83 s Very quiet tail with faint low residue, fading to silence in the last 0.33 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Make it about 1.5 to 1.8 s long. Layer a sub-bass swell or pulse (40 to 120 Hz) with a gritty filtered-noise or granular rumble in the 100 to 500 Hz range. Add a thin, bright crackle or energy sizzle on top, fading out by about 1 s. Peak the main swell at around 0.4 s, then fade smoothly with a short tail. Aim for a peak near -1 dBFS and about -14 LUFS integrated, and keep it mono. Any pitch rise is uncertain: the Qwen notes mention one but the measurements do not clearly show it, so treat it as optional.

### `sounds/guns/biogun_reload.ogg`

- **Recreation notes:** Build a roughly 3.0-3.4s one-shot reload in 4-5 steps: soft latch clicks at the start, a wet or squelchy slide or pump at about 0.5s, a cluster of crisp metallic or plastic snaps near 1.0s, a heavier thunk with a low-mid body near 1.1-1.5s, then a few light settling ticks. Add subtle organic or sci-fi texture such as a gel squish or a servo whir for the biogun theme. Keep it mono, with a peak around -4 dBFS and an integrated loudness of about -26 LUFS. ### `sounds/guns/boltaction_rechamber.ogg`

- **Recreation notes:** Trim the leading 0.42s of silence, or keep it only if the timing is deliberate. Layer about 0.5s of sound: a bolt lift with a light metallic click, a slide-back clack, a short pause, then a slide-forward rattle and a final lock click. Use close-miked metal-on-metal foley with a little steel ring and no low end below about 150 Hz. Peak around -3 dBFS, integrated loudness around -20 LUFS, and keep the tail very short.

### `sounds/guns/boltaction_reload.ogg`

Use metal-on-metal and light wood or plastic foley, high-passed at about 150 Hz with the body at 1-5 kHz and sparkle above 8 kHz. Keep each tail under 0.3 s with a clean ending. - **Elements:** 0.00–0.42 s Engine rumble fades in quickly from near silence and swells to its loudest point, with strong bass pulsing and rough harmonics.; 0.42–0.86 s Engine winds down: level falls steadily to silence, the pulses become more widely spaced and a lower steady pitch appears around 120-200 Hz, suggesting the revs dropping.

Layer a low sawtooth or pulse engine tone (about 60-200 Hz) with a chopped or tremolo amplitude at about 25-40 Hz for the pulsing, and add mid-range mechanical buzz or chain rattle up to about 1.2 kHz. Fade in over about 0.4 s, then ramp the pitch and amplitude down to silence. Add a very light high noise layer. Aim for a peak near -3 dBFS and about -13 LUFS integrated, and match the chainsaw loop's pitch and level at the start so it joins cleanly.

### `sounds/guns/chainsaw_loopstart.ogg`

**A short, low-pitched chainsaw engine rev-up swell: a gritty, rumbling motor burst that fades in, peaks around 0.45 s and fades out within 0.86 s.** *(mechanical, rumbling, buzzy, low-end, engine, pulsing, tonal, swell; confidence medium)*

Very low flatness (0.005) indicates tonal, harmonic content: a dense stack of harmonics from a rough engine fundamental, with vertical striations in the spectrogram showing rapid amplitude pulsing (engine firing). Little air or presence energy; a faint high-frequency haze.

- **Recreation notes:** Build a ~0.85 s one-shot: layer a low sawtooth or pulse engine tone (about 60-120 Hz fundamental, rising slightly in pitch then settling) with amplitude modulation around 25-40 Hz for the putter, add a gritty two-stroke buzz in the 200-1000 Hz region and a brief starter-pull click at the start. Fade in over about 0.4 s, hold briefly, then fade out over about 0.4 s; the tail should settle toward the pitch of the following loop so it blends. Target around -13 LUFS integrated, peak about -2.5 dBFS, and keep highs gentle (little above 3 kHz).

- **Recreation notes:** Make about 2.2s total in 3 beats: (1) a light latch/release click and rustle at 0-0.3s, (2) a cluster of metallic clacks plus a heavy canister clunk at about 0.9s, (3) a second, lower thump or seat at about 1.2s followed by 2-3 small lock clicks at 1.6-1.9s. Layer metal impacts, a body thud of 200-500 Hz, and a small pitched ping. Keep the gap around 1.1s. Aim for a peak around -4 dBFS and about -24 LUFS integrated, with a short tail.

### `sounds/guns/flamethrower_shot_a.ogg`

**A roughly 1.3 second burst of low, rumbling, noisy roar, like a gas jet or flame, that stays fairly steady and then cuts off with a short fade at the end.** *(noisy, rumbling, whoosh, boomy, sustained, fiery, low-end; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.30 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -4.5 dBFS, RMS -16.5 dBFS, -16.9 LUFS, true peak -4.5 dBTP; centroid 376 Hz, flatness 0.0; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned by the game engine for the player's shot, so it localises as a point source, and a mono file keeps distance attenuation and panning clean.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast swell over roughly the first 0.1-0.2s (the measured peak at 0.86s is just the largest random fluctuation in a steady noise, not a true slow attack). Sustains at a roughly constant level with ripple from 0.2 to about 1.05s, then decays about 20 dB by roughly 1.1s and 40 dB by about 1.2-1.3s, with essentially no reverb tail.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in bass (about 66%) and low-mid (about 22%), with some sub (about 6%). Centroid is about 376 Hz and the 85% rolloff is about 345 Hz. The spectrogram shows dense, bright, vertically streaked energy from 20 to about 300 Hz, thinning out through the mids and becoming dark above 5-8 kHz. The texture is a gritty, crackling turbulence rather than a tone. The flatness value of 0.0 is likely an artefact of the strongly low-weighted spectrum, not evidence of a tone, since the spectrogram shows broadband noise with no harmonic lines. The centroid dips to about 276 Hz in the last quarter as the sound darkens at the end.
- **Role:** Flamethrower firing sound for the player's weapon (guns.flamethrowerFire), a short burst of fire/gas jet played per shot or trigger pull. The 'a' suffix suggests one of several variants.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.20 s Quick swell as the roar fades in from a lower level (about -23 dB to -14 dB per tenth-of-duration envelope).; 0.20–1.05 s Sustained turbulent low-frequency roar with irregular, fluttery amplitude modulation, no distinct onsets or pitch.; 1.05–1.30 s Rapid drop-off as the jet cuts, ending in a short quiet tail of low rumble.
- **Recreation notes:** Build from a low-passed pink or brown noise roar (energy mostly 40-400 Hz, gentle roll-off above about 2 kHz) layered with a crackly, gritty mid texture for flame and a soft gas-hiss for the nozzle. Add irregular amplitude modulation (fast flutter, about 10-30 Hz) for turbulence and a subtle low thump or ignition 'whump' at the start. Make it about 1.2-1.3s: a quick 50-150 ms swell, a steady body, and a 150-250 ms fade at the end. Target a peak around -4 to -5 dBFS and about -17 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono and make several variants so repeated fire doesn't sound uniform. Ignore the language model's 'explosion' and 'pitch drop' claims, since the measurements show a steady noise with no clear pitch change.

### `sounds/guns/flamethrower_shot_b.ogg`

**A low, rumbling, roaring burst of noisy fire-like whoosh that holds steady for about a second, then fades out quickly.** *(noisy, rumbling, whoosh, roar, bassy, turbulent, sustained; confidence medium)*

- **Envelope:** Fast but not instant attack, peaking around 0.22 s. Sustains with slight fluctuation of about 3 to 4 dB until roughly 0.8 s. Decays to -20 dB by about 0.86 s and drops sharply to near silence by 1.3 s, with a very short tail.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the bass (about 63%) and low-mids (about 21%), with a centroid around 450 Hz and a rolloff near 345 Hz. The spectrogram shows a dense, bright band up to about 500 Hz with sparse, speckled mid content up to about 4 kHz and very little above that. The texture is noisy and turbulent, with a vertical streaky look from rapid amplitude modulation. The measured flatness of 0.0 is likely a measurement artefact given the broadband-noise look, and there are no clear tonal lines.
- **Role:** Flamethrower fire burst when the player shoots, a short gush of flame. The file is a variant (b) of the fire sound. The context supports this role, and the evidence agrees with a roaring gas or flame whoosh.

Target a peak near -5 dBFS and about -18 LUFS integrated. Make a few variants so repeated shots don't sound identical. The Qwen engine description fits the rumbling texture, but a vehicle is not the intended role, and its claim of a 10 s duration conflicts with the measured 1.3 s.

### `sounds/guns/flamethrower_shot_c.ogg`

**A continuous, low-frequency roaring rush of turbulent noise, like a flamethrower burst, that swells slightly early on and fades out over the last quarter second.** *(noisy, roaring, boomy, turbulent, sustained, low-end, whoosh, rumbly; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.10 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -4.7 dBFS, RMS -17.7 dBFS, -17.9 LUFS, true peak -4.6 dBTP; centroid 420 Hz, flatness 0.0; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono file. It will be positioned by the engine at the shooter, which suits a close, centered weapon sound. It has no built-in width, so spatial feel comes from in-game attenuation and panning.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Starts at full presence (about -20 dB in the first tenth) and swells to a peak at about 0.22 s. Holds a fairly steady plateau of roughly -17 to -19 dB until about 0.8 s, sags to about -24 dB near 0.9 s, then falls to about -43 dB by 1.1 s. It is active almost the whole time with no silence, and the tail is short at about 0.2 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Heavily bass-weighted: about 66% of energy is in the bass band and 23% in the low-mids, with a centroid near 420 Hz and 85% rolloff near 390 Hz. Energy is dense and broadband-noisy from about 20 Hz to 400 Hz, with a mottled, fluttering texture and a dimmer red haze up to about 8 kHz. The centroid drifts from about 590 Hz to about 370 Hz, so it gets darker toward the end. The very high peaks at 16-17 kHz are faint codec or low-pass artifacts. The reported flatness of 0.0 contradicts the noisy look, probably because of the measurement, so I trust the spectrogram.
- **Role:** Player flamethrower fire sound, one variant (c) of several, played when the weapon is fired. It is a short burst of flame whoosh. The listening model's description of an explosion with thunder is loosely consistent with the low rumble, but the measurements show no explosion transient.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.22 s Immediate onset of dense low roar that swells to its peak with no distinct click or transient.; 0.22–0.90 s Sustained turbulent rumble with irregular amplitude flutter, energy concentrated below about 400 Hz and a faint hissy upper layer.; 0.90–1.10 s Quick fade-out: level drops steeply and the roar thins to a quiet tail.
- **Recreation notes:** Make about 1.1 s total. Layer filtered pink or brown noise, low-passed around 400-600 Hz, with an amplitude flutter (fast random modulation, about 20-60 Hz) to give the turbulent gas-burst roar. Add a gentle sub thump at 40-80 Hz and a quiet airy hiss layer in the 2-6 kHz range at about -30 dB relative to the body. Use a soft swell of about 0.2 s, hold to about 0.85 s, then a quick fade of about 0.2 s. Target about -18 LUFS with peaks around -5 dBFS. Make 3 or more variants with different flutter patterns to avoid repetition.

### `sounds/guns/flamethrower_shot_d.ogg`

### `sounds/guns/flamethrower_shot_e.ogg`

### `sounds/guns/grenade_throw_a.ogg`

There are sparse high-frequency ticks and faint thin lines near 9 and 13 kHz, but they are weak. ### `sounds/guns/grenadelauncher_reload.ogg`

Open with a heavier clack at about 0.7s, then groups of 3-4 short clicks around 0.9-1.4s, 1.6-2.1s and 2.3-2.9s. Put the biggest clunk (breech lock) at about 3.2s, followed by a short rattle tail. Keep each hit under 100 ms with body around 500-1000 Hz and some click brightness up to 8 kHz. Aim for about -4 dBFS peak and about -28 LUFS integrated, with little reverb and a dry, mono result. - **Measured:** 2.01 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -2.2 dBFS, RMS -18.2 dBFS, -19.2 LUFS, true peak -2.1 dBTP; centroid 399 Hz, flatness 0.0; 1 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it is positioned entirely by the game engine's 3D panning and attenuation. That suits a player-fired weapon, but the long tail will not carry any built-in spatial width.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Very fast attack (peak at ~0.03 s), no real sustain, then a steady, slightly stepped decay. Level is about -16 dB through the first 0.3 s, -20 dB by about 1.1 s and -40 dB by about 1.8 s, with a long tail and about 0.08 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is dominated by sub and bass (about 32% and 43%), with low-mid at about 15% and very little above 2 kHz. Centroid is about 400 Hz, and the spectrogram shows bright low-frequency energy under 200 Hz that stays strongest for the first second. The texture is noise-like and dense, with high-mid content fading quickly after the first 0.5 s. The 16-17 kHz peaks are likely codec artifacts. The flatness value of 0.0 is probably a measurement quirk, since the spectrogram looks noisy rather than tonal.
- **Role:** Player handgun fire sound (guns.handgunFire), a punchy pistol shot with a heavy low end and a room-like tail. This is a variant (b) of the shot.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.12 s Initial muzzle blast: loud broadband crack with strong sub and bass thump, peak around 0.03 s.; 0.12–0.80 s Dense decaying body of the shot: low-mid rumble and noisy room or reflection texture, with bright speckled mid content gradually thinning out.; 0.80–1.40 s Reverb or echo tail with a small secondary bump near 1.0 s (onset detected at 1.02 s) and a faint high-frequency flick around 1.3 s.; 1.40–2.00 s Fading low rumble tail, darkening and dropping into near silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Build it in three layers. First, a short sharp transient or crack of 20-40 ms for the attack. Second, a low boom or thump at 50-200 Hz with a fast pitch-down, about 150-300 ms. Third, a noisy reverb or room tail of 1.5-2 s, low-passed progressively to about 2-4 kHz. Total length is about 2 s with a fast fade at the end. Aim for a peak near -2 dBFS, RMS around -18 dBFS and about -19 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono. Avoid much content above 5 kHz after the first 0.3 s. Make 2-3 slight variations so repeated shots don't sound identical.

### `sounds/guns/handgun_shot_c.ogg`

**A boomy, bass-heavy handgun shot with a loud low-frequency crack at the start, a long decaying reverberant tail, and a faint secondary clatter around 1.4 seconds.** *(transient, boomy, noisy, low-end, gunshot, reverberant, impact; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.29 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -2.4 dBFS, RMS -18.4 dBFS, -20.2 LUFS, true peak -2.4 dBTP; centroid 413 Hz, flatness 0.0; 3 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it will be positioned by the game engine. It suits a player-fired sound with 3D attenuation, and there is no built-in width.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack, peaking at about 0.05 s. Rapid fall of roughly 20 dB by 0.7 s, then a long tail reaching -40 dB at about 2.0 s. Small bump of secondary energy at 1.35-1.7 s. Ends at about 2.29 s with only a short trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is dominated by sub (31%) and bass (40%) bands below about 250 Hz, with a centroid around 410 Hz and an 85% rolloff near 495 Hz. The spectrogram shows noisy broadband content up to about 10 kHz in the first 0.4 s, then the highs die away and the tail is mostly low rumble. The flatness reading of 0 is likely a measurement quirk, since the sound is clearly noisy and not tonal. The 16-17 kHz peaks are faint codec artifacts.
- **Role:** Player handgun firing sound, one of several variants (shot_c), with a trailing shell-casing or room-tail element.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.30 s Main gunshot blast: sharp broadband transient with heavy sub and bass thump, brightest in the first 0.1 s.; 0.30–1.20 s Decaying body and room or tail: rumbling low-mid noise with a darkening spectrum, with a gap in the envelope near 0.5 s.; 1.30–1.70 s Secondary soft events (onsets at about 1.36 s and 1.44 s): low-pitched thuds or a shell casing bounce, with energy around 50-200 Hz.; 1.70–2.29 s Very quiet fading tail of low rumble, with highs gone, ending in near-silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer a sharp click or crack (1-5 kHz noise burst, about 30 ms), a low thump or boom (50-120 Hz, falling pitch, about 200 ms), and a noise body with a low-passed reverb tail of about 1.5-2 s. Optionally add a soft casing clink or low thud at about 1.4 s. Total length about 2.0-2.3 s. Aim for a peak near -2 dBFS and about -20 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono, with a fast attack and a smooth decay.

### `sounds/guns/lasergun_reload.ogg`

**A multi-part mechanical reload sequence of clacky, metallic handling clicks in three bursts, ending with a decaying electronic charge-up or latch with a faint high whine.** *(transient, mechanical, metallic, clicky, noisy, electronic, multi-part; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 3.05 s, 32000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -3.8 dBFS, RMS -30.4 dBFS, -25.6 LUFS, true peak -2.5 dBTP; centroid 4687 Hz, flatness 0.103; 12 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono (single channel). It will be positioned by the game engine, which suits a first-person player-attached reload sound.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Not a single envelope: three bursts separated by near-silence. Burst 1 (0.1-0.6s) is moderate with fast transients. A single tick lands at 0.78s. Burst 2 (1.55-1.85s) is the loudest, peaking at about 1.69s. Burst 3 starts abruptly at about 2.04s and decays over roughly 0.8s into a quiet tail, ending in silence by about 2.8s. Each transient decays in under 0.15s.
- **Frequency profile:** Mid-dominant (about 49% in the mid band), with presence and air each around 20%, and almost no sub or bass energy. The centroid is about 4.7 kHz and the rolloff about 9 kHz. The clacks are broadband and noisy with a metallic, woody-plastic character. Brightest peaks are at 0.8-3 kHz, with a thin narrow-band whine at 8-9 kHz in the tail.
- **Role:** Player reload sound for a laser gun: removing a cell, inserting a new one, and a final latch or charge-up. This is inferred from the event name, the three-stage structure and the electronic whine; Qwen's gunshot description conflicts with the measurements and is not trusted.
- **Elements:** 0.08–0.60 s Cluster of 4-5 sharp, broadband mechanical clacks (about 0.28, 0.36, 0.45, 0.53s), like a magazine or cell being removed and handled, with energy from 400 Hz to 8 kHz.; 0.76–0.80 s Single short, isolated click or tick, followed by faint low-level rustle and a weak mid-band resonance around 0.8-1.2s.; 1.55–1.85 s Loudest cluster of 3-4 heavy metallic clunks/clacks (1.58, 1.69, 1.80s) with strong 800 Hz-3 kHz energy, like a new cell being inserted and locked.; 1.95–2.85 s Final impact or latch at about 2.04s with a bright noisy body, then a decaying tail with a faint high-pitched whine near 8-9 kHz that rises slightly, like a charge-up settling; a small tick at about 2.53s.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a roughly 3 s one-shot in three stages: (1) 4-5 light plastic or metal clicks over 0.5s, (2) a gap with a single tick, (3) 3-4 heavier clunks then a final latch with a short 0.7s electronic charge-up and a faint 8 kHz whistle. Keep the low end below 150 Hz thin. Aim for a peak near -4 dBFS and about -25 LUFS integrated, with a quiet tail and clean silence at the end.

### `sounds/guns/lasergun_shot_a.ogg`

**A heavy, bass-laden sci-fi laser blast with a brief high-frequency downward zap at the start, dissolving into a rumbling noisy tail.** *(boomy, electronic, laser, sweep, noisy, bassy, one-shot, sci-fi; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.79 s, 44100 Hz, 2 ch; peak -3.4 dBFS, RMS -20.7 dBFS, -15.0 LUFS, true peak -3.4 dBTP; centroid 962 Hz, flatness 0.005; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Fairly narrow and mostly centered (L/R correlation 0.81, side-to-mid -9.7 dB), with some width in the texture. Because it is a player weapon shot, the centered image keeps it punchy and readable at the listener's position.. **Pitch:** falling. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Very fast onset, with the measured peak at about 0.24 s inside a loud body. It stays strong to about 0.3 s, then drops 20 dB by about 0.37 s and 40 dB by about 0.61 s. It fades to silence by about 0.9 s, and the rest of the file is empty tail.
- **Frequency profile:** Dominated by sub and bass, about 66% of energy below 250 Hz. The centroid is about 960 Hz and falls from about 1.5 kHz in the first quarter to about 220 Hz. Spectral flatness of 0.005 is low, which suggests tonal or low-frequency-heavy content, though the spectrogram shows a textured, noisy low band. Thin bright streaks early on come from the laser sweeps, and there is little air above 5 kHz.
- **Role:** Player-fired laser gun shot (guns.lasergunFire), a punchy sci-fi weapon report with a heavy low-end thump.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.10 s Sharp onset with a bright, thin downward zap sweeping from about 10 kHz toward the low mids, over a burst of broadband noise.; 0.00–0.45 s Dense body of sub and bass energy (roughly 20-300 Hz) with a gritty, noisy mid texture. This forms the weight of the shot.; 0.10–0.65 s A faint secondary falling sweep curves from about 5 kHz down to roughly 450 Hz, giving a pew-like pitch drop.; 0.45–1.00 s Decaying low rumble and fading noise tail, darker and quieter.; 1.00–1.79 s Near-silent residual tail, effectively inaudible; the measured trailing silence is about 0.89 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Build it from three layers. (1) A sub/bass thump or boom, 40-200 Hz, with a quick decay of about 0.4 s. (2) A filtered noise burst with a gritty mid texture, 0.3-0.5 s. (3) A fast descending synth zap sweeping from about 8-10 kHz down to 400 Hz over 0.1-0.5 s. Add a short low rumble tail. Keep the total length about 0.9-1.0 s with no long silence. Target a peak near -3 dBFS and about -15 LUFS integrated. Keep it near-mono with slight stereo width on the noise layer. The Qwen notes (sine tone, 10 s length) conflict with the measurements and were ignored.

### `sounds/guns/lasergun_shot_b.ogg`

**A short, bass-heavy laser gun shot: a punchy, gritty low-end blast with a fast descending zap sweep at the very start, decaying into a quiet tail.** *(electronic, boomy, noisy, gritty, punchy, zap, sci-fi, layered; confidence medium)*

The centroid is about 1.1 kHz, starting near 1.8 kHz and falling to about 330 Hz. Aim for a peak near -3.5 dBFS and about -15 LUFS integrated. Keep a slightly widened stereo image but a mono-compatible low end.

### `sounds/guns/lasergun_shot_c.ogg`

**A heavy, bass-laden laser gun shot with a fast downward chirp at the start, a dense noisy body that fades over about 0.6 s, and a long quiet tail.** *(laser, electronic, boomy, noisy, chirp, punchy, sci-fi, bassy; confidence medium)*

- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Moderately wide stereo (L/R correlation 0.77, side-to-mid -8.9 dB). The bass stays centered while the noisy top layers spread, giving a roomy shot that still locates well in game. Because it is a player weapon, keep the low end mono-compatible.. **Pitch:** falling. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Very fast attack with the loudest point near 0.1-0.2 s. Sustained, steady body until about 0.45 s, then decays: -20 dB by about 0.37 s and -40 dB by about 0.61 s. A quiet tail trails off by about 0.9 s, followed by roughly 0.66 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Most energy is low: sub 26% and bass 36%, with a centroid of about 1.1 kHz and an 85% rolloff near 2.2 kHz. The bright top-end chirp is brief. The spectral flatness figure of 0.007 conflicts with the visibly noisy, broadband texture, so the body should be treated as a thick, distorted blend of tone and noise rather than a pure tone. The centroid drops from about 1.3 kHz to about 200 Hz over the sound, so it gets darker and boomier as it decays.

Faint diagonal sweeps (falling) are visible in the 1-6 kHz range.; 0.50–0.70 s Fast decay as the energy falls from about -25 dB to -50 dB, with a weak falling sweep toward 500 Hz.; 0.70–1.00 s Faint low-frequency rumble and hiss tail fading to near silence. The file has no audible content after about 0.9 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Build 3 layers: (1) a short, bright, fast downward pitch sweep (about 10 kHz to 500 Hz over about 100-300 ms) as the laser zap; (2) a distorted, buzzy saw or noise body with a strong 40-150 Hz thump, held about 0.4 s and decaying by about 0.7 s; (3) a soft filtered noise or rumble tail to about 0.9 s. Trim the file to about 1.0 s. Aim for a peak near -3 dBFS and about -15 LUFS integrated. Add slight stereo widening on the upper layers only, and make 2-3 pitch variants for variety.

### `sounds/guns/lmg_reload.ogg`

**A multi-stage light machine gun reload of mechanical handling: a low thump, clicks and clacks, a mag-out and mag-in, a bolt or charging handle rack, then a final slap, with silence gaps between the stages.** *(mechanical, transient, metallic, clicky, layered, foley, noisy; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 5.72 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -0.8 dBFS, RMS -21.4 dBFS, -17.8 LUFS, true peak -0.8 dBTP; centroid 3492 Hz, flatness 0.004; 40 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono, so it sits as a centered point source. In game it can be spatialized or positioned on the player with no stereo width.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Many sharp transient attacks (a few ms) with fast decays of about 0.1-0.4 s each, separated by quiet gaps. The loudest peak is near 1.56 s. A near-silent gap sits at about 4.3-4.7 s. A short tail fades out by about 5.5 s, with 0.2 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Noise-like and broadband (flatness about 0.004 is low only because of the dense clicks). Energy is mostly mid (about 39%) and low-mid (about 22%), with presence about 20% and air about 12%. The centroid is about 3.5 kHz. There is some low thump at the start, and metallic click content extends up to about 7 kHz at the 85% rolloff.
- **Role:** Player LMG reload sound: a multi-step sequence covering mag or belt removal, insertion, and bolt or cover action.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.55 s Soft low-frequency thumps (sub 40-100 Hz) with a muffled handling feel, likely the weapon being shifted or the mag release being hit.; 0.55–0.85 s Cluster of sharp metallic clicks and clacks with broadband energy, probably the magazine or cover latch unlatching.; 0.90–1.20 s Faint, quiet ticks with a thin tonal pair near 1.5-2 kHz, a small metallic rattle or a spring.; 1.40–2.00 s Loudest event: a heavy, dense burst of mechanical clacks with strong low-mid body, like a magazine or ammo belt being slammed in or the cover closing.; 2.00–3.30 s Sparse smaller clicks and rattles at moderate level, belt or round handling and small adjustments.; 3.50–4.20 s Two or three distinct punchy clacks, probably a bolt or charging-handle pull and release, with some faint high-frequency ticks.; 4.30–4.70 s Near silence gap.; 4.70–5.50 s Final heavy clack pair, a bolt slam or lock-in, followed by a light rattle tail.
- **Recreation notes:** Build a roughly 5.5 s one-shot from separate foley layers: a soft low thump, metallic latch clicks, a heavy magazine or belt slam (the loudest at about 1.5 s), small rattles, a bolt rack, and a final lock clack. Keep gaps between the stages and leave a silent beat before the last action. Leave the tail short. Target about -18 LUFS integrated with peaks near -1 dBFS, mono. Keep the transients crisp and the low end modest.

### `sounds/guns/lmg_shot_c.ogg`

**A single heavy, bass-rich machine-gun shot with a noisy crack at the start, a booming body and a fading reverberant tail.** *(transient, boomy, noisy, explosive, broadband, gunshot, punchy, tail; confidence high)*

- **Measured:** 1.98 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 0.3 dBFS, RMS -17.3 dBFS, -17.4 LUFS, true peak 0.4 dBTP; centroid 1158 Hz, flatness 0.002; 2 onset(s).

Add a short, dark reverb or room tail of roughly 1 s that rolls off the highs quickly. Keep the total length to about 1.5-2 s, with most energy in the first 0.3 s. Aim for peaks near -1 dBFS, about -17 LUFS integrated, and avoid clipping. Make 2-3 slight variations (the file is named _c) so rapid fire does not sound repetitive.

### `sounds/guns/mac10_reload.ogg`

**A mechanical SMG reload made of three separated bursts of clicky metal handling sounds: a magazine drop and rattle, a magazine insertion with clacks, and a final bolt or slide rack.** *(mechanical, metallic, transient, clicky, noisy, handling, dry; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.64 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -1.3 dBFS, RMS -24.6 dBFS, -19.7 LUFS, true peak -0.8 dBTP; centroid 4202 Hz, flatness 0.143; 12 onset(s).

- **Frequency profile:** Broadband and click-like, centroid about 4.2 kHz, with most energy in the mids (44%) and presence (43%) bands, plus some air. Almost no sub or bass content in the measurements, though the spectrogram shows low-frequency smear under each transient, probably from impacts or codec spread. The texture is metallic and noisy rather than tonal, with flatness of about 0.14.
