### `sounds/guns/pdw_shot_c.ogg`

**A single boomy, bass-heavy gunshot with a noisy crack at the start and a long rumbling reverberant decay into a quiet tail.** *(gunshot, boomy, noisy, low-end, reverberant, transient, rumbling; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.26 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -1.9 dBFS, RMS -13.8 dBFS, -14.2 LUFS, true peak -1.8 dBTP; centroid 632 Hz, flatness 0.0; 1 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned by the game engine, so it works as a point-source shot. The player-fired event can sit centered without width issues.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack, peaking at about 0.07s. Held at a high level through roughly 0.3s, then steps down. A small bump near 0.5s. Falls 20 dB by about 0.59s and 40 dB by about 0.88s. Faint tail fades to silence by about 1.0s, with about 0.3s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Dominated by bass (about 51% of energy) and sub (about 17%), with a centroid near 630 Hz and 85% rolloff under 1 kHz. The spectrogram shows a strong bright low band below 200 Hz and a noisy, mottled mid and high band that fades within about 0.6s. The very top end is nearly empty; the quoted peaks at 16-17 kHz are faint artifacts. Flatness is reported as 0, but the texture looks noisy, so the figure is probably unreliable. Brightness drops over time, with the centroid falling from about 760 Hz to about 100 Hz.
- **Role:** Player-fired weapon sound for the PDW (personal defense weapon) gun, played on each shot. The Qwen notes suggest a large gun, but the measurements show a short, heavy, bass-rich shot.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.30 s Initial blast: dense broadband noise burst with heavy sub and bass weight, brightest in the first ~0.1s, the loudest part of the sound.; 0.30–0.65 s Body and secondary bump: a slightly detached lower-level pulse near 0.5s, likely a reflection or second layer, while bass thump continues.; 0.65–1.00 s Decaying low rumble and reverb tail, upper mids fading out and energy shrinking to below about 500 Hz.; 1.00–1.26 s Very faint tail dissolving into silence, with the last ~0.3s near-silent.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a roughly 1.0-1.2s one-shot with a tight, punchy transient: a noise crack and mid burst in the first 50-100ms, layered with a sub thump (40-120 Hz) and a short low-passed reverb or room tail rumbling out to about 0.9s. Keep most energy under 1 kHz with only a little air. Aim for a peak near -2 dBFS and about -14 LUFS integrated. Trim the tail to silence, and add slight variation by pitch or layer swaps if several variants are needed, since the file name suggests variant c. There is a small DC offset (-0.036) to remove.

### `sounds/guns/pistol_reload.ogg`

**A pistol reload made of several separate mechanical handling sounds: a click-clack, a loud magazine thunk with rattles, a softer slide or scrape and a final slide-release snap, with gaps of silence between them.** *(transient, mechanical, metallic, clicky, bright, noisy, foley, one-shot; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.81 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -2.9 dBFS, RMS -25.1 dBFS, -22.1 LUFS, true peak -2.7 dBTP; centroid 6000 Hz, flatness 0.001; 11 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono, so it is positioned by the game engine. This works well for a first-person player reload and gives a clear, localized sound that pans and attenuates naturally in 3D.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Multiple sharp transients with near-instant attacks (<5 ms) and fast decays of about 0.1-0.15 s each. The main peak is at about 0.39 s (-2.9 dBFS). Silent gaps at 0.17-0.38 s and 1.27-1.5 s. The ending has a short tail of around 0.1 s and no reverb.
- **Frequency profile:** Very broadband and noise-like (flatness about 0.001 per the measurement, though that is likely distorted by the dense clicks). Centroid is about 6 kHz and 85% of energy lies below 8.6 kHz. Most energy is in the presence band (56%), with mid and air at about 21% each. The spectrogram shows a low-frequency thump below 150 Hz on the heavy hits and a hard cut-off near 10-11 kHz on the clicks, which gives a metallic, bright-clicky character.
- **Role:** Player pistol reload sequence (mag out, mag in, slide rack or release), played as a single one-shot when the player reloads. The event name guns.pistolReload supports this reading.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.17 s First handling sound: a small rattle leading into a sharp click-clack, such as the magazine release or mag pulled out. Short and bright.; 0.17–0.38 s Complete silence gap.; 0.38–0.55 s Loudest event: a heavy metallic thunk or clack, like the magazine seating, with strong low body and bright transient, followed by quick decay.; 0.55–1.00 s Series of small, quieter clicks and rattles, like seating or fiddling with parts, gradually fading to near silence around 0.8-1.0 s.; 1.00–1.27 s Swelling scrape or slide movement with a noisy burst, like the slide being racked back, then dying away.; 1.27–1.50 s Near silence.; 1.51–1.81 s Closing gesture: a rattle, then a sharp click and a heavier snap, like slide release or chambering, with a short tail.
- **Recreation notes:** Make it about 1.8 s in four stages: (1) mag release click and rattle at 0.0 s; (2) a strong mag-insert thunk at about 0.4 s with a low-end body layer around 80-150 Hz plus a metallic click layer; (3) light clicks and a slide-rack scrape around 0.5-1.2 s; (4) slide-release snap at about 1.55 s. Keep the gaps of near silence so the stages stay readable. Peak around -3 dBFS, integrated about -22 LUFS, and keep the transients tight with no reverb. Record or synthesize original metal and plastic foley.

### `sounds/guns/pistol_reload_akimbo.ogg`

**A sequence of crisp, bright mechanical handling sounds, like two pistols being reloaded in turn with clicks, slides and magazine snaps, separated by short gaps.** *(transient, mechanical, metallic, clicky, bright, noisy, layered; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.60 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -2.5 dBFS, RMS -23.9 dBFS, -20.7 LUFS, true peak -2.4 dBTP; centroid 6002 Hz, flatness 0.002; 19 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned by the game engine at the player, so it works well as a close first-person handling sound.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Multiple sharp transient attacks (about 19 onsets) each decaying within 0.1-0.4s, with silent gaps at about 0.22-0.4s and 1.4-1.5s. Loudest peak at about 1.0s, and the file ends with a short tail of about 0.1s.
- **Frequency profile:** The energy is dominated by the presence band (about 57%, 2-6 kHz or so), plus the mid and air bands (about 21% each), for a centroid of about 6 kHz and rolloff near 8.6 kHz. Flatness is very low and the spectrogram shows broadband vertical strokes, so the metallic clicks are brief and broadband. Low-frequency thumps appear under the bigger hits, but sub and bass content is negligible. There is a cutoff near 11-16 kHz.
- **Role:** Reload sound for the akimbo (dual wielded) pistols, a sequence of magazine-out, magazine-in, slide and cocking actions for each gun. The Qwen notes describe typing, but the context and the clicky metallic spectrum point to gun handling. They are probably wrong about the source.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.22 s First short metallic click/handling burst, with a small peak near 0.1s.; 0.22–0.38 s Second similar click burst, slightly quieter, after a brief gap.; 0.46–0.85 s Sharp loud mechanical impact (magazine or slide) at about 0.46s, followed by a quiet rattling tail with small ticks.; 0.99–1.40 s Strongest hit at about 1.0s, a heavy metallic clack with a low thump, followed by secondary clicks and a fading tail.; 1.50–1.75 s Softer cluster of handling noise and clicks, with very quiet ticks leading in.; 1.85–2.20 s Rising metallic rattle building to a snap at about 2.06s, then a short decay.; 2.25–2.58 s Final click cluster with a snap near 2.4s, ending in a quick decay to silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Build a roughly 2.6s sequence from layered foley: metallic clicks, magazine slide-out and snap-in, slide racks and small rattles. Do the first gun's actions in about 0-1.4s and the second's in about 1.5-2.6s, with a slightly different timbre or timing for the second. Add a short low thump under the main impacts. Keep the bright 2-8 kHz character, dry with little reverb, peaks near -3 dBFS and about -21 LUFS integrated.

### `sounds/guns/pistol_shot_a.ogg`

**A single loud, bass-heavy pistol shot with a quick crack and a thick noisy body that decays into a long reverberant tail of about one second.** *(transient, boomy, noisy, broadband, gunshot, reverberant, punchy; confidence high)*

- **Measured:** 1.14 s, 44100 Hz, 2 ch; peak -2.3 dBFS, RMS -16.5 dBFS, -13.3 LUFS, true peak -2.1 dBTP; centroid 1317 Hz, flatness 0.014; 1 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Fairly narrow and near-mono (L/R correlation about 0.95, side about 16 dB below mid), with a slight stereo spread in the tail. It stays centered and punchy in game, and positions cleanly with 3D panning.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Near-instant attack, peaking at about 0.07 s (about -2 dBFS peak). Decay is quick at first, reaching -20 dB by about 0.39 s. The tail is long and smooth, reaching -40 dB by about 0.88 s. It fades to silence at about 0.95 s, with no sustain.
- **Frequency profile:** Broadband and noise-like, with a low-weighted body. Most energy sits in the sub to mid range: about 12% sub, 18% bass, 32% low-mid and 25% mid, with little presence (6%) and air (2.7%). The centroid is about 1.3 kHz and falls to about 600 Hz in the tail as the highs die away. Flatness of 0.014 is low and suggests a strong low-frequency tonal thump under the noise burst. There are no stable harmonic peaks. A bright crack at the start shows up in the spectrogram up to about 10 kHz or more.
- **Role:** Player pistol fire sound (guns.pistolFire), a one-shot weapon discharge triggered on each shot.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.07 s Very fast onset: a sharp crack/transient that reaches full level at about 0.07 s, with energy across the whole spectrum.; 0.07–0.40 s Dense, boomy body of the shot, with strong sub and bass energy under a broadband noise burst. It falls steadily by about 20 dB.; 0.40–0.95 s Reverberant tail or room decay: the low-mid rumble fades while the highs roll off, and the sound gets darker and thinner.; 0.95–1.14 s Near-silent end, about 0.19 s of trailing silence after the tail has faded out.
- **Recreation notes:** Build it from 3 layers. First, a sharp noise crack or click of about 20-50 ms, bright up to about 8-10 kHz. Second, a low thump or boom body with a pitch-dropping sine or kick in the 40-150 Hz range plus a mid noise burst, lasting about 300 ms. Third, a short room or reverb tail of about 0.6-0.8 s, darkened with a low-pass filter. Keep the total at about 1.0-1.1 s with a fast attack. Target a peak of around -2 to -3 dBFS and about -13 LUFS integrated. Keep it near-mono with only slight stereo width in the tail. Make several variants (this is 'a') to avoid repetition.

### `sounds/guns/pistol_shot_b.ogg`

**A heavy, boomy pistol shot with a thick low-end body and a noisy decaying tail that fades over about a second.** *(transient, boomy, noisy, gunshot, low-mid heavy, reverberant, impact; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.14 s, 44100 Hz, 2 ch; peak -2.0 dBFS, RMS -16.3 dBFS, -12.2 LUFS, true peak -1.7 dBTP; centroid 1267 Hz, flatness 0.007; 1 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Fairly narrow and near-mono (L/R correlation 0.93, side-to-mid -14 dB), with a little width in the tail. It stays centered and punchy, which suits a first-person player weapon, and it collapses to mono safely.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Attack about 0.06 s to the peak at -2 dBFS. Strong body from 0.06 to 0.3 s, then a smooth decay: -20 dB by 0.38 s and -40 dB by 0.89 s. Long diffuse tail, with silence from about 0.95 s to the end at 1.14 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the bass and low-mids (about 10% sub, 24% bass, 32% low-mid, 24% mid), with a centroid near 1.27 kHz and 85% rolloff around 2.3 kHz. Flatness is very low (0.007), though this likely reflects the strong low-frequency concentration rather than a clean tone, since the spectrogram shows broadband noise with no steady harmonic lines. Highs up to about 10 kHz are present only in the first 0.2 s and fade quickly. The centroid drops from about 1.2 kHz to about 600 Hz over the tail, so the sound gets darker.
- **Role:** Player pistol firing sound (guns.pistolFire), one variant 'b' of several that are likely rotated to reduce repetition.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.06 s Very fast onset of the muzzle blast, a broadband crack that rises quickly to the peak.; 0.06–0.38 s Dense, boomy body with strong sub, bass and low-mid energy and a gritty broadband texture, decaying about 20 dB.; 0.38–0.95 s Reverberant noisy tail that thins out and darkens as the highs die away first and low rumble lingers.; 0.95–1.14 s Near-silent trailing fade, with about 0.19 s of silence at the end.
- **Recreation notes:** Build about 1.0 to 1.1 s total with three layers: (1) a sharp, short broadband crack or snap for the transient, (2) a low thump or boom with plenty of 40-300 Hz body for 0.2-0.3 s, (3) a medium-sized room or reverb tail that darkens as it decays, fading out by about 0.9 s. Keep the centroid near 1 kHz, a peak around -2 dBFS, and about -12 LUFS integrated. Keep it nearly mono with slight width in the tail, and make 2-3 pitch or timbre variations to match the variant naming. The listening notes describe an artillery-like deep tone, but the measured 1 s decay and low-mid focus suggest a pistol with a heavy, roomy character.

### `sounds/guns/pistol_shot_c.ogg`

**A loud, full-bodied pistol shot with a sharp noisy crack at the start and a heavy low-end thump, followed by a long reverberant tail that fades out over about 0.9 s.** *(transient, noisy, boomy, gunshot, broadband, reverberant, punchy; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.14 s, 44100 Hz, 2 ch; peak -1.1 dBFS, RMS -16.2 dBFS, -12.6 LUFS, true peak -0.7 dBTP; centroid 1544 Hz, flatness 0.015; 1 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Moderately wide but mostly center-weighted (L/R correlation 0.84, side-to-mid -10.6 dB). The slightly decorrelated tail gives some room width, yet the shot stays centered for the player. It is fine for a first-person, player-centered shot, but it should be checked for mono compatibility when the sound is positioned in 3D.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Very fast attack of about 10 ms to a peak of -1.1 dBFS. The level falls about 20 dB by 0.45 s and about 40 dB by 0.9 s, giving a long exponential-like decay with a slight plateau around 0.3-0.5 s. The tail is gone by about 1.0 s, followed by 0.21 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Broadband and noise-like (flatness 0.015, which is low but consistent with a dense, pulsed low-frequency texture). Most energy sits in the bass and low-mid range (about 55% across 100-1000 Hz, with sub energy near 10%). The centroid is about 1.5 kHz and the 85% rolloff about 3.2 kHz. The bright crack in the highs lasts only the first 0.2 s or so. The centroid falls from about 1.4 kHz to about 550 Hz across the quarters, so the sound darkens as it decays.
- **Role:** Player pistol firing sound (guns.pistolFire), one of several variants (the _c suffix suggests a random variation).
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Sharp muzzle crack: a broadband noise burst that reaches its peak at about 0.01 s and has energy up to the top of the spectrum.; 0.05–0.40 s Body of the shot: dense low and low-mid noise and thump below about 500 Hz with fluttering, vertically streaked texture, and a slight secondary bump near 0.05 s.; 0.40–0.95 s Reverb or room tail: low-mid rumble that thins out, darkens and drops steadily in level, with the highs gone by about 0.6 s.; 0.95–1.14 s Near-silence, with only faint residual noise and then digital silence in the last 0.2 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Build it from three layers. (1) A short, bright noise or click transient of 20-50 ms for the crack. (2) A low thump or body from a pitched-down impact, with energy at 60-300 Hz, lasting about 150-300 ms. (3) A medium-sized room or reverb tail of about 0.8 s, low-passed and fading. Keep the total length near 1.1 s with a quick fade at the end. Aim for a peak near -1 dBFS and about -12 to -13 LUFS integrated, and keep the stereo image mostly centered with a slightly wider tail. Make a few variants with small pitch and transient changes. The listening notes call it artillery-like with a steady 900 Hz pitch, but the measurements show a noisy broadband shot with no stable pitch, so ignore the pitch claim.

### `sounds/guns/powerhammer_charge_a.ogg`

**A roughly 2.5 second rumbling, noisy low-end swell, like a heavy energy weapon charging up, that peaks near 1.2 s and then tails off into a muffled decay.** *(noisy, rumbling, boomy, sustained, mechanical, charge-up, low-end heavy, turbulent; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.53 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -3.6 dBFS, RMS -18.5 dBFS, -17.5 LUFS, true peak -3.5 dBTP; centroid 1343 Hz, flatness 0.0; 4 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it sits centered and can be positioned by the engine in 3D at the player. It stays consistent and does not smear when the player turns.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Attack is quick, about 0.1-0.2 s to the plateau. It sustains at a roughly constant level from 0.2 to 1.3 s with its peak at 1.17 s, then decays by 20 dB at around 1.8 s and by 40 dB around 2.3 s. A short silent tail of about 0.13 s follows.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is dominated by the sub and bass bands (about 62% together), with strong content below 300 Hz and a broad, gritty noise bed through the mids and highs up to about 10 kHz. The centroid is about 1.3 kHz overall, rising to about 2 kHz in the second quarter and falling to under 300 Hz in the tail. Flatness is reported as 0, but the texture looks noisy and turbulent, probably because of the strong low-frequency content. Spectral peaks near 16-18 kHz are probably encoding artifacts.
- **Role:** Charge-up or wind-up sound for the Powerhammer gun, played at the player when the weapon is charged before firing. The Qwen notes about a flame and an explosion are speculative and mainly reflect the rumbling noise texture.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.30 s Fast fade-in of low rumble and noise, with a small tick-like blip near 0.28 s visible in the highs.; 0.30–1.30 s Sustained, dense, turbulent rumble with strong sub and bass and gritty mid and high noise; it stays near its full level and reaches its peak around 1.2 s.; 1.30–1.90 s Energy falls off quickly, the highs thin out and the sound becomes mostly low rumble, with small bumps near 1.42 s and 1.71 s.; 1.90–2.53 s Quiet, dark, muffled tail that fades to silence, with a final faint bump near 2.2 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a 2.5 s one-shot. Layer a low sub rumble or motor-like drone (40-150 Hz) with filtered noise, and add some mechanical or electrical grit in the mids. Fade in over about 0.2 s, hold a strong plateau to about 1.2 s, and ramp brightness and intensity slightly toward the peak. Then roll off the highs and decay over about 1 s with a dark tail. Target a peak around -3 to -4 dBFS and about -17 LUFS integrated. Leave out a clear pitched tone, since the original has no distinct pitch sweep.

### `sounds/guns/powerhammer_charge_b.ogg`

**A rumbling, noisy mechanical charge-up: a dense low-frequency roar that holds for about 1.3 seconds, then falls away into a fading, rattly tail.** *(rumbling, noisy, mechanical, boomy, sustained, gritty, low-end heavy; confidence medium)*

- **Frequency profile:** Dominated by sub and bass (about 65% of energy below roughly 250 Hz) with a low-mid body. The spectrogram shows a bright wash up to about 1-2 kHz in the first half, with a fine grainy texture and little tonal structure. The centroid is about 1 kHz, peaking in the second quarter, then falling to under 250 Hz in the tail. Very little energy sits above 5 kHz, apart from faint high lines near the top.
- **Role:** Charge-up or spin-up sound for a heavy weapon (the powerhammer) while the player holds the fire button. The rumble builds to a sustained peak and then releases. This is inferred from the path and event name. The model heard an engine rev, consistent with the mechanical rumble.

- **Recreation notes:** Make a roughly 2.5s one-shot. Layer a sub/bass rumble (a low motor or turbine, 40-200 Hz) with a filtered noise or grit layer (300 Hz-2 kHz) and a few small mechanical clicks or rattles near the start and around 1.4s. Keep a fast attack and a flat sustain for about 1.2s, then a quick drop and a 1 second decaying tail. Avoid strong pitch sweeps. Target about -18 LUFS, with a peak near -4 dBFS. Trim high frequencies above about 5 kHz.

### `sounds/guns/powerhammer_charge_c.ogg`

**A noisy, low-heavy rumbling whoosh-like charge-up that swells quickly, holds a churning rumble for about a second, then fades into a long dark tail.** *(rumble, noisy, boomy, mechanical, churning, low-end, charge-up; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.53 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -3.9 dBFS, RMS -19.6 dBFS, -18.7 LUFS, true peak -3.5 dBTP; centroid 1051 Hz, flatness 0.0; 6 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono, so it will be positioned by the engine, which suits a player-attached sound. It stays centred and attenuates with distance as expected.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Attack about 0.2s (peak at 0.21s). Sustain at roughly -16 to -23 dB per tenth until about 1.0s, then decays: -20 dB at 1.33s, -40 dB at 1.98s. About 0.23s of trailing silence at the end. Soft, gradual fade rather than a hard cut.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is dominated by sub (about 32%) and bass (about 29%), with 15% in low-mid. Mid and above are only about 5-7% each. Centroid is about 1 kHz overall, rising to about 980 Hz in the second quarter, then falling to under 250 Hz. Spectral flatness reads 0 here, though the picture shows a broadband, noisy texture. There are faint high-frequency peaks near 16-19 kHz, which look like encoder artefacts. The brightness is concentrated in the first half and the tail is dark.
- **Role:** Charge-up or spin-up sound for a heavy weapon (the powerhammer) as the player winds it up. Qwen's spaceship beam interpretation is only a guess. Its claim of rising pitch is not supported by the measurements: the centroid actually falls in the second half.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.21 s Fast swell from near silence into a loud low rumble, with a small transient click around 0.05-0.15s.; 0.21–1.30 s Sustained churning rumble with heavy sub and bass energy and a gritty mid/high noise layer that is brightest around 0.3-0.7s; amplitude flutters like a mechanical or pulsing motor.; 1.30–2.30 s Decay: the noise layer thins out and energy falls to low rumble only, with a few faint small onsets (about 1.35, 1.6, 1.7, 2.0, 2.2s) that are probably texture or artefacts rather than distinct events.; 2.30–2.53 s Silent tail.
- **Recreation notes:** Make about 2.3-2.5s total. Layer (1) a sub/bass rumble of a low-passed noise or a motor drone around 40-200 Hz with a fast 0.2s swell; (2) a band-passed gritty noise or air hiss layer around 1-6 kHz, strongest in 0.2-0.8s and fading by about 1.3s; (3) optional light mechanical flutter or pulsing at a fast rate, plus a small click at the start. Let it decay over about 1s into a darker tail. Target peak around -4 dBFS and roughly -19 LUFS integrated. If you want a real charge feel, you could add a rising pitch, but the original does not clearly have one.

### `sounds/guns/powerhammer_impact_a.ogg`

**A heavy, low-end-weighted percussive impact thump with a noisy crunchy body that decays over about half a second into a faint, dark tail.** *(impact, boomy, transient, noisy, heavy, low-end, crunchy, one-shot; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.65 s, 32000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -1.8 dBFS, RMS -18.8 dBFS, -17.0 LUFS, true peak -1.7 dBTP; centroid 1016 Hz, flatness 0.013; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono (single channel). That suits a positional, player-triggered impact in game, since it can be panned and attenuated by distance without phase issues.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Very fast attack (~0.02 s peak), no sustain, rapid decay: -20 dB by ~0.33 s and -40 dB by ~0.71 s, then a very quiet tail that vanishes by ~0.9 s, leaving ~0.74 s of trailing silence in the file.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in bass (about 50%) with sub (~10%) and low-mid (~18%) support; centroid ~1 kHz and 85% rolloff ~1.7 kHz. Low flatness (0.013) stems from the dominant low-frequency content, but the body is noisy and broadband up to the top during the first ~0.3 s. A short faint tonal line around 1.6 kHz appears early on. Later in the tail the centroid rises to ~4.5 kHz only because the low end has decayed and faint hiss remains.
- **Role:** Impact/hit sound of a heavy power-hammer weapon when it strikes (the event is guns.powerhammerImpact, played for the player). The Qwen notes about rain and footsteps conflict with the measurements and look invented; only its 'muffled explosion' impression fits.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Instant hard attack: broadband burst with a strong sub/bass thump, brightest in the first tens of milliseconds.; 0.05–0.45 s Dense, crunchy noisy body with a pulsing low-frequency rumble (vertical striations below ~300 Hz) and mids fading as it decays.; 0.45–1.00 s Dark decaying tail of low rumble with sparse faint high-frequency flecks; a very faint tone near 800 Hz is barely visible.; 1.00–1.65 s Near-silent residual; only inaudible-level traces, with trailing silence from about 0.9 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Build 3 layers: (1) a sub/low thump, a quick pitch-dropped sine or kick around 40-120 Hz lasting ~0.3 s; (2) a noisy crunchy mid layer of debris or metal-on-concrete smack, 100 ms to 400 ms, with energy up to ~2 kHz; (3) a short bright transient click and a little dark rumble tail to ~0.7 s. Keep the total under ~1 s, with a hard attack and no pitch movement. Target peak around -2 dBFS and about -17 LUFS integrated; trim the trailing silence. Mono is fine.

### `sounds/guns/powerhammer_impact_b.ogg`

**A heavy, boomy low-frequency impact thud with a crunchy noisy body that decays over about 0.6 seconds into a short rumbling tail.** *(impact, boomy, transient, noisy, heavy, rumble, percussive; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.14 s, 32000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -1.8 dBFS, RMS -17.0 dBFS, -17.2 LUFS, true peak -1.6 dBTP; centroid 802 Hz, flatness 0.008; 1 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono (single channel). This suits positional 3D playback in game, so the impact can be placed at the hit location with distance attenuation.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Very fast attack (peak at 0.04 s), immediate decay: -20 dB by about 0.35 s and -40 dB by about 0.66 s. A small secondary bump near 0.32 s. Quiet tail fades to silence around 0.85 s, with about 0.28 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is dominated by bass (about 49%) and sub (about 15%), centered around 40-250 Hz, with low-mid about 18%. Centroid is about 800 Hz and 85% rolloff about 1.2 kHz. The early part has a gritty broadband noise wash up to about 8 kHz that darkens quickly. The very low flatness figure reflects the strong low-frequency tonal and thump content, but the texture is crunchy. The Qwen notes (multiple gunshots over 6 s) conflict with the 1.1 s measured duration and single onset, so they are discounted. Its 'gritty, metallic' impression is partly consistent.
- **Role:** Impact or hit sound of the 'powerhammer' gun, played when a shot or hammer blow lands (event guns.powerhammerImpact, a variant 'b'). Reads as a heavy blunt impact.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.10 s Instant heavy hit: broadband crunchy burst with strong sub and bass thump, peak around 0.04 s, with mids up to about 4 kHz.; 0.10–0.40 s Body of the impact: pulsing, churning low-end rumble (40-250 Hz) with fading mid grit, like debris or metallic rattle.; 0.32–0.60 s Small secondary bump or rattle (detected onset at 0.32 s) riding on the decaying tail.; 0.60–0.90 s Low rumbling tail fading out, very quiet by 0.9 s.; 0.90–1.14 s Near silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Make about 1.0-1.1 s total. Layer: (1) a sub thump, a pitch-dropped sine or kick at 40-80 Hz, 0.3 s; (2) a mid-bass body hit with a crunchy noise burst, 100-400 Hz, 0.3 s; (3) a short metallic or debris transient with a 1-4 kHz noise burst, under 0.1 s; (4) a low rumble tail fading over 0.6 s, with a small rattle at about 0.3 s. Keep the highs dark and avoid long reverb. Aim for a peak around -2 dBFS and about -17 LUFS integrated. Make 2-3 variants, since this is 'b'.

### `sounds/guns/powerhammer_impact_c.ogg`

**A heavy, boomy impact thump with a noisy crack at the start that decays over about 0.7 s into a quiet, rumbling tail.** *(impact, boomy, transient, noisy, low-end, explosive, decaying; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.65 s, 32000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -1.8 dBFS, RMS -18.7 dBFS, -17.2 LUFS, true peak -1.5 dBTP; centroid 939 Hz, flatness 0.014; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it is centered and easy to position with in-game panning and distance attenuation, which suits a player-triggered impact.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Near-instant attack (peak at 0.02 s), fast decay to -20 dB by 0.37 s and -40 dB by 0.71 s, then a very quiet tail. Nearly silent for the last 0.74 s of the file.
- **Frequency profile:** Mostly low end: about 46% of energy in the bass band and 12% in sub, with 19% in the low-mids. Centroid is about 940 Hz and 85% rolloff is about 1.5 kHz. The top end is noisy and bright only in the first 0.2 s, and the high-frequency content dies out quickly. Flatness is low, so the dense low-frequency thump reads as fairly tonal and boomy rather than hissy. There is a faint tone near 1.6 kHz early on and a weak one near 700-800 Hz late.
- **Role:** Impact sound for a heavy hammer-type weapon (powerhammer) hitting a target, played from the player. It is one of several variants (the 'c' suffix).
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.10 s Loud broadband attack: noisy crack on top of a strong sub/bass thump, peaking at about 0.02 s.; 0.10–0.45 s Pulsing low-frequency body with a noisy mid/high smear fading quickly; a faint tonal line near 1.6 kHz is visible up to about 0.4 s.; 0.45–0.75 s Fading rumble with a few lower-frequency bursts around 0.6-0.7 s as the energy drops.; 0.75–1.65 s Very quiet reverb-like tail with a faint, weak tone near 700 Hz, then near silence from about 0.9 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer a short noisy crack or click (about 50-100 ms) with a deep sub thump around 50-100 Hz and a mid-heavy body hit with a slight metallic ring near 1.5 kHz. Add a short, dark reverb tail. Total length about 1-1.5 s, with the main energy done within 0.5 s. Aim for a peak near -2 dBFS and about -17 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono, and make a few variations to avoid repetition.

### `sounds/guns/powerhammer_impact_d.ogg`

**A heavy, boomy low-frequency impact thump with a noisy crunchy mid layer that decays over about a second into a rumbling tail.** *(boomy, impact, noisy, heavy, transient, rumble, mono; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.14 s, 32000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -2.1 dBFS, RMS -17.0 dBFS, -17.3 LUFS, true peak -1.9 dBTP; centroid 853 Hz, flatness 0.007; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it sits centered and is easy to position or attenuate with distance in 3D. It is a player-triggered event, so it should be played at the player's position.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Near-instant attack (about 0.02 s), no real sustain. Rapid fall to -20 dB by about 0.38 s and -40 dB by about 0.81 s. A short rumbling tail ends around 0.9 s, followed by about 0.28 s of silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Mostly low end: about 50% of the energy is in the bass band and about 10% in the sub, with 19% in the low-mids. The centroid is about 850 Hz and the 85% rolloff is around 1.4 kHz. The spectrum is noisy and broadband with low-mid grit and little air. The measured flatness of 0.007 is low, but that reflects the dominant low-frequency mass rather than a clean tone. There are no steady tonal lines. The mid and high noise brightens briefly in the early body and then darkens as it fades.
- **Role:** Impact or explosion-like hit of a heavy weapon (a 'power hammer' impact) when the player's shot or strike lands. This is inferred from the path and event name plus the boomy percussive profile. The Qwen description of gunshots and multiple events at 0.8-4 s does not fit the 1.14 s file, so it was disregarded, though its 'sharp start, fading tail' agrees.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Sharp onset with the peak at about 0.02 s: broadband crack and a strong sub/bass thump.; 0.05–0.50 s Dense, crunchy body of bass-heavy impact with debris-like mid noise up to about 3-4 kHz; energy flutters in the bass and fades steadily.; 0.50–0.90 s Low rumble tail with faint residual mid noise, falling quickly below -40 dB.; 0.90–1.14 s Near-silent fade; the last 0.28 s is effectively silent.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer a sub thump (about 40-80 Hz, short pitch drop) with a mid-heavy crunchy noise burst or debris (low-passed around 3-4 kHz) and a brief transient crack for the first 20 ms. Add a short, dark rumble tail. Total length about 1.0 s with a fast exponential decay and a clean fade to silence. Target a peak around -2 dBFS and about -17 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono. Make several variants, since the filename suffix 'd' suggests a set of randomized variations.

### `sounds/guns/powerhammer_impactGround_a.ogg`

**A heavy, low-frequency ground slam with a thick rumbling body that decays over about a second into a dark rumble tail.** *(boomy, impact, rumbling, noisy, low-end, heavy, transient, dark; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.72 s, 32000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -2.4 dBFS, RMS -15.5 dBFS, -17.7 LUFS, true peak -1.9 dBTP; centroid 407 Hz, flatness 0.008; 3 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it will be positioned by the engine's 3D panning and distance attenuation. That suits a localized ground impact the player can place in the world.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Near-instant attack (peak at 0.03 s), a sustained, bumpy body from about 0.05 to 0.6 s at roughly -10 to -16 dB, then decay: -20 dB by 0.7 s, -40 dB by about 1.0 s, trailing off to silence around 1.2 s. The file ends with about 0.49 s of silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Strongly low-weighted: about 41% of energy is sub and 30% bass, with 85% rolloff at only 188 Hz. The bright body sits around 20-250 Hz and thins out above about 400 Hz. Some mid and high content is present only in the first ~0.2 s as the impact crack. Very low flatness (0.008) is a measurement quirk of the dominant low energy, though the texture itself sounds rumbly and noise-like. After the first quarter the centroid drops to about 60-100 Hz.
- **Role:** Impact sound for a power hammer striking the ground, played from the player. It is a heavy melee or weapon slam with a thick bass thud and debris rumble. The Qwen notes about gunshots and bells conflict with the measurements and are not supported by the spectrogram, so I discount them.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Sharp initial strike transient with a brief broadband burst reaching into the highs.; 0.05–0.70 s Dense, churning sub and bass rumble body with energy up to about 250 Hz and rough, crumbling texture in the mids; amplitude fluctuates, suggesting debris or layered hits.; 0.70–1.05 s Rapid fall-off of the body; a small secondary bump near 0.74 s and 1.0 s as the rumble collapses.; 1.05–1.23 s Very quiet low rumble tail fading to silence; the last ~0.49 s is silent.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer three parts: (1) a short 30-50 ms punchy crack or click for the transient; (2) a deep sub thud (around 40-90 Hz) with a fast downward pitch drop and about 0.5 s of sustain; (3) a granular rock or debris rumble, low-passed near 300-500 Hz, lasting about 0.6-1.0 s with a bumpy amplitude. Total length about 1.2 s with a natural fade and no trailing silence. Aim for a peak near -2 dBFS and about -17 LUFS integrated. Keep the mids sparse so the sound stays dark and heavy. Make 2-3 variations, since the file is a variant 'a'.

### `sounds/guns/powerhammer_impactGround_b.ogg`

**A heavy, bass-dominant rumbling slam with crunchy debris texture that sustains for about 0.8 s and then decays into a quiet tail, like a massive hammer smashing into the ground.** *(boomy, impact, rumble, noisy, heavy, crunchy, low-end, explosive; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.55 s, 32000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -1.9 dBFS, RMS -15.4 dBFS, -16.9 LUFS, true peak -1.6 dBTP; centroid 405 Hz, flatness 0.006; 2 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. The impact stays centered and is easy to position in 3D as a point source in game, with no width to cause phase problems.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack of about 0.07 s to the peak at -1.9 dBFS. A dense sustain holds roughly -10 to -19 dB per tenth-second until about 0.8 s. It then decays quickly: -20 dB at 0.77 s and -40 dB at 1.0 s, with a very quiet tail fading out by about 1.25 s. The last 0.3 s is silent.
- **Frequency profile:** Dominated by sub (about 33%) and bass (about 50%) energy below roughly 200 Hz, with an 85% rolloff at 203 Hz. The centroid is low at about 405 Hz, and about 83-118 Hz in the middle of the sound. There is a little noisy crackle in the low-mids and mids, mostly in the first 0.3 s, and very little air. Flatness is very low (0.006) because the energy is concentrated in the bass, though the texture is rumbly and noise-like rather than a clean tone. There are no stable harmonic peaks.
- **Role:** Impact sound of the powerhammer weapon hitting the ground, a heavy slam with a debris rumble. It is the 'b' variant, meant to alternate with other variants.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.10 s Sharp initial impact with a broadband crack, brightest in the first 0.1 s, peaking around 0.07 s.; 0.10–0.85 s Sustained, churning sub and bass rumble with granular debris and crackle texture in the low-mids. The level stays fairly steady with small pulsing fluctuations, and a couple of extra bursts at about 0.67 s and 0.78 s.; 0.85–1.25 s Rapid decay of the rumble, losing about 40 dB by 1.0 s, with only a faint low-frequency murmur remaining.; 1.25–1.55 s Near-silent tail and trailing silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer 1: a punchy transient (a short thud or crack of 50-100 ms) for the strike. Layer 2: a sub boom at about 40-90 Hz with a quick pitch drop, holding for about 0.7 s. Layer 3: low-passed rubble, gravel or noise bursts with a gritty texture, adding a couple of secondary bursts around 0.65-0.8 s. Fade out sharply by about 1.0-1.2 s with a short, subtle tail. Keep it mono, with a total length of about 1.2-1.5 s, peak around -2 dBFS and about -17 LUFS integrated. Keep most energy below 250 Hz, with little above 2 kHz. Make several variants with slightly different pitch and debris.

### `sounds/guns/powerhammer_reload.ogg`

**A multi-stage mechanical gun reload: soft handling rustles, a small clunk, a heavy slam with noisy metallic impact around 1.8s, then a long bright ratcheting/sliding rattle with a final impact that fades out.** *(mechanical, metallic, noisy, transient, layered, clunky, rattle, heavy; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 3.43 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -4.6 dBFS, RMS -27.5 dBFS, -24.0 LUFS, true peak -4.6 dBTP; centroid 4244 Hz, flatness 0.004; 8 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono, so it is positioned entirely by the game engine; fine for a player-attached reload sound and will pan or attenuate by distance correctly.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Several discrete events separated by near-silent gaps (around 1.0-1.1s and 2.2-2.3s). Quiet start, small bump at about 0.8s, peak at 1.8s with sharp attack and fast decay (about 0.15s to -20 dB, 0.4s to -40 dB), then a second, longer event starting at 2.3s with sharp attack and sustained rattly decay out to about 3.4s.
- **Frequency profile:** Broadband and noisy (flatness very low in value but spectrum looks dense and wideband). Centroid rises from about 1.1 kHz early to about 7.6 kHz in the last quarter. The bulk of energy sits in the mids (38%) and presence (22%) with a notable air band (18%). Slams add sub and bass thump, while the final section is bright, hissy metallic noise up to 10 kHz and beyond.
- **Role:** Reload sound for the Powerhammer gun (likely a shotgun/hammer-style weapon), played at the player with several mechanical steps: grab, latch, slam, pump/cock and settle.
- **Elements:** 0.08–0.60 s Quiet handling rustle and soft metallic scrape with a small click near 0.09s and 0.36s, energy mostly 300-1000 Hz.; 0.72–1.00 s Short sharper clunk/latch with a bright broadband burst, brighter than the preceding rustle, quickly decaying.; 1.10–1.70 s Quiet low-level mechanical movement or slide, sparse small ticks around 1.14 and 1.28s, mid-frequency.; 1.75–2.10 s Loudest heavy slam/clack with strong low thump (about 60-100 Hz) plus bright noisy metallic crack, decaying within about 0.4s.; 2.30–3.40 s Final big bolt/pump action: loud broadband impact at 2.3s with bass thump and bright metallic noise, then a long ratcheting rattle tail with extra clicks near 2.7s and 2.85s, fading to the end.
- **Recreation notes:** Build about 3.4s total with distinct stages: 1) soft cloth/metal handling rustle with two light clicks; 2) a small latch clunk near 0.8s; 3) a heavy metallic slam near 1.8s with low thump layer and bright clack; 4) a second heavier bolt/pump impact near 2.3s followed by a ratcheting metal rattle and a couple of extra clicks, tailing off by 3.4s. Keep silent gaps between stages for readability. Aim for about -24 LUFS integrated, peak around -4 to -5 dBFS, with the loudest hits at the 1.8s and 2.3s marks. Use mono output.

### `sounds/guns/powerhammer_shot_a.ogg`

**A heavy, bass-dominated gun blast with a noisy crack at the start, followed by a rumbling, crackling tail that fades over about 2 seconds.** *(boomy, noisy, explosive, rumbling, transient, heavy, low-end, tail; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 3.19 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -0.6 dBFS, RMS -19.3 dBFS, -20.1 LUFS, true peak -0.6 dBTP; centroid 776 Hz, flatness 0.0; 13 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned by the game's 3D panning and attenuation. The sound needs no stereo width, and the low-end weight will carry at close range.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack, peaking at about 0.1 s. The loudest part holds until about 0.3 s, and the level falls to -20 dB by about 0.8 s and to -40 dB by about 1.85 s. A long, gradually thinning tail follows, with silence from about 2.4 s to the end at 3.19 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Dominated by sub and bass energy (about 35% sub, 12% bass), with a spectral centroid of about 776 Hz that falls to under 100 Hz in the tail. The first 0.8 s has broadband noise reaching 10 kHz and above, which gives the crack and grit. Flatness is reported as 0, but the spectrogram looks noise-like and dense, not tonal. The prominent peaks at 16 to 18 kHz are probably codec or band-limit artifacts. The tail is mostly low-mid rumble.
- **Role:** Firing sound for the 'powerhammer' gun, a heavy weapon with a big, explosive report and a lingering rumble. The context is the player-fired gun event.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.15 s Initial blast: a sharp noisy crack with a broadband burst reaching up to the top of the spectrum, layered over a strong sub and bass thump.; 0.15–0.85 s Sustained, churning body of low rumble and gritty noise. Many small energy bursts (onsets near 0.34, 0.57, 0.71 and 0.85 s) make it sound like a rolling, crackling explosion rather than a single hit.; 0.85–2.10 s Decaying tail. High frequencies drop away, leaving low rumble with faint scattered crackles and debris-like ticks. Faint, thin tonal-looking lines around 1 kHz appear near 1.9 to 2.1 s.; 2.10–3.19 s Very quiet low-frequency residue fading into silence, with trailing silence of about 0.8 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Build it in three layers: (1) a short, punchy transient of noise or a snap, about 50 to 100 ms, with some energy up to 8 to 10 kHz; (2) a deep sub thump and bass body at about 40 to 120 Hz, lasting 0.5 to 0.8 s, with a fast attack and a downward pitch drop; (3) a rumbling, crackling tail of low-passed noise and debris ticks that decays over 1.5 to 2 s. Aim for a total of about 2.5 to 3 s, a peak near -1 dBFS and about -20 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono, and leave the tail quiet so it does not mask other sounds. The Qwen notes mention glass shattering, but the spectrogram does not clearly confirm that, so treat it as crackly debris at most.

- **Envelope:** Very fast onset with a crack at 0 s, then the level builds to its peak at about 0.29 s. It holds near that level to about 0.5 s, falls 20 dB by about 0.85 s and 40 dB by about 1.5 s, then tails off to silence by about 2.0 s. Trailing silence is about 0.63 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is dominated by sub-bass and bass, with about 26% in the sub band and a rolloff at about 94 Hz. The spectrogram shows a strong wash from 20 to 200 Hz, with a rough, crackly mid and high noise layer in the first ~0.9 s that fades afterward. Little is present above 5 kHz. The flatness reading of 0 is probably dominated by the low-frequency content. The listed peaks at 16-19 kHz are low-level artifacts. The DC offset is noticeable (-0.048).
- **Role:** Firing sound for the 'powerhammer' gun, played for the player. It is a heavy, powerful weapon shot, one of several variants (suffix _c).
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.30 s Initial blast: a sharp noisy crack with bright mid/high content (centroid ~500 Hz) over a rapidly building sub-bass thump.; 0.30–0.90 s Main body: the sub-bass boom peaks (~0.29 s) and sustains with a fluttering, rough texture; secondary bursts at about 0.47 and 0.55 s.; 0.90–1.50 s Rumbling decay: smaller bursts near 0.94, 1.15 and 1.33 s, with energy sinking to deep lows (centroid down to ~65 Hz) and the highs fading.; 1.50–2.00 s Fading low rumble tail, dropping steadily toward silence.; 2.00–2.58 s Near-silent trailing tail, about 0.63 s of silence at the end.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer three parts: (1) a short, sharp noise crack or snap of about 50-100 ms for the attack; (2) a deep sub-bass thump or boom at about 40-90 Hz with a slight downward drop, lasting about 0.5-0.8 s; (3) a rumbling low-passed noise tail with some flutter or reflections for about 1.2-1.5 s. Keep the total length at about 2 s or less with a clean fade to silence, and remove DC offset. Target a peak around -2 dBFS and about -19 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono and make a few slight variations to match the _a/_b/_c set.

### `sounds/guns/powerhammer_swing_a.ogg`

**A heavy, rumbling whoosh-swing of low-frequency noise that bursts in with a gritty, textured body, then gradually tapers into a low rumble over about 1.5 seconds, like a big heavy tool swung through the air.** *(whoosh, noisy, boomy, rumbling, heavy, low-end, gritty, one-shot; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.67 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -3.2 dBFS, RMS -20.8 dBFS, -21.0 LUFS, true peak -3.1 dBTP; centroid 834 Hz, flatness 0.0; 7 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. It stays centered on the player, so the swing sounds locked to the player's position and is easy to layer with hit or impact sounds.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Quick attack (~0.1 s peak), sustained high level through about 0.5 s, then decay: -20 dB by ~0.65 s and -40 dB by ~0.99 s. Pulsing, fluttery amplitude throughout. Faint tail to ~1.3 s, with about 0.37 s of trailing silence.

The spectrum is dense and noise-like, with a gritty mid/presence texture in the first 0.65 s and a hard cut-off of highs around 0.65 s. The measured flatness of 0.0 is likely a metric artifact. The peaks at 16-17 kHz are probably encoder artifacts and can be ignored.
- **Role:** Swing sound for a heavy melee weapon, the 'power hammer', played when the player swings it. The measurements support a weighty, air-moving swing rather than a gunshot or water splash.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.10 s Fast onset of broadband noise with a bright, gritty top end up to roughly 10 kHz, heavy low-end beneath.; 0.10–0.65 s Main swing body: dense low-frequency rumble (20-300 Hz) with a gritty mid texture and irregular pulsing or flutter, with a few louder bursts around 0.05, 0.4 and 0.53 s.; 0.65–1.20 s Upper content drops away abruptly near 0.65 s, leaving a darker, fading low rumble with pulsing amplitude and small bumps at 0.85, 1.1 and 1.19 s.; 1.20–1.50 s Very quiet low-frequency tail that thins out into near silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a ~1.5 s one-shot. Layer a low-passed pink/brown noise whoosh (roughly 20-400 Hz emphasis) with a sub rumble or thud body, plus a gritty mid-noise layer (500 Hz-5 kHz) that cuts or filters down at ~0.65 s. Add amplitude flutter or tremolo for a heavy, wobbling feel. Attack ~0.1 s, fast decay over ~1 s and a quiet tail. Peak around -3 dBFS, integrated loudness around -21 LUFS. Keep mono and leave room at the end for the following hit sound.

### `sounds/guns/powerhammer_swing_b.ogg`

The flatness reading of 0.0 is unreliable given the visibly noisy texture early on.

### `sounds/guns/pulserifle_reload.ogg`

**A mechanical weapon reload made of a rapid series of metallic clicks, clacks and rattles in three bursts separated by short silent gaps.** *(mechanical, metallic, transient, clicky, rattly, bright, electronic, multi-part; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.22 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -3.1 dBFS, RMS -24.3 dBFS, -21.6 LUFS, true peak -2.3 dBTP; centroid 5229 Hz, flatness 0.003; 15 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned by the engine as a point source at the player, which suits a first-person or held-weapon reload cue.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Percussive and sparse. Each click has a near-instant attack and decays in roughly 0.04-0.16 s. There is silence from about 1.0-1.05 s, from about 1.55-1.8 s, and about 0.11 s of trailing silence. The sound is active about 67% of the time. The overall peak is at about 1.39 s, and the last event fades over about 0.2 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Broadband and bright. The centroid is around 5 kHz and the 85% rolloff is near 8.3 kHz. Most energy is in the presence band (44%) and mid band (30%), with 19% in the air band and almost nothing below 200 Hz. Spectral flatness is very low in the measurements, though the spectrogram shows clusters of noisy, metallic strokes with a few warm patches at 1-2 kHz. Faint low-frequency smears in the spectrogram are minor in energy terms.
- **Role:** Player reload sound for the pulse rifle: a sequence of mag-out, mag-in and charge or bolt actions with a sci-fi or mechanical feel. The Qwen notes mention gunshots, but the measurements show no gunshot-like low-frequency boom, so I discount them.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.20 s Opening cluster of two or three sharp metallic clacks (magazine release or latch), strongest in the 1-4 kHz region.; 0.30–0.95 s Longer run of lighter clicks and rattles around 0.33, 0.45, 0.56, 0.64, 0.72 and 0.8 s, like a mag being slid or a mechanism cycling, with a faint low thump near 0.5 s.; 1.05–1.55 s Second burst of spaced clicks at about 1.08, 1.17, 1.27, 1.36 and 1.46 s. Several are sharper and louder, and the loudest peak (about -3 dBFS) is near 1.39 s, probably the mag seating or bolt slap.; 1.78–2.10 s Final burst starting at 1.78 s: a bright, noisy clack followed by a second clack around 1.93 s, the closing or charging action, with a short tail.
- **Recreation notes:** Build an original reload of about 2.2 s in three beats: (1) mag release and removal clacks at 0-0.2 s, with a light slide or rattle from 0.3-0.9 s; (2) after a short gap, a mag-insert click series at about 1.05-1.5 s with one firm seat or slap as the peak; (3) after a gap of about 0.25 s, a charging handle or bolt double-clack at about 1.8-2.0 s. Layer metal and plastic clicks with a little electronic whine or chirp for the pulse-weapon feel. Keep the energy at 1-10 kHz with little below 200 Hz. Target a peak of about -3 dBFS and about -21 LUFS integrated, and keep it mono with tight, short tails.

The opening quarter is the brightest (about 8.8 kHz centroid).

- **Elements:** 0.00–0.35 s Loud opening handling burst: a cluster of low thuds and metallic clacks (likely cylinder release or swing-out) with the strongest bass and a short ringing tail.; 0.35–0.90 s Quieter scattered ticks and faint metallic rattle, with a thin high-frequency whine (about 9-10 kHz) early on.; 0.90–1.30 s Distinct sharp metallic clicks at about 0.91s and 1.03-1.17s, like rounds or the ejector rod being tapped, with a bright mid-range ping.; 1.30–2.10 s Sparse series of single clicks and taps (about 1.38, 1.57, 1.73-1.84 and 2.0s), the strongest being around 1.8s, with short decays between them.; 2.10–2.25 s Faint final tick and a short soft tail before near-silence.; 2.33–2.72 s Rapid burst of sharp bright clicks (about 2.33, 2.41 and 2.49s) that is likely the cylinder snapping shut or spinning, followed by a noisy decaying tail.
- **Recreation notes:** Build from separate foley layers: a heavy cylinder-release clunk, small metallic ratchet or round-insertion ticks spaced about 0.1-0.2s apart, and a final snappy cylinder-close click flurry. Keep the total near 2.7s, with a gap before the closing sequence. Add a light high-frequency click layer for brightness and a touch of low thud on the first hit. Target peaks around -5 dBFS and integrated loudness near -26 LUFS, since this is a quiet, detailed sound with high dynamics. Keep it mono.

### `sounds/guns/revolver_reload_akimbo.ogg`

**A sequence of dry mechanical gun-handling sounds: two similar bursts of metallic clicks and ratchets at the start, sparse ticks through the middle, then two clusters of rapid clicks at the end, like a revolver being reloaded with both hands.** *(transient, metallic, mechanical, clicky, bright, noisy, layered, dry; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 3.51 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -4.5 dBFS, RMS -27.8 dBFS, -24.7 LUFS, true peak -4.3 dBTP; centroid 6228 Hz, flatness 0.009; 26 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned by the game as a point source at the player, which suits a first-person handling sound. Players need no stereo width.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Starts instantly with a loud transient (~-19 dB tenth-second level). Two dense bursts decay over about 0.4s each. A long, quiet middle with isolated clicks fades to a trough around 2.5-2.6s (~-73 dB). The final clusters rise again with sharp attacks, then end with a short soft tail and no silence at the end. The sound is a one-shot sequence of about 3.5s.
- **Frequency profile:** Mostly bright: the centroid is about 6 kHz, with 85% rolloff near 10 kHz. Energy is split across mid (35%), presence (27%) and air (36%). The low end is minimal apart from the thumps in the first half-second. Narrow high-frequency lines near 8-10 kHz around 0.2-0.9s are likely metallic ringing. Very low flatness (0.009) is surprising for such clicky material and likely reflects tonal metallic resonances.
- **Role:** Player reload sound for akimbo (dual-wielded) revolvers: a pair of reload actions (two similar opening/ejecting bursts), then shell loading and cylinder closing or cocking clicks.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.45 s First handling burst: low thump or bass knock with dense metallic clicks, then a short ratchet-like rattle that fades.; 0.45–0.95 s Second burst with a similar pattern, a thump plus clicks and a rattle. It is slightly lower in pitch, and the loudest transient falls near 0.92s.; 0.95–2.40 s Sparse, quieter metallic ticks and clinks about every 0.1s (cylinder or shell handling) with faint tails and low background energy.; 2.40–2.65 s Near-silent gap, the quietest point of the file.; 2.65–3.50 s Two clusters of rapid bright clicks (cylinder spin or closing and cocking) at about 2.7-2.9s and 3.1-3.3s, each followed by a soft rattling tail.
- **Recreation notes:** Build it from layered Foley or synthesized one-shots: metal clicks, a cylinder ratchet, shell or casing tinks, and a dull thud for gun handling. Keep the two initial bursts similar but not identical, one for each gun. Space the sparse loading ticks at about 0.1s, leave a short gap near 2.5s, and finish with two crisp closing or cocking clusters. Total length should be about 3.5s. Peak around -4 to -5 dBFS, integrated loudness around -25 LUFS, and keep the sound dry with no reverb.

### `sounds/guns/revolver_shot_a.ogg`

**A single heavy revolver gunshot with a sharp, broadband crack and a thick low-end boom that decays into a reverberant tail over roughly one second.** *(transient, boomy, noisy, explosive, reverberant, low-heavy, impact; confidence medium)*

- **Frequency profile:** Energy is dominated by sub and bass (about 52% below roughly 250 Hz) with a noisy broadband body through the mids. The centroid starts near 860 Hz and falls to about 190-240 Hz as the highs die away first. The initial crack carries bright content up to 10 kHz and beyond, but the high bands account for little energy overall (presence 4%, air 1.5%). The flatness figure of 0.0 looks unreliable for such a noisy sound. The listed peaks at 16-19 kHz are probably codec artifacts rather than musical tones.
- **Role:** The firing sound of a revolver, played for the player's shot (guns.revolverFire). It is a punchy, heavy single shot with some room tail.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Sharp muzzle crack: instant broadband transient reaching the peak at about 0.01 s, with bright energy up to the top of the spectrum.; 0.05–0.50 s Boomy body: strong sub and bass energy below about 200 Hz with a noisy mid-range wash of blast and room reflections, fading quickly.; 0.50–1.20 s Decaying reverb or room tail: low rumble and diffuse noise thinning out, with a small bump near 0.7 s that may be a reflection or echo.; 1.20–1.78 s Near-silent residual tail, very low-level and mostly low-frequency, fading to silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Build it from three layers: (1) a sharp noise or snap transient of about 10-30 ms for the crack; (2) a pitched-down boom or low thump around 50-150 Hz, decaying over about 300-500 ms, for the body; (3) a short, dark reverb or room tail of about 0.8-1 s. Keep the total length near 1.2 s, with no long silent tail, and trim the 0.6 s of silence at the end. Target a peak around -3 dBFS and about -18 LUFS integrated, with a fast attack and no clipping. Keep it mono. If making variations, change the crack and tail slightly but keep the same low-end weight.

Layer (1) a short sharp noise crack or transient of about 30-50 ms for the muzzle blast, (2) a deep sub/bass boom with a body around 50-200 Hz, held about 0.4-0.5 s, lightly saturated, and (3) a noisy mid layer with a room or reverb tail decaying about 0.8 s with a low-pass filter that closes over time. Keep most energy below 1 kHz. Target a peak near -1 dBFS, roughly -11 LUFS integrated and about -11 dBFS RMS. Keep it mono, and fade out cleanly to silence.

### `sounds/guns/sawedoff_shot_b.ogg`

**A heavy, boomy shotgun blast with a fast attack and a long rumbling noise tail that fades over about 1.2 seconds.** *(boomy, noisy, transient, low-heavy, explosive, rumbling, dark; confidence medium)*

The spectrogram shows a bright block from 20 to 300 Hz and a noisy mid/high crack that fades out within about 0.5 s. Spectral flatness is very low, but the spectrogram shows broadband noise, so this is likely an artefact of the dense low-frequency content. Layer: (1) a sharp noise-burst crack of 10-30 ms for the transient; (2) a deep boom or pitched-down impact with energy at 40-200 Hz, held for about 0.4 s; (3) a filtered-noise roar or room tail that decays to silence by about 1.2 s with a darkening low-pass sweep. ### `sounds/guns/SCAR_reload.ogg`

**A noisy, clicky mechanical rifle reload in three distinct phases: rattly magazine handling, a mid-clip clack with a sharper snap, then a heavy bolt or charging slam, followed by a quiet tail.** *(mechanical, transient, metallic, noisy, clicky, gritty, percussive; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 3.53 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak 0.2 dBFS, RMS -22.5 dBFS, -16.4 LUFS, true peak 0.7 dBTP; centroid 5578 Hz, flatness 0.311; 13 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned by the engine, so it works well as a player-attached sound with no fixed stereo width.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Three bursts separated by gaps near 0.9-1.05s and 1.8-1.95s. Each burst has an instant attack. The final slam peaks at about 2.1s and drops 20 dB in about 0.1s and 40 dB in about 0.27s, with a very quiet tail until about 2.9s. About 1s of near-silence trails the end.
- **Frequency profile:** Bright and broadband. The centroid is around 5.6 kHz, with energy mostly in the mid (35%) and presence (34%) bands and a good deal of air (23%). Very little sub or bass. The slam adds low-mid thump below 500 Hz. Spectral flatness of 0.31 indicates a noisy, partly tonal metallic texture. A faint narrow line near 3 kHz appears after 2.5s, and a short ring near 8-10 kHz appears at the slam.
- **Role:** First-person reload sound for the SCAR rifle: mag out, mag in, then bolt/charging-handle slam. The Qwen notes describing gunshots conflict with the evidence, which shows handling clicks and a mechanical slam rather than muzzle blasts.
- **Elements:** 0.21–0.80 s Magazine release and handling: a cluster of small rattling clicks and scrapes, broadband and bright, with a few stronger taps near 0.3s and 0.5s.; 0.84–1.05 s Brief quiet gap with only a faint isolated tick at about 0.84s.; 1.09–1.65 s Second phase: series of light clicks (mag insertion/seating) building to a sharper, louder snap around 1.4s with a lower thump body.; 1.65–1.95 s Near-silence between phases.; 1.97–2.50 s Loudest event: a heavy bolt/charging-handle slam peaking at about 2.1s, a full-spectrum crack with strong low-mid thump and a short metallic ring, then a quick decay.; 2.50–3.30 s Faint residual rattle and a thin high tone at around 3 kHz, barely audible, fading to silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a roughly 2.5-3.0s one-shot with three staged layers: (1) light plastic/metal rattles and a mag-release click at 0.2-0.6s, (2) a mag-insert clack with a small thump at about 1.1-1.5s, (3) a heavy bolt slam at about 2.1s with a low-mid body (200-500 Hz), a bright metallic crack and a short ring. Keep gaps of about 0.2s between phases. Trim the silent tail to under 0.3s. Aim for about -16 LUFS with the slam peaking near -1 dBFS and no clipping. Keep it mono.

Sustains roughly 0.1-0.6 s, drops 20 dB by about 0.6 s and 40 dB by about 1.2 s, then a short tail ending at about 1.75 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Strongly low-weighted: about 19% sub and 61% bass, with little above 1 kHz (centroid ~290 Hz, 85% rolloff ~215 Hz). Texture is noisy and gritty. The spectrogram shows bright energy below ~300 Hz, a warm orange-red mid haze, and top-end hiss that fades within the first half-second. The centroid falls from ~340 Hz to ~100 Hz, so the sound darkens over time. The faint streak near 3-4 kHz at about 0.4 s is minor.
- **Role:** Player-fired sonic shotgun shot (guns.sonicshotgunFire), a variant of the fire sound, giving a powerful, bassy blast with a rumbling sonic decay.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.46 s Main blast: dense broadband noise with heavy sub and bass energy, reaching peak around 0.46 s; brighter hiss up to several kHz in the first ~0.4 s.; 0.46–1.00 s Sustained rumbling body with flickering low-frequency pulses and fading mid noise.; 0.73–1.25 s Several small crackle or flutter onsets (about 0.73, 0.91, 1.07, 1.18 s) riding on the decaying rumble.; 1.25–1.75 s Dark, quiet tail of low rumble fading to silence, with almost no highs.
- **Recreation notes:** Build about 1.7 s. Layer: (1) a sub thump or pitched-down boom at 40-100 Hz with a quick pitch drop; (2) filtered noise burst with a low-pass that closes from ~6 kHz to ~300 Hz over ~0.5 s; (3) a granular crackle or flutter layer with small pulses at 0.7-1.2 s; (4) a short, dark rumble tail. Roughly 20 dB decay by 0.6 s. Aim for a peak around -2 dBFS, RMS around -12 dB, and about -14 LUFS. Keep it mono and avoid bright content above 5 kHz after the first half-second. Produce several variants for variety.

### `sounds/guns/sonicshotgun_shot_c.ogg`

**A heavy, bass-dominated noisy blast that swells to a peak around 0.2 s and then rumbles and decays over about 1.4 s, like a low sonic shotgun boom.** *(boomy, noisy, bassy, rumble, blast, low-end heavy, sustained, dark; confidence medium)*

- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it is centered and can be spatialized by the engine as a point source. A player-fired weapon sound works fine this way, and it will not collapse or phase when positioned in 3D.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack of about 0.2 s to the peak, with the loudest section from 0.2 to 0.4 s. It then drops about 8 dB, holds a fluctuating sustain from 0.5 to 0.9 s, and decays to -20 dB by about 1.15 s and -40 dB by about 1.35 s. The tail is short and smooth, with only about 0.02 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the bass (about 58%) and sub (about 17%) bands, with a centroid of about 300 Hz and 85% rolloff near 300 Hz. The spectrogram shows a bright band from 20 to 300 Hz, with noisy, speckled mids and highs up to about 10 kHz during the first half that fade to nothing later. The centroid falls from about 400 Hz to about 135 Hz over the clip. The very faint peaks at 16-17 kHz are probably codec artifacts. The texture is noisy and rumbling with no clear tonal lines, so the measured flatness of 0.0 looks unreliable.
- **Role:** Firing sound for the Sonic Shotgun, one of several shot variants (suffix _c) played on the player's fire event. It acts as a heavy, bassy sci-fi shotgun blast.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.21 s Fast swell of broadband noise and low thump from the start up to the loudest point; no sharp click, and the onset is blurred by the sub and bass energy.; 0.21–0.45 s Peak of the blast: dense sub and bass rumble with the brightest noisy mid and high content, peaking around -2 dBFS.; 0.45–1.15 s Sustained, slightly fluttering low rumble with a secondary bump near 0.79 s while the highs fall away.; 1.15–1.57 s Fading low-frequency tail that drops quickly below -40 dB and ends in near silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a one-shot of about 1.4-1.6 s. Layer a sub boom or pitch-dropping thump (40-120 Hz) with a band-limited noise burst (200 Hz-8 kHz) that has a fast swell and fades over about 0.5 s. Add a rumbling low tail with slight amplitude flutter, plus an optional sonic zap or air-pressure whoosh for the sci-fi flavor. Roll off the highs above about 10 kHz. Target a peak near -2 dBFS, about -13 LUFS integrated and RMS near -12 dBFS. Make 2-3 variants with small pitch and timing changes.

### `sounds/guns/teslagun_reload.ogg`

**A two-part sci-fi weapon reload: a quiet, rattly mechanical handling phase with a couple of clunks, then a gap and a loud, sustained, low-heavy electrical charge-up surge that fades out.** *(mechanical, electronic, noisy, buzzy, rumbling, layered, charge-up, transient; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.35 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -2.5 dBFS, RMS -19.4 dBFS, -17.1 LUFS, true peak -2.2 dBTP; centroid 3492 Hz, flatness 0.004; 5 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned as a point source at the player, which suits a first-person weapon sound and means it will not widen in game.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Quiet start around -20 to -28 dB per tenth of a second, with small bumps at about 0.05 s and 0.66 s. It drops to near silence at about 0.8-1.0 s. The surge begins abruptly at about 1.2 s and swells to its peak near 1.4 s. It stays loud at -14 to -22 dB through about 2.1 s, then tapers over about 0.15 s to a clean end. The measured 'attack' of 1.4 s refers to the whole file, not a single hit.
- **Frequency profile:** Heavy low end, with about 31% of energy in the bass band and 8% in the sub band. The loud second half is dominated by a broad 100-300 Hz mass of energy, with a dense, noisy mid and high texture extending past 10 kHz. The centroid is about 3.5 kHz overall, rising to about 5 kHz in the last quarter. The first half is brighter and sparser, with mid-range rattle. The spectrum is broadband and busy, with some very high peaks around 16-17 kHz, likely codec or noise artifacts.
- **Role:** Player reload sound for the tesla gun: mechanical handling and latching, then the coil or capacitor charging up to signal the weapon is ready. The Qwen notes describe an engine and a door slam, which do not match the measured evidence; the engine-rev impression probably comes from the buzzy, sustained surge.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.60 s Quiet mechanical rattling and handling, with a low thump and mid-range grit; several small clicks and scrapes.; 0.60–0.75 s Sharper clunk or latch hit (onset near 0.66 s) with a broadband transient, then a quick decay.; 0.75–1.05 s Near-silent gap with only a faint tail, the quietest part of the file.; 1.05–1.20 s Two or three small clicks or clunks (onsets at 1.08 and 1.22 s) that lead into the surge.; 1.20–2.20 s Loud, dense electrical or motor-like surge. Strong low-frequency energy (roughly 100-300 Hz) with a noisy, crackling upper band, held at a high level.; 2.20–2.35 s Fast fade-out of the surge to silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a roughly 2.3-2.4 s one-shot in two sections. First, 0.7 s of light metallic handling with a clunk or latch at about 0.65 s. Leave a short near-silent gap, then add two small clicks at about 1.1 s. At about 1.2 s, add a charge-up layer of buzzing electrical hum with crackle plus a low 100-300 Hz body, held for about 1 s with a rising brightness sweep, and fade it out over about 0.15 s. Aim for a peak around -3 dBFS, with the surge near -17 LUFS integrated and the handling section about 10 dB quieter. Keep it mono and avoid clipping.

### `sounds/guns/teslagun_shot_a.ogg`

**A loud, broadband, crackling electrical blast with a heavy low-end thump that decays over about two seconds into a sputtering, rumbling tail.** *(noisy, electric, crackle, boomy, blast, energetic, decaying, bright-to-dark; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 3.44 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -0.8 dBFS, RMS -16.0 dBFS, -13.8 LUFS, true peak 0.8 dBTP; centroid 2266 Hz, flatness 0.002; 11 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned and attenuated by the engine, which suits a player-fired weapon sound that needs to be localized.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Near-instant onset, with the measured peak at about 0.43 s inside a loud, dense opening. It holds near full level to about 0.4 s, falls 20 dB by about 1.1 s and 40 dB by about 2.0 s, then a sputtering tail runs to about 3.0 s. About 0.47 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Most energy is in the sub and bass range (about 63% below 250 Hz), giving a heavy thump. Wideband noise extends to 10 kHz and beyond early on, with a spectral centroid of about 3 kHz in the first quarter that collapses to about 50 Hz by the end. Low flatness value is probably skewed by the strong low end, but the spectrogram shows a noisy texture. Faint peaks near 16-17 kHz may be encoder or electrical sizzle.
- **Role:** Firing sound of the Tesla gun, a player-fired electric weapon: a heavy zap-blast with a crackling discharge tail.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.50 s Dense burst of broadband noise and sub/bass thump, the loudest part, with bright hissing crackle reaching above 10 kHz.; 0.50–1.40 s Sustained noisy, buzzy body with strong low end; high frequencies thin out progressively.; 1.40–2.80 s Sputtering tail of small crackle and rumble pulses (about 11 detected onsets, roughly every 0.1-0.2 s) fading into low-frequency rumble.; 2.80–3.00 s Last faint low-frequency residue fading to silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer about 3.4 s total. (1) A sub/bass thump or boom of 40-150 Hz, with a short punchy decay of about 0.5 s. (2) A bright, dense electric zap or noise burst for the first 0.5 s, with hiss up to 12 kHz and a quick lowpass sweep. (3) Arcing crackle pulses and sputters spaced 0.1-0.2 s apart across 1.4-2.8 s, getting sparser and quieter. (4) A low rumble tail that fades out. Aim for a peak near -1 dBFS and about -14 LUFS integrated, and keep the file mono. The tail should decay smoothly with no abrupt cutoff.

### `sounds/guns/teslagun_shot_b.ogg`

**A loud, noisy tesla-gun blast with a heavy low-end thump and bright crackling hiss that fades over about 3 seconds into a dull rumbling tail.** *(noisy, boomy, electric, crackle, explosive, broadband, decaying; confidence medium)*

- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned by the engine, which is fine for a player-fired weapon; spatialization will come from game panning and attenuation.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.

Sustains at about -10 dB until roughly 0.3 s, steps down to about -20 dB by 1.1 s, then decays gradually: -40 dB at 2.77 s, with a fade to silence by about 3.2 s. The tail is long, with a slightly stuttering, uneven decay.

The centroid falls from 2.7 kHz to about 190 Hz across the clip.

Loudest section.; 0.90–2.00 s Highs drop away quickly. The sound becomes a rumbling low-mid noise with fluttering, irregular texture; centroid falls to around 490 Hz.; 2.00–3.20 s Dull low rumble and faint rattle with small flickers (onsets near 1.75 s and 1.89 s) fading toward silence.; 3.20–3.44 s Almost silent tail, with the last 0.24 s below audibility.

Layer (1) a sub/bass thump or low boom with a quick pitch drop for the first 0.5 s, (2) bright filtered noise bursts with electric crackle and zap sweeps at 2-10 kHz, fading out over about 1 s, and (3) a low rumbling noise tail with a slow fade and a few small flutters from 1 to 3 s. ### `sounds/guns/thompson_reload.ogg`

**A short mechanical reload sequence of three clustered bursts of metallic clacks and rattles (likely mag release, magazine insertion, bolt or slide), with quiet gaps between them.** *(mechanical, metallic, transient, noisy, clicky, bright, foley; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.76 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -4.6 dBFS, RMS -27.9 dBFS, -23.5 LUFS, true peak -4.2 dBTP; centroid 5663 Hz, flatness 0.003; 9 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono, so it will be positioned by the game engine. It plays from the player as a close, centered first-person foley sound that does not need width.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Multiple discrete transient events with no sustain. Each has a near-instant attack and a fast decay (about 0.06s to -20 dB, 0.13s to -40 dB). The peak is the final clack at about 1.31s. Gaps of low-level noise separate the events, and the last 0.26s is silent.
- **Frequency profile:** Bright and noise-like (flatness 0.003 is low in the measurement, yet the spectrogram shows broadband bursts). Centroid is about 5.7 kHz, and most energy sits in the presence (47%) and mid (30%) bands, about 1-9 kHz, with 20% in the air band. Bursts show a vertical low-frequency thump extending down to 20 Hz, strongest at about 0.29s, 0.73s and 1.33s, though the sub and bass bands hold little energy overall. Rolloff is about 9 kHz.
- **Role:** Player reload sound for the Thompson submachine gun, played once when the reload starts and covering mag out, mag in and bolt action. The path and event name confirm this, and the audio-model notes of gunshots are probably wrong, since the measurements show no shot-like boom.
- **Elements:** 0.13–0.35 s First cluster of rattly metallic clicks, probably the magazine release or removal, with broadband energy and a few sharper taps around 0.22-0.29s.; 0.52–0.82 s Second and loudest of the early events, a denser, scrapier metallic impact with a strong transient near 0.73s, probably the new magazine seating.; 0.94–1.25 s Faint isolated ticks and light handling noise with a very quiet mid-band tone near 1-2 kHz.; 1.30–1.50 s Final sharp, heavy metallic clack with a strong low thump, probably the bolt or cocking handle slam, then a fast decay.; 1.50–1.76 s Near-silent tail with only faint residual hiss.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a roughly 1.7s one-shot with three or four distinct mechanical beats: a mag release click and slide, a heavier mag-in impact with a short rattle, a quiet pause with handling ticks, and a final bolt clack that is the loudest (about -4 dBFS peak, about -23 LUFS integrated). Layer sharp metal-on-metal transients (2-8 kHz) with a short low thump of 60-150 Hz on the big hits. Keep decays under 0.15s, keep it dry with no reverb, add a little silence at the end, and render in mono.

The very high peaks (14-20 kHz) are likely codec or resampling artifacts. The flatness value of 0.0 is not a reliable sign of tonality here, since the spectrogram shows a noise-like texture.

Roll the highs off progressively over the tail. Aim for a peak near -1 dBFS, about -19 LUFS integrated, and keep it mono.
