- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the bass and low-mids (about 55% combined), with a centroid around 1.1 kHz and an 85% rolloff near 2.3 kHz. Very noise-like, with a broad dense spectrum that is brightest in the first 0.1-0.2 s and loses highs as it decays. There is a little mid and presence energy and only a weak, faint ultrasonic or air component (peaks at 14-23 kHz are probably encoding or processing artifacts). No sustained tones or harmonics.
- **Role:** Player-fired Thompson submachine-gun shot, one of several variants (the 'c' file) that are likely randomized to avoid repetition.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.12 s Initial muzzle blast: loud broadband noise burst with strong bass and low-mids, peaking near 0.02 s, with a faint very high-frequency click near the start.; 0.06–0.10 s Short secondary crack or mechanical snap (bolt or echo reflection), visible as a brief vertical stroke in the waveform and a high-frequency blip in the spectrogram.; 0.12–0.75 s Decaying reverberant tail of noisy rumble and room or environment reflections, getting darker and quieter over time.; 0.75–1.01 s Near-silent residual tail fading to nothing.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a ~0.8-1.0 s one-shot. Layer a sharp noise-burst crack (a few ms attack), a low thump or boom body around 60-300 Hz for ~100 ms, a short mechanical click or snap near 60-100 ms, and a medium-length reverb/room tail decaying about 40 dB over ~0.7 s. Keep the spectrum mostly below ~3 kHz with modest top end. Target a peak near -1 dBFS and about -19 LUFS integrated. Make 3-4 variants with slight pitch and timing differences. Keep it mono.

### `sounds/guns/vector_reload.ogg`

**A mono, gritty mechanical reload sequence of several clacky metal-and-plastic handling noises (mag out, mag in, bolt or slide action) with a clear silent gap near 0.95-1.10 s.** *(transient, mechanical, metallic, clacky, noisy, handling, multi-hit; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.54 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -1.1 dBFS, RMS -19.3 dBFS, -15.9 LUFS, true peak 0.7 dBTP; centroid 3972 Hz, flatness 0.035; 9 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono, so it is positioned entirely by the game engine. It will pan and attenuate with distance as a spatial first-person or world sound without baked-in width. The Qwen claim of left panning is unsupported by the single-channel file.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Multiple sharp transients with near-instant attacks, each decaying quickly (about 0.09 s to -20 dB, 0.16 s to -40 dB). Moderate-level activity through 0-0.9 s, then complete silence from about 0.95 to 1.10 s, then the loudest hits at 1.19 s and a final click at 1.42-1.52 s. The file ends abruptly with no tail.
- **Frequency profile:** Mid-dominant (58% in the mid band, 25% presence, 11% air), with a centroid around 4 kHz and a rolloff near 7 kHz. Low flatness (0.035) but the energy is broadband and clicky. There are vertical strokes from sub-bass to over 10 kHz at each hit, giving a thump plus a metallic bite. One faint tone near 17 kHz is likely an artifact. Little true bass or sub content.
- **Role:** Player weapon reload sound for the Vector submachine gun: magazine out, magazine in and bolt or charging-handle action, played as a single one-shot when reloading.
- **Elements:** 0.02–0.20 s Double clack at the start (onsets near 0.05 s and about 0.15 s), a bright, broadband mechanical hit like a magazine release or latch, with low thump underneath.; 0.20–0.55 s Quiet rattles and small ticks (onset around 0.3 s) with a low-level tail, like handling or sliding parts.; 0.55–0.78 s Biggest cluster in the first half, a loud bright clack or slap around 0.62-0.68 s, like a magazine seating, with a short noisy decay.; 0.78–0.95 s Faint trailing rattles and a small tick near 0.83 s, fading into silence.; 0.95–1.10 s Digital silence gap.; 1.10–1.38 s Loudest section: two strong, bright metallic clacks at about 1.19 s and 1.26 s (peak at 1.19 s), like a charging handle or bolt racking, with a short ringing decay.; 1.40–1.54 s Final short, bright click-clack (onsets 1.42 s and 1.52 s) that is cut off at the end of the file.
- **Recreation notes:** Build a roughly 1.5 s one-shot from separate foley hits: a mag release click and mag-out slide (about 0-0.2 s), a handling rattle, a firm mag insertion clack (about 0.65 s), then a short pause and a bolt rack of two sharp metal clacks (about 1.2 s) and a final light click. Layer a short low thump (100-200 Hz) under the main hits and bright metal or plastic snaps (2-6 kHz). Keep it dry and mono, with peaks around -1 dBFS and integrated loudness near -16 LUFS. The original's mid-gap silence is not essential, but keep the pacing of reload stages, and avoid long tails so it stays responsive in game. The Qwen notes describe gunshots at 10 s, which contradicts the 1.5 s duration, so treat them as unreliable.

### `sounds/guns/vector_shot_a.ogg`

**A single punchy gunshot with an instant, heavy low-end thump and a noisy mid crack that decays into a short, dull reverberant tail.** *(transient, percussive, boomy, noisy, gunshot, punchy, short tail; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.60 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -0.8 dBFS, RMS -14.3 dBFS, -15.3 LUFS, true peak -0.1 dBTP; centroid 1119 Hz, flatness 0.076; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It sits centered and is easy to position in 3D by the engine. It suits a player-fired weapon sound with no baked-in width.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Instant attack at 0 s with the peak at -0.8 dBFS. Fast decay: -20 dB by about 0.2 s and -40 dB by about 0.48 s. Fades to silence with about 0.12 s of trailing silence; total length 0.6 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the bass and low-mids (about 37% low-mid, 27% bass, 10% sub), with a centroid near 1.1 kHz and 85% rolloff around 1.55 kHz. The first 0.1 s has a broadband noisy burst reaching 10 kHz or more, which darkens quickly. Flatness of 0.076 shows a mix of tonal low thump and noise. There are no stable tonal peaks.
- **Role:** Player weapon fire sound for the Vector (submachine-gun-style) gun, played on each shot. The short length suits rapid repeated firing.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.10 s Muzzle blast: instant full-scale attack with a broadband crack and a strong sub/bass thump below about 200 Hz.; 0.10–0.30 s Body decay: low-mid rumble and mid noise fade quickly, with ragged, pulsing low-frequency texture suggesting a short room or reflection tail.; 0.30–0.48 s Tail: dark, low-level rumble and hiss fading toward silence, with highs gone.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer three parts: a sharp noise-burst crack of 5-10 ms with some highs, a pitched-down low thump or kick of 40-120 Hz lasting about 100 ms, and a short dark room tail of roughly 0.3 s. Keep the total near 0.5-0.6 s with a fast fade so rapid fire doesn't mud up. Target a peak near -1 dBFS and about -15 LUFS integrated. Add slight pitch or sample variation, as this is variant 'a' of several. Keep it mono.

### `sounds/guns/vector_shot_b.ogg`

**A short, punchy, bass-heavy gunshot with an instant noisy crack that decays into a rumbling, crackling tail in about half a second.** *(transient, boomy, noisy, gunshot, punchy, low-heavy, short; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.60 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -0.9 dBFS, RMS -13.0 dBFS, -14.6 LUFS, true peak 0.4 dBTP; centroid 907 Hz, flatness 0.081; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It is easy to position in 3D and stays centered and punchy in game. A close first-person shot is well served by mono, and rapid fire will not cause phase problems.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Instant attack (0 s) at a peak of -0.9 dBFS. Fast decay: -20 dB by ~0.23 s and -40 dB by ~0.48 s. There is no sustain. The tail is short and fades to silence by ~0.5 s, leaving ~0.12 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the low end: about 25% sub, 19% bass and 23% low-mid, with the centroid near 900 Hz and 85% rolloff near 1.2 kHz. The first ~0.1 s has a brighter noise burst reaching 10 kHz, with little air content. Flatness is low (0.08), so it is noise-like but dominated by low thump and rumble. There are no stable tonal peaks.
- **Role:** Player firing sound for the 'Vector' submachine gun (guns.vectorFire), one of several shot variants (the _b file) that are rotated to avoid repetition.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.12 s Instant full-band attack: a loud muzzle blast with a strong sub/bass thump and a noisy crack extending up to about 10 kHz.; 0.12–0.35 s Rapid decay into a crackling, rumbling body; low-frequency energy flutters in vertical striations and the upper highs fade.; 0.35–0.48 s Quiet low-frequency tail with faint rattle and room-like residue; the highs are nearly gone.; 0.48–0.60 s Near-silent trailing fade; the last ~0.12 s is effectively silent.
- **Recreation notes:** Build 3 layers. (1) A sub/low thump of 40-120 Hz with a fast pitch drop, ~0.15 s. (2) A bright noise crack or transient, 1-8 kHz, ~30-50 ms. (3) A short, dense, crackly low-mid body or tail, ~0.3 s, fading out by ~0.5 s. Keep the total length at ~0.5-0.6 s with no reverb tail so it works at high rates of fire. Target a peak near -1 dBFS and about -14 to -15 LUFS integrated. Make 2-4 variations. Treat the Qwen 'metallic' remark as unverified; the spectrogram shows only noise and low-end thump. Its 0.8 s duration also conflicts with the measured 0.6 s.

### `sounds/guns/vector_shot_c.ogg`

**A short, punchy, bass-heavy gunshot with an instant noisy crack and thump that decays into a rumbling tail in about 0.45 s.** *(transient, boomy, noisy, gunshot, bass-heavy, punchy, low-end, short tail; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.60 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -0.7 dBFS, RMS -14.4 dBFS, -16.3 LUFS, true peak -0.0 dBTP; centroid 703 Hz, flatness 0.057; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, single channel. This suits a positional in-game weapon fire sound because the engine can pan and attenuate it by distance without phase issues.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Instant attack at 0 s with the peak at -0.7 dBFS. Fast initial drop of about -9 dB by 0.1 s, reaching -20 dB by about 0.18 s and -40 dB by about 0.43 s. Smooth decaying tail, then silence for the final ~0.13 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the sub and bass bands (about 65% combined, strongest around 40-200 Hz), with some low-mid body. The spectral centroid is about 700 Hz, with 85% rolloff near 775 Hz. The first ~0.1 s has a broadband noisy burst reaching above 10 kHz that gives the crack, and the highs vanish after about 0.3 s. Low flatness (0.057) shows a fairly tonal, thumpy low end under the noise.
- **Role:** Player firing sound for the 'Vector' weapon (guns.vectorFire), most likely one of several variants (the '_c' suffix) so repeated shots do not sound identical. The listening notes describe falling bodies and explosions, which the measurements do not support; this is a single shot with a short tail.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.09 s Initial blast: broadband crack with a strong sub and bass thump, loudest at the very start, with a bright burst of high-frequency noise up to the top of the spectrum.; 0.09–0.35 s Body and decay: low-mid and bass rumble with mottled, irregular texture that suggests a short reflection or reverb tail, fading steadily.; 0.35–0.47 s Dying tail: faint low rumble that thins out and loses all highs.; 0.47–0.60 s Near-silence, with only a trace of residue before the end of the file.
- **Recreation notes:** Build a single-shot gunshot of about 0.45-0.6 s. Layer a sub/bass thump (about 40-150 Hz, pitch-dropping sine or kick, ~0.15 s), a short broadband noise crack (about 50-80 ms, bright, with a fast decay), and a mid-heavy body layer of filtered noise or a mechanical clack. Add a short, dark room tail that fades within ~0.4 s with little high-frequency content. Make several slight variations for rapid fire. Aim for a peak near -1 dBFS, about -16 LUFS integrated, and trim the end cleanly to avoid clicks.

### `sounds/effects/biodeath.ogg`

**A loud, heavy, noisy burst with a deep rumbling low end and a gritty wet-sounding texture that lingers for about 1.5 s and then fades into a long tail, like a bio-organic death or collapse.** *(noisy, boomy, rumble, gritty, wet, layered, impact, decaying; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 3.99 s, 32000 Hz, 1 ch; peak 1.8 dBFS, RMS -17.4 dBFS, -15.8 LUFS, true peak 2.0 dBTP; centroid 2325 Hz, flatness 0.327; 6 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned and attenuated by the engine in 3D. Use a single localized emitter so the death sound reads as coming from the mob.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack of about 0.09 s to the peak. The loud body holds for about 1.0-1.5 s, with the bass stable until about 1.4 s, then a cut-off in sub energy. It decays to -20 dB at about 1.57 s and -40 dB at about 2.93 s, with a quiet tail to about 3.5 s, then silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is heaviest in the sub and bass (20-250 Hz, about 44% together), with a broad noisy spread through the mids to the air band above 6 kHz. Spectral flatness is 0.33, so it is mixed noise and low tonal rumble. The centroid is about 2.3 kHz overall; it is brighter at the start and in the last quarter, where the tail's few remaining sounds are mostly hiss. There are no clear harmonic peaks.
- **Role:** Death sound for a hostile biological or organic creature or hazard (event effects.biodeath, hostile category). It is a heavy, gory collapse or burst. The Qwen description of an explosion plus splash fits the wet, noisy, low-heavy character, though the explosion label is a guess.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.15 s Sharp impact transient with a broadband burst reaching a peak at about 0.09 s, slightly clipped.; 0.15–1.50 s Sustained heavy sub and bass rumble with crackly, granular mid and high noise on top. Several small bursts at about 0.87, 1.15 and 1.23 s add a rough, churning feel.; 1.50–3.00 s Energy drops and darkens. Bass fades through the 100-300 Hz range and the highs thin out. Small noisy blips near 2.39 and 2.58 s.; 3.00–3.55 s Faint, dark tail of low rumble and a few residual noise flecks that dissolve into silence.; 3.55–3.99 s Near silence (trailing silence of about 0.86 s by the measurements).
- **Recreation notes:** Build it from about 4 s of audio with a tail that fades by about 3.5 s. Layers: (1) a short punchy transient or thump at 0 s; (2) a sub and bass boom or body drop of 40-150 Hz, held for about 1.2 s then faded; (3) wet, squelchy, granular noise with crackles and slimy bursts in the 500 Hz-8 kHz range, with a few extra blips at about 0.9-1.3 s; (4) a dark, low-passed noise tail. Aim for a peak around -1 dBFS (avoid the original's clipping), RMS around -17 dB and integrated loudness around -16 LUFS. Keep the file mono.

### `sounds/effects/bullet_impact_a.ogg`

**A short, punchy bullet impact: a quick tick, then a loud noisy crunch with heavy low thuds and a faint metallic ring that decays over about half a second.** *(transient, impact, noisy, boomy, metallic, punchy, short; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.98 s, 22050 Hz, 1 ch; peak 0.4 dBFS, RMS -20.2 dBFS, -19.0 LUFS, true peak 0.6 dBTP; centroid 1272 Hz, flatness 0.19; 1 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned by the engine in 3D, so it reads as a point-source hit at the impact location without any baked-in width.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Near-silent start, then a fast jump to peak by about 0.05-0.12 s. Sustains loud until about 0.25 s, drops about 20 dB by 0.12 s after the peak and about 40 dB by 0.5 s. A quiet ring tail lasts to about 0.7 s, with silence for roughly the last 0.3 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Broadband, with energy spread from sub-bass to mids: about 16% sub, 22% bass, 29% low-mid and 22% mid, with little air. Centroid is about 1.3 kHz and the 85% rolloff is about 2.3 kHz. The thuds below 150 Hz give weight, the noise burst gives the crunch, and a thin tonal line near 780 Hz in the tail gives a metallic ring. Flatness is 0.19, so it is mixed noise and tone.
- **Role:** Bullet impact on a surface (the event is effects.bulletimpact in the player category), likely a hit on a hard or metallic material. It plays as short feedback on a shot landing.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Very quiet initial tick or click before the main body.; 0.05–0.25 s Loud broadband noise burst (crunch/crack) reaching up to about 6 kHz, with a faint descending tonal sweep around 300-600 Hz.; 0.08–0.34 s Two or three heavy low-frequency thuds below about 150 Hz at roughly 0.08-0.12 s, 0.17-0.24 s and 0.26-0.33 s, giving a boomy body.; 0.25–0.70 s Fading tail with a thin, steady metallic ring near 750-800 Hz and light noisy residue.
- **Recreation notes:** Total length about 0.6-0.8 s. Layer: (1) a tiny click or tick transient; (2) a 150-250 ms noise crack or crunch, band-limited to about 300 Hz-6 kHz, with a slight downward tonal sweep; (3) a low thud or boom layer at 40-150 Hz, with a couple of stuttered pulses for body; (4) a quiet, short, resonant metallic ring around 700-800 Hz decaying by about 0.5 s. Peak at or just below -1 dBFS, and avoid clipping. Aim for roughly -19 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono. The original has some inconsistency in how the Qwen notes describe timing, so the structure above is based on the measurements.

### `sounds/effects/bullet_impact_b.ogg`

**A short, loud, crunchy impact with a heavy low-end thump burst and a brief metallic ring that fades into a quiet tail, like a bullet hitting a hard surface.** *(transient, impact, noisy, boomy, metallic, crunchy, short; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.98 s, 22050 Hz, 1 ch; peak 0.4 dBFS, RMS -20.2 dBFS, -19.0 LUFS, true peak 0.6 dBTP; centroid 1272 Hz, flatness 0.19; 1 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned by the engine in game, so it suits a localized hit and spatial attenuation.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Near-instant onset, peaking at about 0.12s. Strong sustain of roughly 0.25s with two or three thump pulses. Falls about 20 dB by 0.25s and 40 dB by 0.5s, then a very quiet tail to about 0.9s and silence for the last 0.3s.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is spread broadly: about 16% sub, 22% bass, 29% low-mid and 22% mid, with little air. The centroid is about 1.3 kHz and the 85% rolloff about 2.3 kHz. The noisy burst reaches up to about 6 kHz or more early on, with heavy thumps below 100 Hz. A narrow tonal line near 800 Hz gives the metallic ring. Flatness of 0.19 shows a mix of noise and tone.
- **Role:** Bullet impact or hit feedback played for the player (effects.bulletimpact), a punchy impact on a surface or target.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Very quiet tick or lead-in click just before the main hit.; 0.05–0.25 s Main impact: broadband noisy crunch with strong sub and bass thumps near 0.08-0.12s and 0.18-0.25s, plus bright debris-like texture up to several kHz.; 0.25–0.40 s Low thud trailing off as the noise thins, with a last bass pulse near 0.3s.; 0.24–0.90 s Faint thin metallic ring or whine near 750-800 Hz, slightly sagging in pitch, sitting over a fading low-level tail.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer a short noisy crack or debris burst, a 60-100 Hz sub thump (two pulses about 0.1s apart), and a faint metallic ping around 800 Hz with a quick fade. Keep the total to about 0.6-1.0s with a quiet tail. Peak near -1 dBFS, about -19 LUFS integrated. Avoid clipping. Make 2-3 variations since this is a 'b' variant.

### `sounds/effects/bullet_impact_c.ogg`

**A short, punchy, noisy impact with a heavy low-end thump, a brief metallic ring and a quick decaying tail, like a bullet hitting a surface.** *(impact, transient, noisy, boomy, metallic, punchy, short; confidence medium)*

Add a slight downward pitch sweep from 600 to 250 Hz in the body. Peak near -1 dBFS, integrated about -19 LUFS, and avoid clipping. Leave a short silent tail.

### `sounds/effects/bullet_impact_d.ogg`

### `sounds/effects/C4Charge_Init.ogg`

**A mechanical C4 arming sequence: two sharp clicks, a smaller tick, then a short steady high-pitched electronic beep at about 4 kHz, all over a low thump rumble, followed by a quiet tail.** *(transient, clicky, mechanical, electronic, beep, tonal, explosive-device; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.88 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 4.2 dBFS, RMS -19.4 dBFS, -14.9 LUFS, true peak 4.2 dBTP; centroid 5956 Hz, flatness 0.198; 4 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono (single channel). It will be positioned by the engine as a point source at the block, so the click-and-beep cues localize well in game.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Very fast attack of about 10 ms on the first click, which drops 20 dB within 20 ms. Two more transient hits at roughly 0.1 s and 0.21 s, then a flat-topped beep sustained from 0.29 s to about 0.43 s with an abrupt stop. Low-level tail decays to -40 dB by about 0.55 s, and the final 0.23 s is silent.
- **Frequency profile:** Clicks are broadband, with energy mostly in the mids (about 43%), presence (27%) and air (28%), and a centroid near 6 kHz. A strong low-frequency thump below about 200 Hz shows on the spectrogram, though it makes up little of the measured band energy. The beep is a narrow tonal line near 3.8 kHz, the only prominent peak. A weak resonance band sits around 900 Hz. Overall texture is a mix of noisy transients and one pure tone.
- **Role:** Sound of a C4 explosive charge being initialized or armed when placed or activated, as the block event c4charge.init suggests: clicks of a mechanical switch followed by a confirmation beep.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.08 s Loud sharp click/thump with broadband energy up to the air band and a heavy low-frequency body; peak clips slightly.; 0.10–0.17 s Second click of similar brightness but a little quieter, with a short low thud underneath.; 0.20–0.27 s Smaller, softer tick with a scattered mid-range texture and a low bump.; 0.29–0.43 s Onset click followed by a steady, pure, high-pitched electronic beep at about 3.8-4 kHz, flat in pitch and level, with a hard cutoff around 0.43 s.; 0.43–0.88 s Quiet decaying tail of low rumble and faint noise fading to silence; the last ~0.23 s is essentially silent.
- **Recreation notes:** Make an original arming sound of about 0.8-0.9 s. Layer 3 short clicks (plastic or metal switch snaps with a low thump) at roughly 0, 0.1 and 0.21 s, getting a little softer on the third. Add a 0.12-0.15 s pure sine or square beep near 3.5-4 kHz at about 0.29 s, with a hard gate and no pitch movement. Put a quiet low rumble tail underneath, fading by about 0.6 s. Target about -15 LUFS integrated with the peak below 0 dBFS, and avoid the clipping in the original. Keep it mono.

### `sounds/effects/C4Charge_Tick.ogg`

A short broadband burst at the onset adds a little click, and a weak low-frequency thump appears below 200 Hz in the first 0.15 s. The listening notes claim about 60 Hz, which conflicts with the measurements, so trust the 1.7 kHz tone.

The amplitude bulges and peaks at the start and end of this section.; 0.20–0.55 s Quiet decaying tail. The 1.7 kHz tone lingers with slight amplitude flutter and fades.; 0.55–0.70 s Near-silent residual fade-out.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a roughly 0.7 s one-shot, with the audible part lasting about 0.2 s and a short ringing tail. Layer a pure or slightly square-ish tone at about 1.7 kHz, a quieter harmonic at 3.4 kHz, and a tiny noise click at the onset. Add a subtle amplitude double-bump over the first 0.2 s, then a quick fade of about 0.15 s. Target a peak near -1 dBFS and about -15 LUFS integrated, and keep the low end clean.

### `sounds/effects/disintegrate.ogg`

- **Recreation notes:** Make it about 5s total. Layer (1) a short low thump or sub burst of about 0.3-1s, (2) a bright filtered noise or fire-sizzle bed with a long exponential decay of about 4s, and (3) randomly spaced crackle and pop ticks that thin out toward the end, especially from 2.5s on. Add a slight high-frequency sweep or sparkle if desired. The original is hot and clips (peak +3 dBFS, LUFS about -9), so aim lower: about -14 to -12 LUFS with peaks under -1 dBFS and no clipping. Leave a short silent tail.

### `sounds/effects/explosion1_a.ogg`

**A very loud, bass-heavy explosion with a fast roar-like onset and a rumbling, crackling decay lasting about 2.2 seconds.** *(boomy, explosive, noisy, rumble, low-end, distorted, crackle, one-shot; confidence high)*

- **Measured:** 2.47 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 0.7 dBFS, RMS -9.7 dBFS, -10.3 LUFS, true peak 0.7 dBTP; centroid 196 Hz, flatness 0.002; 7 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** very loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned and attenuated by the game engine, so it works as a point source with distance falloff; no baked-in width.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack (~0.08 s) to a clipped peak, near-full level held until about 1.0 s, then a stepped drop and gradual decay: -20 dB at about 1.24 s, -40 dB at about 2.03 s, and silence from about 2.19 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Dominated by sub and bass (about 25% sub, 65% bass; centroid about 196 Hz, 85% rolloff about 172 Hz). The strongest energy is in 20-250 Hz as a dense, noise-like rumble (flatness 0.002 is skewed by the heavy low end). A thin band of mid and high noise appears at the start and fades quickly, with almost no air above 5 kHz after the first 0.5 s. No stable tonal peaks.
- **Role:** Explosion sound, such as TNT or a creeper blast, played from the effects.explosion1 event on the master category.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.10 s Sharp blast onset with broadband burst reaching up to the highest frequencies and a quick peak at about 0.08 s, clipping.; 0.10–1.10 s Sustained heavy low-frequency roar and boom, loudest section, with turbulent fluctuating energy and some brief bursts around 0.5-0.7 s.; 1.10–2.00 s Level drops by roughly 6 dB; rumble continues with irregular debris-like crackle pulses around 1.37, 1.53, 1.81 and 1.93 s while brightness fades.; 2.00–2.47 s Low rumble dies away into a quiet tail and then silence for the last ~0.28 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a roughly 2.3-2.5 s mono one-shot. Layers: (1) a short noise/crack transient of about 80 ms for the initial blast; (2) a deep sub boom with a pitch-down sweep from about 100 Hz to 40 Hz; (3) a filtered noise roar, low-passed around 500 Hz and sustained for about 1 s; (4) sparse debris crackles and rumble in the tail, with a low-pass that closes over time. Add a slight saturation but avoid the hard clipping of the original, aiming for about -1 dBFS peak and around -12 to -10 LUFS integrated. Fade out cleanly by about 2.3 s. The listening notes about a vehicle driving away and an 8.7 s duration conflict with the measurements and should be ignored; the debris-like pulses are likely the source of that impression.

### `sounds/effects/explosion1_b.ogg`

Mids and highs are present as a crackly, hissy texture mainly in the first 0.4 s (centroid 287 Hz in the first quarter, then about 160-170 Hz). The top end is almost absent by the end.

Add a few small secondary bumps around 0.7-1.2 s. Total length is about 1.6-1.8 s with a natural fade and no long reverb. Keep the high end subdued, since it is largely below 1 kHz. Aim for about -9 LUFS and a peak near 0 dBFS; avoid the heavy clipping and DC offset of the original by using a limiter instead. Make 2-3 variations for variety.

Keep the total length around 1.0-1.2 s with a smooth fade-out. Peak the mix at about -1 dBFS (the original clips at +3.8 dB, which should be avoided) and aim for roughly -16 LUFS integrated. Make two or three variants (this one is the 'a' version) with slight pitch and timing differences.

### `sounds/effects/steamarmor_jump_b.ogg`

**A loud, low-heavy, noisy mechanical rumble-burst with a jittery, rattling texture that decays over about a second, like a steam-powered armor thrust or clank.** *(noisy, boomy, mechanical, rumble, hissy, rattling, heavy, one-shot; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.21 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 3.8 dBFS, RMS -12.4 dBFS, -15.7 LUFS, true peak 3.8 dBTP; centroid 268 Hz, flatness 0.003; 2 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It sits centered on the player, which suits a self-triggered jump sound that needs no positional cue. The heavy low end will feel centered and weighty.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack of about 0.05 s to the peak, then a sustained loud body with fluctuating pulses to about 0.4 s, then a smooth decay: -20 dB at about 0.65 s and -40 dB at about 1.08 s, then a short silent tail of about 0.06 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is overwhelmingly in sub and bass: about 44% below 60 Hz and 40% at 60-250 Hz, with a centroid around 270 Hz and 85% rolloff near 194 Hz. The spectrogram shows a wide band of broadband noise-like energy rising from the lows, with highs fading faster over time. The low flatness figure comes from the dominant low end; the texture is rough and rumbly, not a pure tone. There is a faint narrow high-frequency streak around 12 kHz early on, which is minor.
- **Role:** Player jump sound for the steam-powered armor: a heavy pressurized mechanical thrust or burst with a rumbling release, one variant (b) of several.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Very quick onset ramping from quiet into the main burst.; 0.05–0.50 s Dense, loud low-frequency rumble and rattle burst with irregular amplitude pulses (a second accent near 0.35 s), with some mid and high hiss on top.; 0.50–1.08 s Gradual decay of the rumble, losing highs first, fading to a thin low tail.; 1.08–1.21 s Near-silent tail.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer a low thump or sub rumble (40-150 Hz) with a filtered noise burst for steam hiss and some metallic clank or rattle for the pulsing texture. Use a fast attack, a body of about 0.4 s with a second small accent near 0.35 s, and a decay to silence at about 1.1-1.2 s. Roll off the highs over the tail. The original peaks above 0 dBFS with some clipping, so avoid that: aim for a peak around -3 dBFS and about -16 LUFS integrated. Make 2-3 variants with slight pitch and timing changes.

### `sounds/effects/steamarmor_jump_c.ogg`

**A heavy, low-end rumbling burst of noisy, gritty energy with a quick onset and a gradual fade over about 1.2 seconds, like a steam-and-metal thump.** *(boomy, noisy, rumble, gritty, low-end, mechanical, steamy; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.21 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 2.3 dBFS, RMS -13.7 dBFS, -15.9 LUFS, true peak 2.3 dBTP; centroid 289 Hz, flatness 0.003; 1 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** very loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned as a point source at the player, so the spatial feel in game comes from the engine's panning and attenuation rather than the file.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Near-instant attack (about 0.06 s, peak at 0.07 s), then a steady decay: -20 dB by about 0.63 s and -40 dB by about 1.06 s. The tail runs to about 1.15 s with roughly 0.06 s of trailing silence. The waveform shows irregular bursts rather than a smooth curve.
- **Frequency profile:** Strongly low-weighted: about 42% of energy in the sub band and 34% in the bass band, with a centroid near 290 Hz and 85% rolloff near 260 Hz. Very low spectral flatness (0.003) means energy is concentrated in the low range, though the texture is gritty and rumbly rather than a clean tone, with no stable harmonic peaks. A brief burst of mid/high-mid noise appears in the first 0.2 s, and the highs are sparse.
- **Role:** Jump sound for the steam armor, played on the player: a heavy mechanical puff or thump as the steam-powered boots or suit launch the player.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.12 s Sudden onset of a dense, loud low-frequency thump with a brief brighter hiss/crunch in the upper mids (about 1-5 kHz) that peaks near 0.07 s.; 0.12–0.70 s Sustained, pulsing rumble with irregular, crackly modulation in the sub and bass range, gradually losing upper-mid content.; 0.70–1.21 s Low rumble thinning out and fading into a quiet tail, with energy concentrated below about 250 Hz.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer three elements. (1) A low thump or sub impact at about 40-120 Hz. (2) A short burst of filtered noise or steam hiss, bright in the first 0.1-0.2 s, then rolled off. (3) A faint metallic or mechanical rattle or crunch for the gritty texture. Keep the total length around 1.2 s with a fast attack and an exponential-like decay. The original is hot (peak above 0 dBFS with clipped samples, about -16 LUFS), so aim for a slightly lower, clean level, around -16 to -18 LUFS with peaks below -1 dBFS. Keep it mono and remove DC offset. Make a few variants, since this looks like one of several alternates (the _c suffix).

### `sounds/effects/steamarmor_jump_d.ogg`

**A heavy, low-frequency rumbling whoosh-burst with a noisy, churning texture that swells quickly and fades over about a second, like a hissing, pulsing steam-powered thrust.** *(rumbling, boomy, noisy, mechanical, pulsing, low-end heavy, steam, burst; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.21 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 3.8 dBFS, RMS -13.0 dBFS, -16.9 LUFS, true peak 3.8 dBTP; centroid 257 Hz, flatness 0.003; 2 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it will be positioned by the game engine at the player, giving a centered, anchored sound. This suits a player-attached sound, with no stereo width to compete with other effects.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Attack of about 0.1 s to the peak, with a second surge near 0.3 s. Sustain is held at high level until about 0.45 s, then decays. It is 20 dB down by about 0.63 s and 40 dB down by about 1.03 s, with a short silent tail at the end (about 0.06 s).
- **Frequency profile:** Dominated by sub and bass: about 53% of energy is sub and 26% bass, with a centroid around 257 Hz and an 85% rolloff near 194 Hz. Spectral flatness is very low, but this reflects the strong low-frequency concentration and no clear discrete peaks; the texture reads as a dense rumble or noise. A sparse speckle of mid and high content up to about 5-10 kHz in the first half fades by the end. The pulsing vertical striations suggest a rapid amplitude modulation, like a chuffing or rattling.
- **Role:** Jump sound for the steam armor, played on the player: a burst of steam or pressure venting that gives a heavy mechanical thrust. The Qwen description of an explosion or fireball is plausible in terms of weight, but the context and the short, decaying noisy rumble suggest a steam release rather than a literal explosion.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.10 s Fast onset of a low thump and noisy burst, rising quickly in level.; 0.10–0.50 s Loudest part: dense, churning sub and bass rumble with pulsing amplitude bursts (a second surge near 0.3 s), with some mid-frequency grit.; 0.50–1.00 s Gradually decaying rumble with a flickering, chuffing texture; highs fade away.; 1.00–1.21 s Quiet low tail dying out to near silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer a soft low thump or sub hit (about 40-100 Hz) with filtered noise for a steam hiss and chuff, adding amplitude modulation or short pulses at about 20-30 Hz for the churning texture. Add a small mechanical piston or valve click at the start, optionally. Duration about 1.1-1.2 s, with a fast 0.05-0.1 s attack and an exponential decay to silence. The original peaks above 0 dBFS with some clipping (87 samples), so avoid that: aim for a peak around -1 dBFS and about -17 LUFS integrated. Roll off the highs above about 5 kHz over time. Make several variants, since the event name suggests alternatives (jump 2).

### `sounds/items/antigrav_start_a.ogg`

**A loud, low-heavy rumbling whoosh-burst of noisy, distorted energy that surges for about half a second and then decays into a dark rumbling tail.** *(boomy, noisy, rumble, distorted, whoosh, electronic, heavy; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.81 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak 4.9 dBFS, RMS -12.7 dBFS, -12.5 LUFS, true peak 5.0 dBTP; centroid 262 Hz, flatness 0.006; 2 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** very loud. **Stereo:** Mono file, so no width. In game it will be positioned by the engine as a point source on the player, with no built-in stereo movement. The Qwen left-to-right claim conflicts with the mono measurement and should be ignored.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Instant attack with a peak at about 0.24s after two quick onsets (0.05s and 0.23s). It holds near full level to about 0.3s, then falls: -20 dB by about 0.39s and -40 dB by about 0.93s. The tail is very quiet, and the last 0.58s is effectively silent.
- **Frequency profile:** Mostly sub and bass: about 22% sub (below 60Hz) and 57% bass, with a centroid near 260Hz and 85% rolloff near 350Hz. The spectrum is broadband and noise-like, with no stable tonal peaks. In the first 0.6s, brighter noisy content extends up to roughly 10kHz and forms an arched sweep. The upper bands are almost empty overall, so the sound is dark and heavy, with a gritty, distorted texture.
- **Role:** Activation sound for an anti-gravity item (items.antiGravStart), played at the player. It works as a heavy power-up or engage burst, a surge of energy rather than a tonal beep.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.60 s Dense, clipped burst of low-frequency rumble with bright noisy mid and high content. A brighter arch-shaped sweep in the spectrogram peaks near 0.45s, and there are two onsets at about 0.05s and 0.23s.; 0.60–1.20 s Energy falls steadily as the rumble decays and the upper noise fades, leaving a darker, low-mid texture.; 1.20–1.80 s Very quiet residual tail with faint high-mid streaks around 2-3 kHz, fading to silence by about 1.25s in the measurements.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a one-shot of about 1.0-1.3s. Layer: (1) a sub thump or boom with a fast attack and a decay of about 0.5s, (2) filtered noise or distorted rumble with a quick downward-closing lowpass, (3) a short bright whoosh or electric swell, with an arched sweep peaking around 0.4s. Add a light saturation for grit. Keep most energy below 500Hz and let the highs fade within about 0.7s. The original is very hot, peaking above 0 dBFS with clipping, so for the replacement aim at about -12 LUFS integrated with a peak of -1 dBFS and no clipping. Trim the silent tail.

### `sounds/items/antigrav_start_b.ogg`

**A loud, bass-heavy, distorted whooshing burst that hits hard in the first ~0.3s and then decays into a fading low rumble with a faint airy tail.** *(boomy, noisy, distorted, whoosh, heavy, transient, low-end; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.81 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak 5.5 dBFS, RMS -12.1 dBFS, -11.9 LUFS, true peak 5.6 dBTP; centroid 251 Hz, flatness 0.006; 1 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** very loud. **Stereo:** Mono file, so it is centered with no left-right movement. The Qwen description of left-to-right panning is not supported by the measurements. In game it will be positioned by the engine at the player.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack: onset at about 0.05s, peak at 0.23s. Strong sustain until about 0.3s, drops 20 dB by 0.29s after the peak and 40 dB by about 0.94s. Smooth long tail fading to silence around 1.17s, with about 0.64s of trailing silence in the file.
- **Frequency profile:** Dominated by bass (about 61%) and sub (about 21%) with a centroid near 250 Hz. Mids and highs are minimal, though the spectrogram shows noisy broadband content with a curved upper-mid sweep early on. Very low flatness reading is due to low-frequency dominance, but the texture is gritty and noisy, not a clean tone.
- **Role:** Activation sound for an anti-gravity item or device (start event, played at the player): a heavy power-up or thrust burst when the effect is engaged.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.30 s Powerful impact-like onset with clipped, distorted low-frequency burst; peak around 0.23s with broadband grit extending up the spectrum.; 0.30–0.60 s Energy stays strong in sub and bass with a brighter swell in the upper mids (a curved sweep around 3-10 kHz that rises then falls), giving a whooshing quality.; 0.60–1.20 s Rumbling, pulsing low-frequency decay with fluttering texture, getting steadily quieter.; 1.20–1.17 s Very faint high-frequency air and a thin band near 2.2 kHz fading to silence by about 1.17s.
- **Recreation notes:** Build in about 1.2-1.8s total. Layer a deep sub thump or boom (40-120 Hz) with a noise whoosh that sweeps up and down in the 1-10 kHz range over about 0.6s. Add some saturation for grit and a rumbling low tail fading over about 1s. Keep the attack under 0.25s. Avoid the clipping of the original: peak at about -1 dBFS, with integrated loudness around -14 to -12 LUFS. Qwen's laser-like and 8 kHz description is not supported, since the energy is mostly low.

### `sounds/items/glider_loop.ogg`

**A continuous, low-pitched rumbling wind or air-rush texture with gentle swells, sounding like a glider moving through air.** *(noisy, whoosh, wind, rumbly, continuous, low-heavy, loop, airy; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 3.02 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -10.3 dBFS, RMS -22.9 dBFS, -24.1 LUFS, true peak -10.2 dBTP; centroid 347 Hz, flatness 0.0; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono, so it will be positioned by the game engine. This suits a held-item loop that follows the player, and it can be spatialized as a point source.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** seamless loop.
- **Envelope:** No real attack or decay: sound is present from 0.0s to 3.0s with no silence. Level wanders within about 5 dB (-20.5 to -25.9 dB per tenth), peaking around 1.22s, with a softer dip near 0.6-0.8s. Starts and ends at similar levels, which suits looping.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the bass and low-mid regions: about 13% sub, 48% bass and 33% low-mid, with a centroid near 350 Hz and 85% rolloff near 630 Hz. Very little above 2 kHz (presence 0.4%, air 0.1%). The spectrogram shows a dense, grainy, fluttering texture with the brightest energy below 200 Hz and a ragged ceiling around 1 kHz. The texture is noise-like, with no steady harmonic lines. The spectral flatness reading of 0 is likely a measurement artifact given the broadband texture. The single peak at 18 kHz is probably a codec artifact.
- **Role:** Looping sound for using or holding a glider item, representing air rushing past during flight. The Qwen notes mention a car engine, which the measurements do not support; the evidence points to wind-like rumble instead.
- **Elements:** 0.00–3.02 s Steady turbulent air-rush bed with fluctuating low-frequency rumble; slight swells near 0.2s, 0.5s and 1.2s and a dip around 0.6-0.8s, with no distinct transients.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a roughly 3 s seamless loop from filtered brown or pink noise, low-passed around 600-1000 Hz, with a strong low shelf under 200 Hz. Add slow, irregular amplitude modulation of about 3-5 dB at 1-3 Hz for a gusting feel, plus subtle fluttery turbulence. Crossfade the ends so the loop is seamless. Target about -23 dBFS RMS (-24 LUFS) with peaks near -10 dBFS. Keep the high end soft so it does not become fatiguing when it plays continuously.

### `sounds/items/glider_loop_stereo.ogg`

**A continuous, low-to-mid-heavy rushing wind-like noise bed that swells gently toward the middle and fades back at the edges, like air streaming past a glider in flight.** *(noisy, whoosh, wind, rumbly, sustained, loop, broadband, soft; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 9.41 s, 48000 Hz, 2 ch; peak -5.9 dBFS, RMS -23.8 dBFS, -19.2 LUFS, true peak -5.8 dBTP; centroid 1148 Hz, flatness 0.004; 2 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Moderately wide with partial decorrelation (L/R correlation 0.43, side-to-mid -4 dB). Gives an enveloping airflow around the player, but the sound should still play from the player and not be positional; check mono compatibility, since the decorrelated noise may thin out when summed.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** loop with audible seam.
- **Envelope:** No attack or decay: the sound is active the whole 9.4 s with no silence. It starts at a moderate level, dips slightly around 1 s, swells to the loudest around 4-7 s (about -21 dB per-tenth envelope against -30 dB lowest), then eases down to about -29 dB at the end. Loudness range is 4 LU. The edges are quieter and darker, which suggests designed loop edges, but a seam is not confirmed.
- **Frequency profile:** Very noise-like (flatness 0.004 is low by the measure, but the spectrogram shows a dense, textured broadband spread with no steady tonal lines or listed peaks). Energy is mostly bass (31%) and low-mid (39%), with mid at 21%, and little presence (2%) or air (4%). Centroid is about 1.1 kHz, rolloff 85% at 1.3 kHz. Vertical streaky texture below 500 Hz gives a fluttering, buffeting rumble; the mids brighten in the center. Little above 5 kHz.
- **Role:** Looping airflow/wind sound while the player uses a glider item (event glider.loop, player category), giving a sense of speed and gliding. The Qwen description of a vehicle on a wet road with water splashing is likely a misreading of wind noise; the spectrogram shows broadband airflow with no splash transients.
- **Elements:** 0.00–3.00 s Steady low rushing noise, centroid around 500 Hz, level slightly dipping near 0.9 s then rising.; 3.00–7.20 s Swell to the loudest, brightest section; energy extends higher around 1 kHz and the mid-high haze brightens, centroid about 1.1 kHz, peak near the middle of the clip.; 7.20–9.41 s Gradual relaxation back to darker low-frequency rush, with a few faint tick-like flecks near 8 s; level lower at the end.
- **Recreation notes:** Build from filtered pink or brown noise with a low-pass around 1.5-2 kHz and a gentle high-pass near 40 Hz. Add slow amplitude modulation (random flutter, roughly 5-15 Hz at low depth) for the buffeting, plus a faint band of mid-high air hiss around 1-4 kHz that swells in the middle. Use decorrelated noise for L/R. Make it a loop of about 8-10 s with a slow 3-4 dB swell; crossfade the ends so the loop is seamless and no tonal whistles stand out. Target about -19 LUFS integrated, peaks around -6 dBFS, so it sits under music and other effects as a constant bed.

The spectrogram looks like turbulent noise, not a pitched hum, so the model's 75 Hz pitch claim is unreliable.

Add a faint airy hiss above 2 kHz at low level. Do not add tonal or pitched elements, and avoid prominent transients. Target about -18 dBFS RMS, around -20 LUFS integrated, with peaks near -5 dBFS. - **Role:** Looping jetpack thrust or engine sound played while the player is using the jetpack (event jetpack.loop, player category). The '_b' name suggests one of several variants picked at random.
- **Elements:** 0.00–1.10 s Continuous low rumbling thrust with rapid amplitude flutter and grainy texture. Energy is mostly below 500 Hz with a fuzzy, mottled haze of mids up to about 2 kHz. No distinct hits or pitch changes.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer a filtered brown or pink noise (low-passed around 400-600 Hz) with a low rumbling oscillator or engine buzz at about 70-100 Hz. Add fast random amplitude modulation (roughly 20-60 Hz flutter) for turbulence, and a quiet airy hiss layer in the 1-3 kHz range for flame or air. Make it about 1.1 s long with crossfaded loop points and no fades at the ends. Aim for about -20 LUFS integrated, with peaks around -5 dBFS and a crest factor of about 13 dB. Make 2-3 variants with slightly different modulation. The Qwen notes (a motor hum at 70-80 Hz plus static) agree with the low-frequency emphasis, but their 10-second duration is wrong, since the file is 1.1 s.

### `sounds/items/jetpack_loop_c.ogg`

**A continuous low, rumbling, rough thruster-like roar of band-limited noise with a bass-heavy body, like a jetpack engine burn, with slight amplitude flutter throughout.** *(rumbling, noisy, boomy, low-end, continuous, mechanical, turbulent, dark; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.10 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -5.9 dBFS, RMS -17.8 dBFS, -19.4 LUFS, true peak -5.9 dBTP; centroid 233 Hz, flatness 0.0; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono, so it is centered and positioned by the game engine. This suits a player-attached looping sound that pans and attenuates in 3D by distance.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** loop with audible seam.
- **Envelope:** Attack of about 0.1 s up to the peak, then a sustained level that wobbles within roughly 3-8 dB by tenth of a second (-13 to -24 dB). It is active the whole time with no silence, and it ends 6-8 dB lower than the peak with no decay tail, so loop-style playback.
- **Frequency profile:** Dominated by bass: about 56% in the bass band, 21% sub and 16% low-mid, with centroid around 230 Hz and 85% rolloff near 305 Hz. Very little above 1 kHz, and the spectrogram shows vertical streaky low-frequency energy fading into a dull red haze up to about 10 kHz. There is a hard cutoff near 11 kHz, typical of codec lowpass. Flatness reads 0, but the texture is rough and noisy rather than a pure tone. Listening notes describe a chirp or sine and a communication signal, which conflicts with the measurements: no steady tonal lines or pitch sweep are visible, so I trust the rumble reading.
- **Role:** Looping engine/thrust sound while the player uses a jetpack, variant C of several loop alternates, played in the player category.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.10 s Quick swell into the loop, with a faint click-like broadband mark at the very start.; 0.10–0.85 s Steady turbulent rumble with irregular amplitude flutter, a rough burbling texture and most energy under 500 Hz.; 0.85–1.10 s Level dips by a few dB toward the end, same texture continuing with no tail.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer a low filtered noise rumble (lowpass around 400-500 Hz, with a sub body at 40-120 Hz) with a gentle turbulent amplitude modulation of roughly 10-30 Hz for flutter. Add a small touch of mid hiss below 2 kHz for the flame. Avoid obvious tonal pitches. Make it about 1.1 s long with a crossfaded start and end for a clean loop, with matched levels at both ends (the original dips about 6 dB at the end). Target around -19 LUFS integrated, peaks near -6 dBFS, and keep it mono. Make several slightly different variants so the repeats are not obvious.

### `sounds/items/jetpack_start_a.ogg`

**A loud, rumbling burst of low-frequency noise, like a gas or rocket thruster igniting, that stays strong for about 0.7 s and then fades away over roughly 0.5 s.** *(noisy, rumbly, whoosh, boomy, thruster, turbulent, low-end heavy; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.29 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -0.2 dBFS, RMS -15.3 dBFS, -15.5 LUFS, true peak -0.2 dBTP; centroid 850 Hz, flatness 0.001; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono file. It will be positioned as a point source at the player, so it stays centered and spatializes simply; any width should come from the game's panning rather than the file.. **Pitch:** falling. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack of about 0.04 s to the peak. Sustained at a roughly constant level until about 0.7 s, then a gentle decay: -20 dB by about 1.09 s and -40 dB by about 1.23 s. Almost no silent tail, ending at about 1.29 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Dominated by bass (50% of energy, 100-300 Hz) with a strong low-mid body (25%) and little sub energy (5%). The centroid is about 850 Hz and falls from about 1.2 kHz in the first quarter to about 300 Hz at the end, so the sound darkens over time. Flatness is very low in the measurements, but the spectrogram shows dense noise-like speckle with vertical gust streaks, so it reads as rough, turbulent noise rather than a clean tone. There is a thin faint line near 5-6 kHz and a small peak near 9.8 kHz, which are minor details.
- **Role:** Jetpack start or ignition sound when the player activates the item; it works as a thruster spool-up or burst that leads into a separate looping flight sound. The Qwen notes of an explosion or fireworks are plausible for the boomy noise, but the sustained, gradually fading shape suggests a thruster ignition rather than a blast.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Sharp ignition onset with a brief bright burst reaching up to the highs, plus a faint high-frequency ping near 9-10 kHz.; 0.05–0.70 s Sustained turbulent roar of broadband noise, strongest below 500 Hz, with a flickering, gusty texture and slight amplitude fluctuation.; 0.70–1.27 s Gradual fade as the highs and mids drop out, leaving a dull low rumble that dies away.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer filtered white or pink noise (bandpass about 100-400 Hz, with a lowpass sweep falling from about 3 kHz to 500 Hz) with a short bright transient or burst of air at the start (about 50 ms) for ignition. Add a low rumble layer around 60-150 Hz and optional subtle flutter or gust modulation. Hold the level for about 0.7 s, then fade to silence by about 1.3 s total. Target a peak near -1 dBFS, RMS about -15 dBFS, and about -15 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono. Leave the tail clean so it hands off to the jetpack loop.

### `sounds/items/jetpack_start_b.ogg`

**A dense, rumbling burst of low-frequency noise that swells in almost instantly, holds as a roaring, crackling blast for about a second, then fades out like a jetpack igniting.** *(noisy, rumbling, boomy, roar, mechanical, dense, turbulent, whoosh; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.29 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak 0.9 dBFS, RMS -14.7 dBFS, -15.0 LUFS, true peak 0.9 dBTP; centroid 699 Hz, flatness 0.0; 1 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** very loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it is centered and easy to position in 3D. In game it follows the player and should be spatialised by the engine. The player category suggests it plays near the listener.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Very fast attack of about 0.03 s to the peak, then a held, slightly fluctuating plateau until about 0.5 s. A slow decay follows, reaching -20 dB at about 1.11 s, with a short tail ending at about 1.25 s (-40 dB). Almost no silence; 96% of the file is active.
- **Frequency profile:** Dominated by bass (about 57% in the bass band, 5% sub, 23% low-mid), with the centroid near 700 Hz and 85% rolloff under 1 kHz. The spectrogram shows a bright, vertically streaky low band below about 500 Hz, with a mottled, sparse texture above it that fades toward the top. Faint narrow lines near 11-16 kHz are likely artifacts or minor whine, and the air band is negligible. The texture is noisy, though the flatness metric reads 0.0, which is probably a measurement quirk. The energy is not tonal and shows no clear harmonics.
- **Role:** Jetpack start or ignition sound, a variant 'b' for the jetpack.start event played on the player, representing the thrusters firing up. The Qwen description of an explosion or eruption fits the dense burst, but the clip is not followed by silence. Measurements show a sustained roar that tails off.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Sharp ignition thump with a brief click-like transient peak, brightest part of the sound (centroid ~870 Hz).; 0.05–0.55 s Full-intensity roaring burst of bass-heavy turbulent noise, fluttering with fast amplitude modulation, around -10 to -14 dB per-tenth level.; 0.55–1.10 s Sustained rumble that gradually weakens and darkens, with energy shrinking toward the low end (centroid ~350-470 Hz).; 1.10–1.29 s Quick fade of the tail to silence, with the level dropping more than 25 dB in the final tenth.
- **Recreation notes:** Build it from three layers. First, a short ignition thump or whoomph of 50-100 ms, with a low-mid click for the transient. Second, a pink or brown noise roar, low-passed around 1 kHz with fast tremolo or turbulence modulation, held for about 0.5 s then slowly fading. Third, an optional faint mechanical rattle or gas hiss in the mids. Total length about 1.3 s. Fade out over the final 0.2 s with no hard cutoff. Aim for around -15 LUFS and a peak at or below -1 dBFS; the original clips slightly, which should be avoided. Keep it mono, with little content above 5 kHz.

### `sounds/items/jetpack_start_c.ogg`

**A short, gritty, low-heavy burst of rushing noise with a sharp thump at the start, sustaining as a rough rumbling roar before fading out over about 1.3 seconds.** *(noisy, rumbling, gritty, whoosh, mechanical, boomy, transient; confidence medium)*

A fairly level sustain at roughly -12 to -15 dB until about 0.6 s, followed by a stepped fade: -22 dB around 0.6 s, -26 dB around 0.8 s, -30 dB around 0.9 s. It is about 20 dB down at 1.06 s and 40 dB down at 1.2 s, with a very short tail and 0.02 s of trailing silence.

The spectrogram shows a dense, flickering texture from 20 Hz to 500 Hz, speckled mids, and a brief bright burst around 3-6 kHz at the start. There is very little air. The texture is noisy and turbulent. The flatness reading of 0 is probably a measurement artifact, since there are no steady tonal lines or harmonics.
- **Role:** Jetpack start or ignition sound (variant c) for the player: a thruster igniting and spooling into a burning roar. The Qwen notes mention an explosion followed by a revving engine, which fits the burst-plus-roar structure. The grunting or snoring detail is likely a misreading of the low rumble.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Quiet lead-in of low rumble, around -29 dB, before the main hit.; 0.05–0.15 s Sharp ignition thump or burst with a broadband transient. It peaks at 0 dBFS at about 0.07 s, with extra brightness up to about 5 kHz.; 0.15–0.75 s Sustained rough, flickering low-frequency roar (thruster or engine flame), strongest between 50 and 500 Hz, with a granular, pulsing texture.; 0.75–1.27 s Gradual decay of the roar, thinning and getting slightly brighter in the highs, then fading to silence by about 1.27 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Make it about 1.2-1.3 s long. Layer (1) a short ignition thump or whump with a noise click at 0.05 s, (2) a low-passed, filtered noise roar with an amplitude flutter of about 20-40 Hz, concentrated at 60-500 Hz and rolling off above 1 kHz, and (3) an optional light gas-hiss at 3-6 kHz on the attack only. Keep the sustain level and fade out over the last 0.4 s. Target about -16 LUFS integrated, with peaks around -1 dBFS and no clipping. Keep it mono.

### `sounds/items/jetpack_stop_a.ogg`

The texture is broadband noise with a rough, gritty low end that gets darker as it decays. There is a faint, thin steady tone near 6.2 kHz, which is probably an artifact or whine and is barely audible. The spectrogram shows some mid-range brightness (1-4 kHz) in the first 0.3 s.
- **Role:** The stop or shutoff sound when the player turns off the jetpack: thrust cutting out and the engine spooling down.

Layer (1) a low filtered noise or rumble with a 20-200 Hz body, swelling over about 0.3 s and fading out by about 1 s; (2) a mid-band noise or gas-hiss burst at the start, filtered around 1-3 kHz and dropping off quickly; (3) optional sputter or flutter modulation, with amplitude chopping at roughly 20-30 Hz in the middle section, to suggest the engine dying. Do not add a clear pitched tone. Aim for about -22 LUFS integrated and a peak around -6 dBFS, leaving a short silent tail. The Qwen notes (whoosh then bang, 10 s long) conflict with the measured 1.4 s duration and absence of onsets, so they were disregarded.

Very little air content.

### `sounds/items/jetpack_stop_c.ogg`

A faint thin tonal line near 6.2 kHz drifts slightly downward, possibly a whine or artifact. Brightness is greatest early and fades with the tail.

Give it a quick swell over about 0.3s, then a smooth exponential decay with some amplitude flutter or sputter as it dies. Optionally add a subtle falling filter sweep. Keep it mono, with a peak near -6 dBFS and an integrated loudness of about -23 LUFS. Avoid a hard transient and fade to complete silence.

### `sounds/items/nightvision_off.ogg`

There is almost no sub or bass content. The texture is part noisy and part tonal (flatness 0.33). The tonal ending is a thin downward whine plus very high ultrasonic-adjacent lines near 16-20 kHz.

A faint diagonal streak near 6-9 kHz hints at a short downward sweep.; 0.14–0.28 s Quiet decaying noisy tail, mostly high-frequency hiss, with weak low-frequency pulsing.; 0.28–0.42 s Soft tonal whine that slides downward from about 5 kHz to 3 kHz, with a few faint amplitude swells (blips) at about 0.29, 0.33 and 0.37 s. A thin high shimmer sits above 15 kHz.

Layer 2 is a soft electronic sine or whine sliding from about 5 kHz to 3 kHz over 0.15 s, with 2-3 tiny blips at 0.29-0.37 s. Optionally add faint air shimmer above 12 kHz. Avoid low end. ### `sounds/items/nightvision_on.ogg`

**A bright, noisy electronic power-up burst made of two quick hissy/crackly hits, followed by a thin, high ringing tone that sinks slightly and fades into a shimmering tail.** *(electronic, bright, noisy, transient, metallic, shimmer, ringing, power-up; confidence medium)*

- **Envelope:** Instant attack (peak at ~0.03 s), first burst lasts ~0.09 s, then a second burst at ~0.1-0.15 s. Drops about 20 dB by 0.34 s, then a quiet ringing sustain decays to -40 dB by ~1.1 s. Tail is near silent from ~1.14 s, with about 0.46 s of trailing silence.

Flatness of 0.3 is a mix of noise and tone. The ringing partials sit at roughly 1-5 kHz, with sparkle peaks near 11 kHz and 15-18 kHz. Very little true bass apart from a faint low rumble in the spectrogram.

The Qwen notes describe a gunshot, which does not match the evidence of a ringing, shimmering electronic tail. The loud noisy onset is the only overlap, so the notes were treated with low confidence.

- **Recreation notes:** Build about 1.2-1.6 s total. Layer (1) a short 80-100 ms filtered noise or click burst with a quick double-hit, (2) a soft electronic whirr or relay thunk for body, (3) 2-3 thin metallic sine/FM partials at 2-5 kHz with a slight downward then upward glide, ringing for ~0.7 s, and (4) a faint high airy shimmer above 8 kHz that fades out. Target about -18 LUFS integrated with peaks below -1 dBFS (the original clips and peaks at about +0.8 dBFS, so avoid that). Keep the sound mono.

### `sounds/blocks/metaldoor_open_a.ogg`

**A short, clunky metal door opening: a latch click followed by a rattling, creaking metallic scrape with heavy low-end thumps that fades out in under a second.** *(metallic, mechanical, noisy, creak, rattle, transient, boomy; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.96 s, 32000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -0.1 dBFS, RMS -20.4 dBFS, -17.9 LUFS, true peak 0.1 dBTP; centroid 2753 Hz, flatness 0.236; 3 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It is easy to position in 3D at the door block, and no stereo width is needed.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Very quiet click at about 0.05 s, then a fast build to the peak at about 0.23 s. It stays fairly sustained and rattly until about 0.5 s, then decays: -20 dB by about 0.4 s and -40 dB by about 0.6 s, with a faint tail to about 0.85 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Broadband, with most energy in the low-mid and mid range (200 Hz to 3 kHz). A strong boomy low end (40-150 Hz) pulses through the middle section. Centroid is about 2.7 kHz overall and falls from about 2 kHz to 640 Hz, so the sound darkens as it goes. Flatness of 0.24 means partly noisy and partly tonal. Faint tonal lines near 800 Hz and 1.6 kHz suggest hinge squeak or metal resonance.
- **Role:** Metal (iron) door opening sound, played when the player or redstone opens it. The Qwen description of gunfire or an explosion is not supported by the measurements. The multiple clanky onsets and decaying scrape fit a door.
- **Elements:** 0.04–0.10 s Small latch or click, quiet, with a faint tonal ping near 1.7 kHz.; 0.10–0.30 s Main metallic clunk and handle release, with bursts of low thumps and broadband clatter at about 0.14 s and 0.23 s.; 0.30–0.58 s Rattling, grinding metal movement with pulsing low-frequency bumps and a faint tonal line near 1.6 kHz.; 0.58–0.85 s Thinning creak or hinge squeal with a weak tonal line around 800 Hz, then decaying scrape.; 0.85–0.96 s Near-silent tail.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer three parts. First, a soft latch click at 0 to 0.05 s. Second, a heavy metal clunk with a low thump (60-150 Hz) and a clatter burst at about 0.1 to 0.25 s. Third, a short hinge creak or scrape with a slight 800 Hz to 1.6 kHz resonance fading by 0.8 s. Total length is about 0.9 to 1.0 s. Peak near -0.5 dBFS, about -18 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono, and make 2 to 3 variations with slight pitch changes.

### `sounds/blocks/metaldoor_open_b.ogg`

**A short, clanky metal door opening: a few quick latch and hinge rattles followed by a heavier low-mid thud-scrape around 0.25 s and a fading creak-like tail.** *(metallic, clanky, transient, mechanical, noisy, low-mid heavy, impact; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.96 s, 32000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -0.3 dBFS, RMS -23.7 dBFS, -20.3 LUFS, true peak -0.3 dBTP; centroid 2094 Hz, flatness 0.149; 3 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono, so it is easy to position in 3D at the door block and it stays consistent with distance attenuation.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Near-instant onset at about 0.05 s with a quiet first rattle, building to the peak at about 0.25 s (the attack to the peak is about 0.24 s). Then it decays, reaching -20 dB by about 0.25 s after the peak and -40 dB by about 0.49 s. Faint tail to about 0.75 s, then about 0.2 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** The energy is mostly mid and low-mid (about 38% mid, 29% low-mid, 13% bass), with almost no sub. The centroid is around 2 kHz early and falls to about 700 Hz in the tail. Spectral flatness of 0.15 suggests a semi-tonal, metallic resonance mixed with noise. Bursts reach the top of the spectrum at the onsets, and there are peaks near 4.2 kHz and 7.5 kHz. Dotted, banded structure at 200-1000 Hz indicates resonant metallic ringing.
- **Role:** Opening sound for a metal (iron) door block, triggered when the player interacts with it. The event name supports this and the evidence is consistent with it.
- **Elements:** 0.05–0.12 s Initial latch or handle rattle, a short broadband burst with bright content up to the highs.; 0.13–0.22 s Second, smaller metallic knock with some energy near 3-4 kHz, still rattly.; 0.24–0.42 s Main event: heavy hinge/door swing impact with the loudest low-mid body (150-800 Hz) and a bright click burst near 0.4 s.; 0.42–0.75 s Decaying rumble and scrape of the door settling, low-mid dominated and thinning out.; 0.75–0.96 s Very faint residual tail fading to silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Make it about 0.9-1.0 s long, mono, in three layers. First, a short latch click or rattle at 0 s plus a second smaller knock about 80 ms later. Second, a heavier hinge or door-body thunk with a 150-800 Hz metallic resonance at about 0.2-0.25 s, with a short creak or scrape. Third, a quick decaying low rumble of 0.3 s. Keep a gentle high-frequency sizzle on the transients only. Aim for a peak around -1 dBFS, about -20 LUFS integrated, and leave a short silent tail. Make 2-3 pitch variants so repeated opens do not sound identical.

### `sounds/blocks/metaldoor_open_c.ogg`

**A short, creaky metal door-opening sound: a heavy latch-like thunk, a rattling metallic scrape, then a second clunk and a fading rattly tail.** *(metallic, mechanical, impact, creak, noisy, low-mid heavy, rattle, transient; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.83 s, 32000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -2.1 dBFS, RMS -23.4 dBFS, -22.2 LUFS, true peak -2.1 dBTP; centroid 1646 Hz, flatness 0.123; 2 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono, so it can be positioned in 3D at the door block with natural distance attenuation and panning, and no stereo width is needed.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack of about 0.07 s after 0.02 s of lead-in, peaking near 0.09 s. Held at a fairly steady moderate level through about 0.6 s with a second bump at 0.26 s, then falls 20 dB by 0.42 s and 40 dB by 0.64 s. Short tail with about 0.1 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Most energy sits in the low-mid (about 44%) and mid (about 38%) bands, with some bass (about 9%) and little sub. The centroid is about 1.6 kHz and the 85% rolloff about 2.75 kHz. Moderately noisy (flatness 0.12) with a woody-metallic, hollow resonant texture. A brief bright spike in the third quarter comes from the second clunk. A faint narrow high-frequency line sits near 8-9 kHz.
- **Role:** Metal (iron) door opening sound for a block interaction, one of several variants (the 'c' variant) chosen at random.
- **Elements:** 0.05–0.20 s Initial heavy latch/handle thunk with a broadband burst and strong low-mid body (about 200-500 Hz), plus a sharp transient peak near 0.09 s.; 0.20–0.26 s Short rattly metal scrape or friction as the door begins to swing, lower in level.; 0.26–0.35 s Second impact, a clunk or hinge knock, brighter than the first with energy extending up to several kHz.; 0.35–0.65 s Decaying rattle and creak of the hinge and door body, with a thin high tonal whistle-like trace around 8-9 kHz near 0.4-0.55 s.; 0.65–0.83 s Very quiet fading tail that dies into silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer a short heavy latch click or thunk (about 0.1 s) with a metallic hinge creak or scrape (about 0.3 s) and a second, softer clunk at roughly 0.25 s after the first. Add a quiet rattly decay. Keep the total at about 0.8 s with a low-mid-weighted body (200-800 Hz) and a little metallic brightness. Target a peak near -2 dBFS and about -22 LUFS integrated. Make a few variations in pitch and timing so repeated opens do not sound identical.

### `sounds/machines/ammopress_work1_a.ogg`

**A heavy mechanical thunk or impact at about 0.25s that rolls into a rumbling, grinding industrial churn with metallic ringing, then fades out by about 2.2s.** *(mechanical, industrial, impact, rumbling, metallic, noisy, boomy, heavy; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.57 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 3.6 dBFS, RMS -18.3 dBFS, -15.5 LUFS, true peak 3.7 dBTP; centroid 1465 Hz, flatness 0.042; 2 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it will be positioned by the game engine. It suits a block-attached machine sound that attenuates with distance and pans in 3D.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Near-silent start for about 0.2s, then an abrupt, clipped attack at about 0.25s (peak at 0.26s). Fast decay to -20 dB by about 0.6s, then a long low-level sustain of roughly 1s with a small bump near 1.3-1.6s. Fades to -40 dB by about 1.6s and a tail that vanishes around 2.2s. About 0.68s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is dominated by sub, bass and low-mid (20-400 Hz) rumble, with a strong mid band (about 40% of energy). The centroid is about 1.5 kHz at the impact and drops to about 430 Hz in the middle. The initial hit is broadband up to about 10 kHz with a bright, metallic ring near 2.1 kHz and 2.9 kHz. The sustain is noisy and rough, with faint horizontal mid bands from machine resonance. Very little air above 8 kHz.
- **Role:** Working sound for the ammo press machine block: a press stamp or clank followed by the machine's mechanical churn. It is one variant (_a) of a work cycle sound that plays while the machine operates.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.20 s Very quiet lead-in with faint low noise and a tiny early tick near 0.07s.; 0.25–0.60 s Sharp, loud machine clank or press impact with a broadband burst and strong low-end thump. Bright metallic ring around 2 kHz and a few small secondary ticks.; 0.60–1.70 s Sustained low grinding or rumbling machinery churn, with a mild swell around 1.3-1.6s and faint mid-frequency metallic resonances.; 1.70–2.20 s Machinery noise fades away, with the low rumble dying out first.
- **Recreation notes:** Make it about 2-2.5s. Layer (1) a heavy metal press or clank hit with a punchy low thump and a short metallic ring around 2-3 kHz, (2) a low rumbling motor or gear grind bed of 100-250 Hz with subtle modulation, lasting about 1.2s with a slight swell, and (3) a few small ticks or servo rattles. Use a short fade-out tail. Keep the peak below 0 dBFS, since the original clips and peaks at about +3.6 dB. Aim for about -16 LUFS integrated, and avoid harsh clipping.

### `sounds/machines/ammopress_work1_b.ogg`

**A heavy mechanical press-style thump with a bright noisy crunch impact at about 0.25s, followed by a rumbling low-frequency machine churn that slowly fades over roughly 2.5 seconds.** *(mechanical, transient, boomy, noisy, rumble, industrial, heavy, clipped; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.77 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 3.9 dBFS, RMS -17.4 dBFS, -11.7 LUFS, true peak 3.9 dBTP; centroid 1590 Hz, flatness 0.03; 4 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** very loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It sits centered and is easy to position in 3D as a block-emitted machine sound, with distance attenuation handled by the game engine.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Quiet lead-in of about 0.1-0.17s, then a sharp attack reaching its peak around 0.25-0.33s. The level falls about 20 dB within roughly 0.4s of the peak, then sits at a low-level sustained rumble from about 0.6s to 2.0s. It reaches -40 dB near 2.0s and fades out by about 2.4s, with a short silent tail.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is strongest in the sub and bass region below 250 Hz, which forms a continuous boomy bed. The initial hit adds dense broadband noise through the mids and highs up to about 4-8 kHz. The centroid starts near 1.6 kHz and falls to about 300 Hz as the sound darkens. Spectral flatness is low at 0.03, mostly because of the strong low-frequency content, though the texture itself is noisy and gritty. Few distinct tones, only weak peaks near 4.4 kHz and 8.6 kHz.
- **Role:** Work or operation sound of an ammo press machine block: a heavy press stamp or crunch followed by machinery rumble. The listening notes mention an engine start, a bang and beeps, but the beeping is not supported by the spectrogram, so treat that as unreliable.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.17 s Near-silent lead-in with a faint low swell and a few soft low thumps building into the hit.; 0.17–0.60 s Main impact: abrupt broadband crunch/clank at about 0.25s with a strong sub and bass thud, bright noise burst reaching up to about 8 kHz and decaying quickly.; 0.60–2.40 s Low rumbling machine churn or grinding with pulsing energy under about 250 Hz and faint mid-range mechanical texture. A small bump near 1.7s may be a secondary clunk.; 2.40–2.77 s Tail fading to silence, with only faint low rumble remaining.
- **Recreation notes:** Make an original sound about 2.5-2.8s long, in three layers. (1) A hydraulic or metal press slam with a sub thud at 40-100 Hz plus a short noise or metal clank, with a fast attack at about 0.2s. (2) A low motor or grind rumble under 250 Hz that slowly decays over about 2s. (3) Optional light mechanical clicks in the mids. Keep it mono. The original peaks above 0 dBFS with clipping and an integrated level around -11.7 LUFS, so aim lower, with peaks around -1 dBFS and about -14 LUFS, and avoid clipping.

### `sounds/machines/ammopress_work1_c.ogg`

**A rumbling low machine hum with a sudden loud metallic crunch/clank around 0.26s that decays into a rattling, grinding mechanical tail with a small clunk near 1.5s.** *(mechanical, metallic, rumble, noisy, transient, industrial, clanky, low-heavy; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.06 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 3.7 dBFS, RMS -15.7 dBFS, -12.2 LUFS, true peak 3.7 dBTP; centroid 1746 Hz, flatness 0.069; 6 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it will be positioned by the game as a point source at the machine; fine for spatialised block sounds.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Quiet rumble from 0s, abrupt attack at ~0.25s peaking at 0.26s, fast drop of ~20 dB by ~0.6s, then a low-level sustained bed around -20 to -25 dB until ~1.8s, with small clunks near 1.5-1.9s, and fade to silence by ~2.03s; -40 dB reached at ~1.66s from peak.
- **Frequency profile:** Strong sub/bass energy below ~150 Hz persists throughout as a rumble; the impact adds broadband energy up to ~5 kHz with bright mid content (1-4 kHz, centroid ~1.7 kHz overall, falling to ~500 Hz and ~70 Hz in later quarters). Little energy above 8 kHz. Mostly noisy and gritty with some faint resonant bands near 1 and 2 kHz giving a metallic tone.
- **Role:** One variant of the ammo press machine's working cycle sound, a block sound played while the machine operates, with the clank as the press stamping down.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.25 s Low machine rumble/hum bed, fairly quiet, with some mid grit.; 0.25–0.60 s Loud sudden impact: metallic press clank/crunch with broadband noise burst and bright upper-mid ringing, peak at 0.26s (clipped).; 0.60–1.45 s Sustained low rumble with rattling mechanical texture and faint mid-frequency resonances around 1-2 kHz, gradually fading.; 1.45–2.00 s A few small clunks/ticks (onsets near 1.49, 1.71, 1.84, 1.93s) over a dying rumble, ending in a fade.
- **Recreation notes:** Make about 2 s: layer a low machine rumble/motor hum (40-150 Hz) as bed, a sharp metal press-stamp impact around 0.25s (noise burst plus metallic clank with 1-4 kHz ring, ~0.3s decay), and gritty rattle/small servo clunks in the tail with a fade. Keep the peak below 0 dBFS (original clips) and target roughly -12 LUFS integrated, though a bit lower is safer. Keep mono.

### `sounds/machines/ammopress_work2_a.ogg`

**A short, low-mid heavy mechanical press-and-clunk burst with a gritty rumble that peaks around 0.2 s and fades into a thin tail by about 0.85 s.** *(mechanical, thuddy, noisy, low-mid, impact, gritty, machine, decaying; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.06 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -3.0 dBFS, RMS -21.5 dBFS, -19.8 LUFS, true peak -2.9 dBTP; centroid 867 Hz, flatness 0.005; 2 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. This suits a positional block sound in game, since it can be attenuated and panned by the engine based on the machine's location.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast but not instant build: about 0.2 s to peak with a small precursor at 0.05 s. Quick drop of about 0.34 s to -20 dB, then a gradual rattling decay to -40 dB at about 0.62 s. The tail fades out by roughly 0.85 s, with about 0.23 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the bass and low-mid range (about 70% between roughly 100 and 900 Hz). The centroid is around 870 Hz and the 85% rolloff is near 1.8 kHz. There is a tonal band near 450 Hz early on, and the mid range has a gritty, noisy texture. Sub and air content is minimal. The 12.8 kHz peak is likely an encoding artifact. The brightest moment is around 0.2-0.3 s, and the sound darkens afterwards (centroid falls to about 230 Hz by the final quarter).
- **Role:** One variant of the ammo press's work or operation sound, played when the machine does a pressing/stamping cycle. The event name suggests a block machine sound that is randomly varied (the 'a' variant of work2). The Qwen notes mention a metallic slam and explosion-like rumble. This is partly consistent with the measured impact and low rumble, but the claimed pitch rise and loud ending are not supported by the measurements.
