- **Recreation notes:** Make about 1.0 s total. Layer (1) a low thud or piston hit at about 0.2 s with body around 100-300 Hz, (2) a short metallic clank or clunk with a tonal element near 450 Hz and some 1-3 kHz grit, and (3) a rattly, pulsing motor or grinding noise bed that fades over about 0.5 s. - **Role:** Working or processing loop of a fabricator machine block, played while the machine is active. The Qwen notes of beeps and an error message do not match the spectrogram, which shows a continuous drone, so they are largely discounted.

- **Recreation notes:** Make a ~5.5 s mono clip. Layer (1) a deep sub/bass rumble or motor hum below 200 Hz, (2) a mid tone around 500 Hz with a faint octave partial near 1 kHz and slight amplitude flutter, and (3) grainy mechanical noise bursts, clicks and servo-like chirps in the 1-8 kHz range that become denser mid-clip. Fade the ends quickly so it can be retriggered or crossfaded in a loop. Aim for about -13 LUFS and keep peaks under 0 dBFS, since the original clips slightly. Lower the level if the machine is meant to sit in the background.

### `sounds/machines/metalpress_small_work_a.ogg`

**A loud, heavy industrial machine sound: a hard metallic stamp-like impact with a bright clangy burst and strong low thump that settles into a droning, rumbling mechanical hum fading over about two seconds.** *(mechanical, industrial, metallic, impact, rumbling, droning, dense, boomy; confidence medium)*

- **Envelope:** Very fast attack (peak at 0.13s, first onset 0.05s), no real sustain; steady exponential-like decay with -20 dB at about 1.22s and -40 dB at about 2.09s, then a short fade to silence by 2.25s. Envelope by tenth drops from about -7.6 dB to about -23 dB by 0.7 of the length, then falls off.
- **Frequency profile:** Broadband but weighted low to mid: about 20% bass, 29% low-mid, 38% mid, with a little sub (4%) and presence (7.5%). Centroid around 1.5 kHz, falling from about 1.57 kHz in the first quarter to about 630-770 Hz afterwards. Prominent peaks at 2.1, 2.7 and 3.4 kHz give the metallic ring; a persistent band near 450 Hz gives the machine drone. Energy rolls off above about 3 kHz (85% rolloff 2.9 kHz). Spectral flatness reads 0.0, which is unreliable given the dense, noisy look of the spectrogram, so treat it as a textured tonal-noise mix.
- **Role:** The working or processing sound of a small metal press machine block, played as a single cycle each time it performs a pressing operation. The path and event name support this reading. The Qwen description of gunshots, engine and explosion is not credible and is most likely a misreading of the heavy impact and rumble.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.40 s Hard press impact: sharp onset around 0.05s peaking at 0.13s, broadband burst with strong sub/bass thump and bright metallic partials (roughly 2-3.4 kHz and up to ~10 kHz) that shine in the first 0.3s.; 0.40–1.40 s Machine body and resonance: a steady mid-low drone/hum around 400-500 Hz with a weaker band near 900 Hz, plus rumbling low-end and gritty noise texture, decaying gradually.; 1.40–2.25 s Tail: quieter rumble and hum fading out, upper content gone, ending near silence at about 2.2s.
- **Recreation notes:** Build in layers: (1) a heavy, short press or thud with a sub/bass punch, about 100-200 ms; (2) a metallic clank or stamp with inharmonic partials around 2-3.5 kHz, decaying within about 0.4s; (3) a motor or hydraulic drone around 400-500 Hz with a little grit and rumble, sustained and fading over about 1.5-2s. Total length about 2.2s. The original is hot, peaking over 0 dBFS (about +4.6 dBTP, with clipping) at about -14 LUFS; aim lower and clean, around -16 to -14 LUFS with peaks at or below -1 dBFS. Keep it mono. Make a few variants, since the file name implies 'a' of several.

- **Envelope:** Near-instant attack at about 0.05 s. Held at a roughly constant, very loud level from 0.1 to 0.8 s, peaking near 0.3 s. It then falls gradually: about -15 dB at 0.8-0.9 s and about -25 dB by the end. There is no true silent tail, and the file ends while still audible.
- **Frequency profile:** Most energy is in the sub and bass range (about 69% below 250 Hz), with a strong band near 200-300 Hz and a low spectral centroid of about 350 Hz. Harmonics are evenly spaced up to around 2-3 kHz and look like a buzzy sawtooth or square-like waveform. Two bursts of broadband buzz reach 5-9 kHz at 0.18-0.3 s and 0.55-0.8 s. Flatness is low (0.02), so it is mostly tonal.
- **Role:** Warning or alarm for a machine (rc_warning), played from a block. It signals a fault, low power or a similar condition on a remote-controlled machine.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.07 s Very quiet lead-in, then a sudden onset of low-frequency energy at about 0.05 s.; 0.07–0.28 s Sustained buzzing tone with a fundamental near 250 Hz and evenly spaced harmonics, over a heavy sub-bass bed. A burst of high-frequency buzz appears at 0.18-0.28 s.; 0.28–0.42 s Bright, dense cluster of energy around 400-2500 Hz, the peak of the sound. It sounds like a brief, noisy inharmonic swell or glitch.; 0.42–0.54 s Tone continues, harmonics steady.; 0.54–0.80 s Second burst of high-frequency buzz, regularly pulsed in texture, up to 8 kHz. A small pitch blip near 0.73 s, after which the harmonics glide slightly upward.; 0.80–1.18 s Sustained tone and bass continue, with the highs gone and the level slowly dropping.; 1.18–1.35 s Sub-bass drops out, leaving a quieter, thinner tone around 250 Hz and its harmonics to the end.
- **Recreation notes:** Build a roughly 1.3-1.4 s one-shot. Layer a buzzy saw or square tone at about 250 Hz (with harmonics to 2-3 kHz) and a sub-bass layer at 40-120 Hz. Add two short bursts of high-frequency buzz or noise, one at about 0.2 s and one at about 0.6 s, plus a short inharmonic metallic swell near 0.3 s. Let the tone hold, then fade it out over the last 0.4 s. Keep the peaks near -1 dBFS but avoid the original's clipping, and aim for about -10 LUFS or a bit lower. Keep it mono. Note that the Qwen notes describe a descending siren, which the measurements do not support: the main tone stays near 250 Hz, so don't copy that.

### `sounds/npcs/alienbug_aggro_a.ogg`

**A gritty, chittering insectoid screech that starts as a dense burst of noisy rasping and breaks into sparse, crackly clicks that fade out over about a second.** *(noisy, gritty, organic, creature, crackle, transient, bright, hostile; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.12 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 1.0 dBFS, RMS -17.7 dBFS, -15.1 LUFS, true peak 1.2 dBTP; centroid 3550 Hz, flatness 0.073; 9 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it is centered and can be positioned and attenuated by the engine in 3D. That suits a creature aggro cue that should localize to the source.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Immediate attack, with no leading silence, into a loud sustained rasp from 0 to about 0.3 s. It drops quickly (about -20 dB by 0.07 s from the loudest point), then continues as isolated click bursts that get progressively quieter. It is about -40 dB down by roughly 0.55 s and nearly silent by 1.0 s, with a short tail ending at 1.12 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Mostly midrange (about 69% of the energy at roughly 500-4000 Hz) with substantial presence (21%), and little bass or sub. The centroid is about 3.5 kHz and rises over time to about 6.9 kHz as the tonal body fades and only bright clicks remain. Flatness is low (0.07) but there are no stable peaks, so it is a gritty, pulsed, semi-noisy texture rather than a clean tone. The early section has mottled, fluttering partial structure typical of a rasping vocalization.
- **Role:** Aggro or alert vocalization for a hostile alien bug creature, played when it notices or targets the player. The Qwen notes suggest crumpling paper, which fits the crackly click texture but not the creature role, so treat that as a timbre analogy only.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.30 s Dense, sustained rasping screech burst with strong energy at 2-5 kHz and a fluttering low body around 200-500 Hz.; 0.30–0.45 s Transition into sharper clicks. The loudest spikes land near 0.28 s and 0.39 s, and the waveform shows high-peak transients.; 0.45–0.95 s Series of discrete chittering clicks or crackles at roughly 0.53, 0.61, 0.73, 0.81 and 0.89 s, each smaller than the last, with brightness around 3-6 kHz.; 0.95–1.12 s Faint tail of very quiet crackles that dies into silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a 1.0-1.2 s one-shot with two layers. First, a 0.3 s rasping, fluttering screech from filtered noise or a processed insect or animal vocal, with energy around 300 Hz-5 kHz and fast amplitude modulation. Second, 5-8 sharp chitter clicks (shell or mandible ticks, crackled foley) that fade out over the remaining time and get brighter, with a short natural tail. Keep it dry and mono. Peaks are very hot in the original (about +1 dBFS with clipping, -15 LUFS), so aim for roughly -1 dBFS peak, around -16 LUFS, with no clipping.

### `sounds/npcs/alienbug_aggro_b.ogg`

**A short, harsh, noisy alien-bug aggro screech/chitter: a loud, dense, gritty burst lasting about 0.4 s, followed by a decaying trail of small clicky, rattling pulses.** *(noisy, gritty, creature, hostile, chittering, transient, harsh, organic; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.41 s, 22050 Hz, 1 ch; peak 1.6 dBFS, RMS -17.7 dBFS, -14.0 LUFS, true peak 2.2 dBTP; centroid 2906 Hz, flatness 0.28; 10 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it will be positioned and attenuated by the game engine. That suits a point-source creature sound that should pan and fall off naturally with distance.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack, peaking at about 0.11 s. Sustained loud plateau until about 0.35-0.4 s, then a sharp drop of about 20 dB by roughly 0.27 s after the peak. A low-level pulsing tail follows, reaching -40 dB at about 0.87 s and fading to silence by about 1.17 s, with trailing silence of about 0.24 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Mostly midrange (about 70% of energy), with the centroid near 2.9 kHz and 85% rolloff near 4.6 kHz. There is a warm, thick low-mid cluster at about 150-400 Hz in the main burst and broadband grit up to the top of the spectrum. Flatness of 0.28 means partially tonal and partially noisy, like a rough, formant-like rasp with no stable pitch lines. The tail is brighter, with centroid around 5 kHz, because it consists of small clicks.
- **Role:** Aggro or alert vocalization of the hostile alien bug NPC, played when it notices or targets the player. The Qwen notes suggest crumpling or ripping paper, which is consistent with the dry, crackly noise texture. The spectrogram shows a short vocal-like burst with a chittering tail.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.40 s Main burst: dense, raspy, noise-like screech with strong low-mid body around 200-400 Hz and bright energy at 1-4 kHz, with irregular amplitude flutter and a brief click near 0.2 s.; 0.40–1.15 s Trailing chitter: a series of small, quieter clicky or rattling pulses roughly every 0.08-0.1 s, getting progressively weaker and more sparse.; 1.15–1.41 s Faint tail fading into silence, with a few barely audible ticks.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a roughly 1.3-1.4 s one-shot. Layer a 0.4 s raspy, formant-filtered noise or growl screech, with energy around 200-400 Hz and 1-4 kHz and a bit of amplitude-modulated flutter. Add a layer of 6-8 quiet insect-like clicks or rattles at about 80-100 ms spacing, decaying over about 0.8 s. Apply a fast attack with no long reverb tail. Peak around -1 dBFS, avoiding the original's clipping, with integrated loudness near -14 LUFS. Provide a few pitch and timing variants, since this is variant 'b'.

### `sounds/npcs/alienbug_aggro_c.ogg`

**A short, gritty, growling creature screech or snarl with a strong formant-like midrange body that fades into a trail of faint clicky chitters.** *(noisy, creature, growl, vocal, gritty, formant, hostile, one-shot; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.28 s, 22050 Hz, 1 ch; peak -2.3 dBFS, RMS -19.1 dBFS, -15.3 LUFS, true peak -1.9 dBTP; centroid 2393 Hz, flatness 0.152; 4 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It is positioned and attenuated by the engine in game, so it works well as a localised 3D enemy cue that tells the player where the creature is.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Quick onset with a soft rise to a peak at about 0.17 s, sustained and ragged until about 0.4 s, then falls roughly 20 dB by 0.3 s after the peak. A low-level chitter tail runs to about 1.0 s (-40 dB at about 0.79 s) before near-silence. About 0.21 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Most energy sits in the mids (about 79% of energy, with a centroid near 2.4 kHz and 85% rolloff around 3.4 kHz). A warm, dense low-mid body at 100-700 Hz and a bright, noisy formant band at 1.5-3.5 kHz suggest a vocal or throat quality. Little sub or air. Spectral flatness of 0.15 shows a mix of tonal and noisy content. The centroid climbs in the tail as the quiet clicks and hiss dominate.
- **Role:** Aggro or alert vocalisation of a hostile alien bug mob, played when it notices or targets the player. The listening notes mention an explosion and a goat cry; these are likely misreadings of a harsh, bleating, rasping creature call. The spectrogram supports a vocal screech, not an explosion.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.06 s Very quiet low-level rustle or breath before the main vocal begins.; 0.06–0.43 s Main aggressive vocalisation: a rough, rasping snarl/screech with strong low body (about 100-700 Hz) and a bright formant band around 1.5-3.5 kHz; the loudest part, peaking near 0.17 s.; 0.43–0.60 s Rapid drop in level; residual low-mid growl tail and breathy noise.; 0.50–1.05 s Series of faint, spaced clicky chitter pulses (onsets near 0.5, 0.9 and 1.0 s) with a rattling, insect-like texture, decaying steadily.; 1.05–1.28 s Near-silent tail; trailing silence of about 0.2 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Make about 1.2-1.3 s. Layer a rasping, distorted animal or throat screech (formant around 2 kHz, body around 200-500 Hz) lasting 0.35-0.4 s with a fast attack, add a little breath noise at the start, and finish with a few soft insect-like clicks or chitters over about 0.5 s. Aim for a peak near -2 dBFS and about -15 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono, and leave a short silent tail so it does not clip off.

### `sounds/npcs/alienbug_aggro_d.ogg`

**A short, gritty, clicky alien-creature screech/chitter that opens with a low growly rasp and breaks into a decaying series of dry crackling clicks.** *(noisy, crunchy, organic, creature, chittering, transient, rasping, hostile; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.12 s, 22050 Hz, 1 ch; peak 0.8 dBFS, RMS -20.2 dBFS, -16.5 LUFS, true peak 1.4 dBTP; centroid 4357 Hz, flatness 0.305; 9 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned by the engine in 3D, so the source location is clear and distance attenuation works properly with no baked-in width.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Immediate onset with no silence, a loud sustained rasp from 0-0.35 s, a peak transient at about 0.40 s, then a stepped decay of ever-weaker clicks. It reaches -40 dB by about 0.55 s and fades to silence at about 1.08 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Most energy is in the mid and presence bands (about 39% and 56%), centroid around 4.4 kHz, rising to about 5.4 kHz late as the low rasp disappears. The first 0.4 s has a warm low-mid growl at 100-350 Hz with a modulated, blotchy texture. Above it sits broadband hissy noise at 3-7 kHz, with a faint peak near 635 Hz. Moderate flatness (0.3) means a noisy but partly pitched rasp. Almost nothing below 80 Hz.
- **Role:** The aggro/alert vocalization of the alien bug hostile mob, played when it notices the player. It is one of several variants (the 'd' variant). The Qwen paper-tearing description matches the crunchy texture, but the chitinous creature role comes from the context.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.20 s Low guttural growl/rasp with strong energy around 100-350 Hz and a bright noisy layer at 3-6 kHz, plus a faint tonal band near 650 Hz and 1.3 kHz.; 0.20–0.45 s Louder, harsher burst of rasping noise with vertical streaks, ending in the sharpest click/crack around 0.4 s (peak).; 0.45–0.85 s Sparser, quieter dry clicks and crackles at roughly 0.53, 0.61, 0.73 and 0.81 s, each with a short noisy burst.; 0.85–1.12 s Very faint trailing ticks at 0.9 and 0.99 s fading into silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Make it about 1.0-1.1 s. Layer a low throaty growl or vocal rasp (100-350 Hz) with amplitude modulation, a bright noisy screech or hiss layer (3-6 kHz), and a handful of dry insect-like clicks or crackles (e.g. crumpled cellophane, wet clicks) with a loud accent near 0.4 s followed by 4-6 decreasing clicks. Keep it mono. Target roughly -16 LUFS integrated with peaks near -1 dBFS, and avoid the clipping of the original. Keep the tail short and dry.

### `sounds/npcs/alienbug_bite_a.ogg`

**A short, wet, crunchy organic bite made of a few quick noisy chomps with a low thump, peaking around 0.13-0.2s and fading into a quiet tail.** *(organic, noisy, crunchy, transient, wet, creature, bitey; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.78 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -2.2 dBFS, RMS -24.3 dBFS, -20.8 LUFS, true peak -1.5 dBTP; centroid 4993 Hz, flatness 0.238; 4 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned by the engine, so in game it localises cleanly as a creature attack at the creature's location.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast, small first tick at about 0.05s, then the main attack builds to a peak at about 0.16s. It decays quickly (-20 dB by about 0.07s after the peak), with a smaller bump at about 0.3s. It falls to -40 dB by about 0.34s and reaches silence by about 0.6s, with no reverb tail.
- **Frequency profile:** Broadband and noisy (flatness 0.24, centroid about 5 kHz, 85% rolloff about 9.7 kHz). Energy is spread fairly evenly from mids to air, with a bass thump of about 100-400 Hz giving weight to the main bite. There is no real sub energy. The texture is crunchy and wet, with a thin high-frequency stripe near 17.8 kHz that is probably an artifact.
- **Role:** Bite attack sound for the alien bug hostile mob, played when it attacks the player. The Qwen notes about footsteps and landing are not supported by the evidence, but the short multi-hit impact and crunch fit a bite.
- **Elements:** 0.03–0.09 s Small soft pre-click/snap with mid-range energy around 700-1000 Hz, like jaws opening or a light first contact.; 0.12–0.25 s Main bite: two close, loud broadband bursts (around 0.13s and 0.19s) with strong 150-400 Hz body and bright noisy crunch up to 10 kHz.; 0.27–0.40 s Secondary smaller chomp or crunch at about 0.28-0.33s, with weaker mid and high content and a low-frequency rumble.; 0.40–0.58 s Fading low-level rattle and wet texture, with a faint last click near 0.44s.; 0.58–0.78 s Near-silent tail, mostly low-level noise, then silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Make it about 0.5-0.8s long. Layer a wet crunch or chew (celery, gristle or flesh squelch) with a quick snap or click at the start, and a short low thump (150-300 Hz) on the main hit. Add two or three tight chomp transients in the first 0.35s, with the loudest at about 0.15s. Add a little bright noise or hiss for the crunch up to about 10 kHz. Keep the tail short and dry. Aim for a peak around -2 dBFS and about -21 LUFS integrated. Make 2-3 pitch and timing variations, since this is variant 'a'.

### `sounds/npcs/alienbug_bite_b.ogg`

**A short, loud, wet and crunchy creature bite made of a few rapid chomp bursts with a noisy, bright bite texture and a thump underneath, followed by a quickly fading low rumble tail.** *(transient, noisy, crunchy, organic, creature, bite, bass-heavy, burst; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.73 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 1.3 dBFS, RMS -22.7 dBFS, -20.3 LUFS, true peak 1.8 dBTP; centroid 4428 Hz, flatness 0.198; 3 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono, so it can be positioned in 3D by the engine and will localise cleanly on the attacking creature in game.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Very fast attack of about 0.02 s to a peak that touches full scale. Three clustered bursts between 0.02 and 0.18 s. The level falls 20 dB within about 0.14 s and 40 dB by about 0.43 s. The tail is very quiet, with roughly 0.17 s of trailing near-silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Broadband and noisy with a centroid of about 4.4 kHz. The strongest energy is a low thump at 80-300 Hz in the bursts, with a lot of presence energy (32%) and air (17%) for the crunchy, wet bite texture. Moderate flatness (0.2) means a mix of tonal body and noise. There is almost no sub energy. The 17.7 kHz peak is probably an artefact.
- **Role:** Attack sound for the hostile alien bug mob when it bites the player or a target. This is one of several bite variants (b), so it should work when randomly alternated with the others. The Qwen description of gunfire or explosion is not supported by the evidence and is probably invented, though the metallic noise and impact texture are partly consistent with the measurements.
- **Elements:** 0.02–0.07 s Initial sharp bite impact, broadband and peaking near full scale, with a bright crack and low thump.; 0.07–0.13 s Second chomp burst, slightly lower in level, with a thick low-mid body around 200-500 Hz and a noisy upper texture.; 0.13–0.18 s Third chomp or crunch burst, with strong low energy and a fast fall-off in the highs.; 0.18–0.45 s Decaying low rumble and soft crunchy residue, with faint scattered clicks.; 0.45–0.72 s Near-silent tail with a few faint isolated ticks, mostly codec residue.
- **Recreation notes:** Build a layered organic bite about 0.4-0.7 s long. Layers: (1) a sharp wet crunch or chitin snap at the start, (2) two quick follow-up chomp or clack bursts at about 0.07 s and 0.13 s, (3) a low thump around 100-250 Hz for weight, (4) a short chittering or hissy noise tail that decays by about 0.4 s. Keep the bite aggressive and close. Aim for a peak around -1 dBFS (avoid the clipping in the original) and integrated loudness about -20 LUFS. Make 3-4 variations with slightly different pitch and timing.

### `sounds/npcs/alienbug_bite_c.ogg`

**A short, wet, crunchy chomp of clustered noisy clicks and snaps with a low thump underneath, fading into a quiet tail.** *(transient, noisy, crunchy, organic, bright, clicky, biting, creature; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.72 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 5.8 dBFS, RMS -21.8 dBFS, -18.9 LUFS, true peak 6.2 dBTP; centroid 5389 Hz, flatness 0.133; 2 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned and attenuated by the game engine at the creature's location, so it works well as a 3D point source for the bite.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.

- **Frequency profile:** Energy is mostly in the presence band (about 58%, roughly 2-6 kHz) and mid band (about 25%), with a centroid near 5.4 kHz and rolloff near 7.7 kHz. Some air content (about 14%) adds snap. There is a short low thump below 200 Hz in the spectrogram, but little sub or bass in the energy balance. Fairly noisy and broadband with no stable tonal peaks; flatness is 0.13. The texture is crunchy and wet.
- **Role:** Attack sound for the alien bug mob: the bite or chomp played when the hostile creature attacks. The Qwen description of crumpling or tearing packaging fits the crunchy, crinkly noise texture, but the packaging reading is not relied on.

- **Recreation notes:** Build a roughly 0.5-0.7 s one-shot from 2-3 layers. (1) Two quick crunchy snap bursts about 80 ms apart, from chitin or shell crunches, celery or crisp-snack bites, or filtered noise bursts with 2-6 kHz emphasis. (2) A short low thump of 80-200 Hz, about 80 ms long, for body. (3) A little wet clicky or crackly squelch and a quiet decaying tail. Keep the attack under 10 ms and the decay under 0.4 s. Note the original peaks above 0 dBFS (+5.8 dB, with 17 clipped samples), so make the replacement properly limited and mixed to about -18 to -16 LUFS with peaks at or below -1 dBFS. Make 2-3 pitch or timing variants to avoid repetition.

Keep a fast attack and a decay of about 0.3s. The original peaks very hot (clipped, +4.9 dBFS true peak), so aim for about -3 to -1 dBFS peak and about -20 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono, and make variants slightly different in pitch and timing. Ignore the gunshot and knife description from the listening notes, as it conflicts with the measurements and the single-event envelope.

### `sounds/npcs/alienbug_death_a.ogg`

**A loud, noisy, gurgling burst with a heavy low-end thump that decays into a long tail of wet crackles and clicks, like an alien creature dying and dissolving.** *(noisy, organic, crackle, transient, boomy, wet, hostile, decaying; confidence medium)*

It then falls about 20 dB by 0.56 s and about 40 dB by 1.66 s, with low-level crackle bumps along the way. A strong sub/bass body (about 23% below 200 Hz) sits under a bright mid/presence texture (about 54% in the mids and presence bands). The centroid is about 3 kHz in the first quarter, rises to 6-6.8 kHz as the crackle takes over, then falls. There are no stable tonal peaks.
- **Role:** Death sound for the alien bug hostile mob (npcs.alienbugDeath): a screech or squelch followed by a dissolving crackle.

The peak is at about 0.11 s.; 0.55–1.30 s Energy drops about 20-30 dB. A noisy, gurgling wash remains, with scattered mid and high content.; 1.30–2.00 s Sparse crackly clicks and pops (onsets near 1.43, 1.62, 1.79 and 1.89 s) with bright vertical strokes across 1-10 kHz, fading as the sound dissolves.; 2.00–2.17 s Very faint residual hiss and a few ticks, then near silence.

Layer (1) a 0.4-0.5 s low thump or body hit at 40-200 Hz, (2) a wet, gurgly or chittering creature screech with a bright 1-6 kHz noise burst, and (3) sparse crackles, pops and clicks spread across 1.2-2.0 s with a fading high-frequency hiss. Avoid clipping: the original peaks above 0 dBFS, so aim for a peak around -1 dBFS and about -16 LUFS integrated. Keep a fast attack and a roughly 40 dB decay over 1.5 s. Add slight pitch variation between variants.

### `sounds/npcs/alienbug_death_b.ogg`

Keep it mono, with no pitch sweep, and bright and noisy. Aim for about -14 LUFS integrated with peaks below 0 dBFS; the original clips slightly, so avoid that. Make 2-3 variants for variety.

### `sounds/npcs/alienbug_hit-01.ogg`

**A loud, noisy, wet crunchy-squelch burst like a chitinous alien bug screech-hit that decays into a series of smaller crackly clicks and gurgles over about a second.** *(noisy, crunchy, organic, transient, bright, wet, creature, crackle; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 1.03 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak 0.5 dBFS, RMS -20.5 dBFS, -18.2 LUFS, true peak 0.9 dBTP; centroid 7287 Hz, flatness 0.002; 5 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** loud. **Stereo:** Mono. It is easy to position in 3D, so the game can pan and attenuate it by the bug's location with no stereo-phase issues.. **Pitch:** unclear. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Near-instant attack (about 0.06 s to peak at 0.07 s), a short loud sustain of roughly 0.3 s, then decay to -20 dB by 0.4 s and -40 dB by 0.75 s. Sporadic small blips continue to about 0.9 s, followed by roughly 0.1 s of silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Very bright and noise-dominated (centroid about 7.3 kHz, rolloff85 about 10.7 kHz). Energy is mostly in the presence and air bands (about 78% combined) and about 21% in the mids. There is almost nothing below 200 Hz. A narrow warm band near 300-450 Hz gives a wet, vocal-like body to the later pulses. The brightness drops in the second half (centroid about 5.2 kHz). The measured flatness of 0.002 conflicts with the visibly noisy texture, so the metric is probably skewed by the dense peaks.
- **Role:** Hurt/damage reaction of the hostile alien bug NPC, played when the creature is hit.
- **Elements:** 0.01–0.30 s Main impact: dense, bright, broadband noise burst with a crackly, granular texture and a weak low-mid body around 300-450 Hz; peaks near 0.07 s and clips slightly.; 0.30–0.50 s Rapid decay of the burst into smaller crackles and clicks, with the noise thinning out and losing some high-frequency energy.; 0.50–0.80 s Several discrete lower-level gurgly or clicky pulses (onsets near 0.54, 0.67, 0.77 s), each with a tonal-ish formant band around 250-400 Hz and some harmonic stripes up to a few kHz.; 0.80–0.95 s Faint trailing crackles and a last small pulse near 0.87 s, fading to near silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a 1.0 s one-shot. Layer a sharp, crunchy noise burst (such as chitin crack, celery snap or crushed shells, high-passed above about 1.5 kHz) with a short wet squelch or gurgle with a formant around 300-400 Hz. Add 3-4 small clicky, crackly after-pulses between 0.5 and 0.9 s that get quieter. Use a fast attack and about 0.4 s of main decay. Aim for about -18 LUFS integrated with the peak under -1 dBFS, avoiding the clipping in the original. Keep it mono. Make 2-3 variations for the hit-01 style set. The Qwen 'paper crumple' impression fits the crunchy texture, but the sound should read as a creature.

The stripy, formant-like patches in the 4-10 kHz range give a chitinous, rattling, clicky texture.

- **Envelope:** Instant attack at 0 s with the loudest peak (clipped, true peak above 0 dBTP). Irregular, bursty sustain from 0.03 to about 0.65 s with a secondary swell around 0.32-0.4 s. Drops about 20 dB by 0.18 s and about 40 dB by 0.78 s, with a fading sparse tail until about 0.84 s.
- **Frequency profile:** Mostly mid and presence energy (about 41% mid, 34% presence, 21% air), with the centroid near 5.7 kHz and a rolloff around 11 kHz. Fairly noisy with a moderate flatness of 0.25 and no stable tonal peaks. Short low thumps below 200 Hz come with the bigger hits, but sub and bass content is minimal. Texture is gritty, crackly and scratchy.
- **Role:** A footstep sound for the hostile alien bug NPC (variant C of several), a skittering chitinous step with crunchy detail. The Qwen2-Audio description of crumpling or ripping paper agrees with the crackly texture, but the context points to a creature step.

- **Recreation notes:** Make a roughly 0.8 s one-shot. Layer: (1) a sharp, bright click or snap at 0 s for the footfall, (2) a crunchy layer of crushed shell, dry twigs or plastic crinkle with 2-6 kHz emphasis at 0.3 s, (3) 3-6 tiny skittering ticks spread across 0.05-0.65 s, (4) a faint low thump under the big hits at 60-150 Hz. ### `sounds/npcs/apache_burst.ogg`

**A heavily clipped, loud burst of rumbling noise and low-frequency chop, like a rotor or machine-gun burst, that swells for about 0.8 s and then decays into a low rumbling tail.** *(noisy, boomy, mechanical, distorted, clipped, rumble, pulsing, explosive; confidence medium)*

- **Envelope:** Immediate loud onset with a sustained plateau from 0 to about 0.8 s, peaking around 0.42 s. It falls about 20 dB by roughly 0.56 s after the peak and about 40 dB by 1.56 s. A long, low-passed tail runs to about 2.07 s, followed by 0.15 s of silence.

The first 0.8 s is bright and noisy up to about 10 kHz, while the tail is mostly below 300 Hz. Flatness is low (0.064) because of the heavy low-frequency weighting. There are no clear tonal peaks, so the sound reads as a noisy, distorted texture.
- **Role:** Hostile NPC attack sound for an Apache helicopter's gun or missile burst. It combines a rapid-fire chop with an explosive low-end impact and a rumbling decay.

Energy reaches up to about 10 kHz.; 0.85–1.60 s Sharp drop in level and high-frequency content. A mid and low rumble with crackly debris decays, with minor onsets near 1.33 s.; 1.60–2.07 s Low-frequency rumble and chop tail, centroid under about 250 Hz, fading to near silence.; 2.07–2.22 s Trailing silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Layer a short automatic-weapon or rotor-chop burst with about 10-12 pulses at roughly 11 Hz over 0.8 s, a low sub thump, and filtered noise for the rumble tail. Make the tail low-passed to about 300 Hz and fade it over about 1.2 s for a total of about 2.0-2.2 s. The original is clipped and well above full scale, with a DC offset, so avoid copying that. Aim for about -10 to -14 LUFS with peaks at or below -1 dBFS, and keep it mono.

### `sounds/npcs/apache_die.ogg`

The level falls about 12 dB by 0.3 s, then sits in a lower plateau of about -20 dB until roughly 2.5 s. It decays through -20 dB at 1.73 s and -40 dB at 5.09 s, with a quiet tail and about 0.4 s of trailing silence.

Little air above 10 kHz.
- **Role:** Death sound for the Apache enemy, probably a helicopter. It is a crash or explosion with failing engine and rotor whine. It is likely clipped or distorted.; 0.00–1.10 s Three harmonic-rich buzzy pulses at roughly 0.1, 0.5 and 0.9 s, seen as stacked horizontal bars between 1 and 5 kHz. They sound like electronic or mechanical beeps, stuttering rotor or alarm-like bursts.; 0.00–3.00 s Thin, steady high whine near 6 kHz that drifts slightly, as if a turbine or engine winding down.; 1.00–4.50 s Sustained noisy rumble at lower level, with faint wavering tonal bands around 1 to 2 kHz and pulsing mid-high flutter. It sounds like debris, engine or rotor decay.; 4.50–5.60 s Fading tail where the highs roll off first and the sound dies into near silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a roughly 6 s one-shot with these layers: (1) a heavy explosion or crash hit with sub thump and distorted noise, with a very fast attack; (2) three short buzzy harmonic pulses in the first second, like a failing alarm or rotor stutter; (3) a thin high turbine whine that falls slightly and fades by about 3 s; (4) a mid-low engine and debris rumble with flutter that decays over 4 to 5 s. Peak around -1 dBFS, avoiding the original's clipping, and aim for about -13 LUFS integrated. Keep it mono, and roll the highs off over the tail.

### `sounds/npcs/apache_explode.ogg`

**A massive, heavily clipped noise-based explosion with a boomy sub-heavy body that decays into a long rumbling debris tail over about 6 seconds.** *(explosion, boomy, noisy, rumble, clipped, low-end heavy, long tail, impact; confidence high)*

It sustains at full level to about 0.3 s, then falls roughly 10 dB per tenth of the file. Early on the noise extends to 10 kHz and above, with a centroid of about 690 Hz in the first quarter. The spectrum then darkens quickly to about 290 Hz, about 100 Hz and about 57 Hz by the last quarter. It is low-flatness overall because of the strong low-end content, but the texture is noisy. There are no stable tonal peaks.

- **Recreation notes:** Make it about 6-7 s long. Layer a sub boom (30-80 Hz, pitched down, with an exponential decay of about 1.5 s), a broadband noise blast with a fast lowpass sweep from about 10 kHz to 500 Hz, a mid-range fireball crack at the start, and a debris/crackle and rumble tail with irregular small hits for 2-5 s. Add a short low-end tail with a little reverb. Avoid the original's clipping (peak +9.6 dBTP, 17k clipped samples) by limiting at about -1 dBFS, and aim for a loud but controlled level around -8 to -10 LUFS integrated, leaving about 0.5 s of silent tail.

The flatness figure of 0.0 reflects the low-frequency dominance, not a pure tone.
- **Role:** Footstep of the large hostile Cyberdemon mob, one variant (c) of several step sounds played in rotation as it walks. It should feel heavy and imposing. Qwen's description of gravel footsteps is only loosely consistent; the spectrogram shows a boomy low-end thud, not gritty gravel.
- **Elements:** 0.01–0.12 s Initial lower-level rumbling scuffs/contact pulses, with bass-heavy energy around 100-1000 Hz and a few mid-frequency flecks.; 0.12–0.23 s Main impact: loudest sub and bass swell made of several fast pulses, with a brief bright burst around 5-10 kHz near 0.16 s.; 0.23–0.40 s Decaying low thumps and rumble, with a weaker secondary pulse near 0.31-0.33 s.; 0.40–0.50 s Very faint residual debris and tail ticks, barely audible.
- **Recreation notes:** Build a roughly 0.5 s one-shot. Layer 1: a sub thump of about 40-80 Hz with a fast attack and a decay of around 0.2 s. Layer 2: a low body impact or stomp of 100-400 Hz, with a few quick staggered pulses to suggest a heavy, mechanical or armored foot. Layer 3: a very short, subtle crunch or metal click at 3-10 kHz at the peak. Add a quiet debris tail that fades by 0.5 s. Aim for a peak near -3 dBFS, an RMS around -18 dBFS and about -21 LUFS. Make 3 or more variants with slight pitch and timing differences so repeated steps don't sound identical.

### `sounds/npcs/cyberdemon_step_d.ogg`

**A heavy, low-frequency boomy footstep thud with a rumbling, crunchy body that swells to a peak around 0.15s and decays quickly, with faint high-frequency ticks at the very end.** *(boomy, heavy, thud, rumbling, impact, low-end, gritty, one-shot; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.53 s, 44100 Hz, 1 ch; peak -2.9 dBFS, RMS -20.5 dBFS, -22.7 LUFS, true peak -2.8 dBTP; centroid 407 Hz, flatness 0.0; 1 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned and attenuated by the game engine, which suits a 3D-located enemy footstep. The heavy sub content means distance rolloff should be tuned so it stays felt at range.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast but not instant swell: the attack takes about 0.14s to peak at 0.15s. The decay is quick, reaching -20 dB about 0.09s after the peak and -40 dB by about 0.22s. The tail is essentially silent after about 0.35s, and the file ends with about 0.03s of silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Dominated by sub and bass: about 65% of the energy is below roughly 60 Hz and about 25% in the bass band, with a centroid around 400 Hz and an 85% rolloff near 130 Hz. Mids and highs are sparse and low in level. The texture is a gritty, crunchy low rumble. The high peaks at 15-16 kHz are very low in level and likely artifacts. The flatness reading of 0.0 reflects the dominant low-frequency content.
- **Role:** A footstep of the Cyberdemon, a huge hostile mob (variant 'd' of several step sounds). It conveys massive weight and mechanical or heavy-armored stomping.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.05 s Very quiet lead-in with a soft low-frequency onset building up.; 0.05–0.20 s Main impact: a dense, gritty cluster of sub and bass thuds that swells to a peak near 0.15s, with a mid-range crunchy scrape on top (about 200 Hz to 3 kHz).; 0.20–0.32 s Secondary rumble and debris tail, with a smaller bump around 0.22s; energy falls steadily.; 0.32–0.53 s Near-silent tail with a few faint high-frequency ticks (around 10-16 kHz) at 0.43-0.52s, probably codec artifacts rather than real content.
- **Recreation notes:** Make it about 0.4-0.5s long. Layer a deep sub thud (roughly 40-80 Hz, slightly pitched down) with a slower attack of about 0.1s; add a gritty, crunchy mid layer of gravel, metal or debris (200 Hz-3 kHz) for the scrape; and a short low rumble tail. Roll off almost everything above 5 kHz. Target a peak around -3 dBFS and about -22 LUFS integrated. Make 3-4 variants with slight pitch and timing changes so successive steps don't repeat. The Qwen notes describe gravel and twigs; that fits the crunchy texture but not the huge scale, so lean on the measured sub weight.

### `sounds/npcs/turret_beep-01.ogg`

**A roughly 2-second robotic turret chatter made of a steady train of short electronic ticks over a constant 7.3 kHz whine, with a cluster of pitched, vocal-like beeps in the middle and a hard cutoff near 2.05 s.** *(electronic, beepy, tonal, metallic, robotic, pulsing, bright, chirpy; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 2.24 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -12.4 dBFS, RMS -30.0 dBFS, -26.8 LUFS, true peak -12.3 dBTP; centroid 4174 Hz, flatness 0.001; 14 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** quiet. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned and attenuated by the game engine, so the turret's location is clear and the sound stays localized.. **Pitch:** stepped. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** There is no single attack: the sound is a sequence of about 14 short bursts roughly 0.1 s apart, each with a fast onset and a quick decay (about 0.11 s to -20 dB). The loudest section is 0.3-0.9 s, with the peak near 0.8 s. Levels dip in the middle, around -42 to -45 dB per tenth-second at 0.5-0.7 of the file, then rise again. It cuts off abruptly at about 2.05 s, followed by 0.14 s of trailing near-silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the mids and presence region (about 46% mid, 44% presence, 9% low-mid), with a centroid around 4 kHz. A very steady narrow tone at about 7.27 kHz runs throughout. The beeps have clear harmonic ladders (about 1 kHz, 2.7 kHz and higher). Flatness is very low, so the sound is tonal, with little real sub or bass content and only a faint broadband haze below 500 Hz.
- **Role:** Hostile turret or robot NPC chatter/beep: an idle or targeting vocalization played while the turret is active or alerted (event npcs.turretBeep, hostile category).
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.38 s Rapid series of soft, short ticks/blips (about every 0.11 s) riding on a faint steady high whine near 7.3 kHz.; 0.38–0.90 s Louder cluster of three pitched, multi-harmonic beeps/warbles (fundamental around 800-1000 Hz with harmonics up to about 5 kHz), each slightly stepping up, like a robotic babble.; 0.90–1.75 s Return to quieter, regular ticks with the high whine continuing; the final beep at about 0.85 s has a bright harmonic stack.; 1.75–2.05 s Last harmonic-rich beep around 1.8-1.9 s, then a final tick near 1.92 s, and the ticks and whine stop abruptly at about 2.05 s.; 2.05–2.24 s Near-silent tail with only a faint residual high tone.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a 2.0-2.2 s one-shot with three layers. First, a steady high sine or whine around 7 kHz, very low in level. Second, a train of tiny square or triangle ticks every ~0.1 s with fast decays of 50-100 ms. Third, 3-4 mid-register synthetic beeps (fundamental 800-1100 Hz with rich harmonics, FM or formant-like) stepping upward around the 0.4-0.9 s mark, with a smaller one near 1.8 s. Keep it mono, with integrated loudness around -27 LUFS and peaks near -12 dBFS, and cut off cleanly or with a very short fade. The Qwen notes about static noise and 10-second timings conflict with the measurements (2.2 s, tonal), so they were disregarded apart from the retro-electronic character.

### `sounds/npcs/turret_beep-02.ogg`

**A short, bright electronic double-beep: two quick tonal chirps with a faint tick between them, then the same pattern repeated about 0.38 s later, like a turret's targeting ping.** *(tonal, electronic, beep, bright, metallic, transient, short, repeating; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.62 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -14.0 dBFS, RMS -35.0 dBFS, -31.3 LUFS, true peak -13.9 dBTP; centroid 6845 Hz, flatness 0.001; 3 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** quiet. **Stereo:** Mono, so in game it will be positioned by the engine and attenuate with distance. That suits a localised hostile-mob cue the player needs to locate.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Each beep has an instant attack (under 5 ms) and a held plateau of about 20 ms. It drops 20 dB within about 20 ms and 40 dB within about 50 ms. A smaller blip follows about 25 ms later. The two groups are about 0.38 s apart, and the second is the louder one. The tail is nearly silent and ends with about 0.08 s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** The energy is tonal (flatness 0.001) and sits in the presence and mid bands (about 54% and 29%), with some air (17%) and almost no bass. There are narrow horizontal lines at about 1.2 kHz, 4 kHz, 7.9 kHz and 9.3 kHz, which are harmonic-like partials giving a hard, glassy or metallic digital timbre. The centroid is about 6.8 kHz. There is a brief low-frequency thump at each onset.
- **Role:** A hostile turret NPC's warning or targeting beep, played repeatedly while it tracks or charges. The Qwen notes about a ping-pong ball and a ticking clock agree with the short, ticking, metallic character, but the sports interpretation is unsupported, and its timings (0.59-1.08 s) exceed the file's 0.62 s length.
- **Elements:** 0.01–0.03 s First beep: a sharp tonal burst with a fundamental near 1.2 kHz and strong partials at about 4 kHz, 7.9 kHz and 9.3 kHz, plus a low thump below 200 Hz at the onset.; 0.04–0.06 s Quieter, shorter second blip with the same pitch content, giving a 'bi-dip' feel.; 0.12–0.20 s Very faint low-level tonal tick and low rumble residue, barely audible.; 0.39–0.41 s Repeat of the main beep at the loudest point (peak about -14 dBFS), with the same harmonic stack and low thump.; 0.42–0.44 s Quieter secondary blip, as in the first group.; 0.44–0.62 s Very quiet fading tonal tail around 1.2 kHz and 4 kHz, with faint low-frequency rumble.
- **Recreation notes:** Build a two-pulse electronic beep from a square or FM tone around 1.2 kHz, with added upper partials at about 4, 8 and 9 kHz. Give each pulse a 1-2 ms attack and about 20 ms of sustain, then a fast decay. Add a quieter echo blip about 25 ms later and a very short low thump (under 200 Hz) at the onset. Total length about 0.6 s with two beeps spaced about 0.38 s apart, or deliver a single beep group so the game can retrigger it. Peak around -14 dBFS, integrated loudness about -31 LUFS, so it sits quietly under other effects.

### `sounds/npcs/turret_beep-03.ogg`

**A very short electronic turret beep: a steady ~5.9 kHz pure tone chopped into a rapid stutter of about eight pulses that shrink in level, then a louder final blip with a brief tail.** *(electronic, tonal, beep, high-pitched, pulsed, sci-fi, mechanical, alert; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.84 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -18.5 dBFS, RMS -29.1 dBFS, -25.3 LUFS, true peak -17.8 dBTP; centroid 5928 Hz, flatness 0.0; 6 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. It will be positioned by the engine as a point source, so the turret's location is clear as the player approaches or moves around it.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Instant attack at 0 s. The gated pulses sit around -28 to -33 dB in the 0.1-0.7 s windows, with a loudest short pulse near 0.5-0.6 s (about -24 dB). Abrupt cutoff at ~0.63 s, then a ~0.1 s very quiet tail (-57 dB or lower). The signal is below -40 dB by about 0.24 s after the peak.
- **Frequency profile:** Almost all energy (99.8%) sits in a narrow line near 5.9 kHz (centroid 5928 Hz, flatness ~0), a pure sine-like tone. Weak secondary components: a faint ~500 Hz band during the pulses, thin high harmonics or aliasing near 13 and 19 kHz, and a hash of broadband low-level noise from the gating. Little real low-end content.
- **Role:** Hostile turret beep or targeting or warning chirp, played when the turret acquires or tracks a target. This is variant 03 of a set.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.04 s Opening louder blip of the ~5.9 kHz tone with a faint ~10.9 kHz click at the very start.; 0.06–0.50 s Rapid stuttering pulses of the same tone (onsets near 0.08, 0.16, 0.27, 0.35, 0.43, 0.51 s), getting narrower and slightly quieter, like a tremolo or gated chirp. A weak ~500 Hz buzz band rides along with each pulse.; 0.53–0.63 s Final, louder pulse with a broadband click at about 0.53 s, then a short swelling sustain that cuts off at ~0.63 s.; 0.63–0.74 s Very faint residual tone tail, fading to silence by ~0.84 s.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a ~0.6-0.85 s one-shot. Use a clean sine or soft-square near 5-6 kHz, gated by 6-8 fast pulses (about 80 ms apart) that tighten and decay, then finish with a slightly louder, longer blip. Add a faint ~500 Hz square buzz layer and a tiny click at the transition. Keep it mono, with peak around -18 dBFS and about -25 LUFS integrated. Vary the pulse count or pitch slightly between variants.

### `sounds/npcs/turret_beep-04.ogg`

**A rapid train of about thirteen short, bright electronic beeps with a metallic, buzzy tone, like a turret's warning chirp repeating at roughly 25 pulses per second.** *(beep, electronic, tonal, metallic, pulsed, bright, rapid, mechanical; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.55 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -15.1 dBFS, RMS -28.1 dBFS, -24.5 LUFS, true peak -15.1 dBTP; centroid 6829 Hz, flatness 0.0; 6 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. In game it can be positioned in 3D at the turret, so distance panning and attenuation will locate the threat clearly.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Each burst has a near-instant attack and a short sustain of about 30-40 ms, then it drops sharply into a gap. The gaps are not fully silent but sit 20+ dB down. The overall train is level, with no build or fade until the last tiny blip at about 0.52 s. The file ends at 0.555 s with essentially no tail.
- **Frequency profile:** Almost no energy below 1 kHz. A strong band sits near 1.2 kHz, with a very strong tonal line near 4 kHz and the loudest peaks at about 7.9 kHz and 9.3 kHz. The spectrum is harmonic-like (4k, 8k) with a bright, buzzy, metallic color. Centroid is about 6.8 kHz, and 54% of energy is in the presence band, 30% in the mid and 16% in the air band. Flatness is near 0, so the sound is tonal. Faint broadband vertical smearing at each burst comes from the gating clicks.
- **Role:** A hostile turret's warning or targeting beep, played when it detects or locks onto the player. The 'beep-04' name suggests one of several variations picked at random. The Qwen notes mention a bell-like chime and pitch changes, but the spectrogram shows steady pitch, so I trust the measurements over those notes.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.50 s Train of about 13 evenly spaced tone bursts, each roughly 40 ms long with a ~40 ms gap, repeating at a steady rate of about 12-13 pulses per second (period ~0.04-0.08 s). Each burst has the same pitch and timbre.; 0.50–0.56 s A final, much quieter faint blip or tail fragment, then silence.
- **Recreation notes:** Build a gated square or pulse-like synth tone at about 4 kHz with strong upper harmonics near 8 kHz, plus a faint 1.2 kHz layer for body. Gate it with a hard envelope of 12-13 pulses at roughly 40 ms on and 40 ms off. Keep the pitch constant, use a tiny 1-2 ms fade on each edge to avoid clicks, and add no reverb. Total length is about 0.5-0.55 s. Target about -24 LUFS integrated with a peak near -15 dBFS, mono. Make a few variants with slight pulse-rate or pitch differences.

### `sounds/npcs/turret_beep-05.ogg`

**A very short, bright electronic turret beep made of a few tonal chirp segments with a mid-section dip and brief gaps, ending abruptly.** *(electronic, tonal, bright, beep, synthetic, buzzy, short, sci-fi; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.32 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -16.9 dBFS, RMS -26.3 dBFS, -70.0 LUFS, true peak -14.9 dBTP; centroid 8802 Hz, flatness 0.004; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. This suits a positional 3D emitter such as a turret, which the engine can pan and attenuate by distance.. **Pitch:** stepped. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Instant onset with no real fade-in, then roughly flat sustain for about 0.29 s with small level dips between segments near 0.075 s and 0.13 s. Abrupt cutoff near 0.295 s, followed by about 0.02-0.04 s of near-silence. Essentially no decay or reverb tail.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the presence and air bands (about 68% and 32%), with a centroid near 8.8 kHz and rolloff near 15.5 kHz. Very tonal (flatness 0.004). Prominent steady lines sit near 5.3 kHz, 7.9 kHz and 12-15 kHz, over a faint harmonic comb of buzz partials. Almost no sub or bass energy.
- **Role:** Hostile turret beep, a short targeting or alert blip played by an enemy turret NPC. One of several variants (beep-05) for variation.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.07 s Bright buzzy tone with strong high partials near 8 and 12 kHz over a harmonic comb, steady pitch.; 0.08–0.13 s Tone shifts to a lower, purer beep centered near 5 kHz with a weaker partial near 2.5 kHz; louder in the waveform, with gaps on each side.; 0.13–0.21 s Returns to a dense, buzzy harmonic texture with energy around 6-12 kHz and a pulsing low-mid flutter around 500-700 Hz.; 0.22–0.29 s Repeat of the opening bright tone with strong lines near 8 and 12 kHz, then a hard cut-off.; 0.29–0.32 s Near-silent tail with faint residual noise.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a ~0.3 s one-shot. Layer a square or pulse synth beep at about 5 kHz with a brighter buzzy tone at 8-12 kHz, and step between two or three pitches in a stuttering pattern (~75 ms, ~50 ms, ~85 ms, ~75 ms segments). Add tiny gaps, a hard stop and no reverb. Keep it mono, with peak around -17 dBFS and RMS near -26 dBFS. Make 4-6 variants with slight pitch shifts. The listening notes mention music and a 30 s duration, which conflicts with the 0.3 s measured file, so ignore that.

### `sounds/npcs/turret_beep-06.ogg`

**A very short electronic turret chirp: a rapid three-step ascending blip (about 1.5k, 3k, then 5k Hz) lasting roughly 0.07s, followed by a faint high-frequency ringing tail.** *(electronic, tonal, beep, chirp, transient, bright, robotic, short; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.31 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -9.5 dBFS, RMS -21.6 dBFS, -70.0 LUFS, true peak -9.5 dBTP; centroid 4543 Hz, flatness 0.0; 1 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. It can be positioned in 3D at the turret, so the piercing high chirp is easy to localize as a warning cue.. **Pitch:** stepped. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Fast attack of about 0.02s to the peak, a short sustain through about 0.07s, then a sharp drop of 40 dB by about 0.06s. The remainder is a near-silent tail up to 0.31s, with 0.12s of trailing silence.
- **Frequency profile:** Energy is concentrated in the mid and presence bands, about 20% mid and 80% presence, with a centroid near 4.5 kHz and rolloff near 5.3 kHz. Flatness is near 0, so it is clearly tonal. Narrow peaks sit at about 2.9, 4.8 and 5.3 kHz, and there is almost nothing below 1 kHz. The Qwen description of squeaks plus continuous buzzing is not supported by the measurements. The faint tail lines are probably codec or ringing artifacts.
- **Role:** A hostile turret's beep or targeting chirp, played as a short alert while it tracks or fires. The numbered variant (-06) suggests it is one of several randomized beeps.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.03 s First blip with a tone around 1.5 kHz and a fast swell, with a soft broadband splash of energy above it.; 0.03–0.04 s Second step up to a tone near 3 kHz, with a hint of 5 kHz.; 0.04–0.07 s Third and fourth steps at about 4.8 to 5.3 kHz, the brightest and most piercing part, ending in an abrupt cutoff.; 0.07–0.31 s Very faint residual ringing at about 1.5k, 3k and 5k Hz at roughly -50 to -70 dB, essentially inaudible next to the chirp.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a 0.07 to 0.1s square or sine blip sequence with three or four quick steps rising from about 1.5k to 5k Hz. Add a slight click or noise burst at the start and a hard cutoff with a very short fade. Keep a peak near -9 dBFS, and if desired, add a very quiet 0.2s ringing tail at -60 dB. Vary the step pitches across the numbered variants.

### `sounds/npcs/turret_beep-07.ogg`

**A very short, extremely bright electronic chirp-buzz made of fast-pulsing high-frequency tones that cuts off abruptly at about 0.16 s and leaves a faint tail.** *(electronic, beep, bright, buzzy, tonal, metallic, short, mechanical; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.26 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -13.4 dBFS, RMS -29.2 dBFS, -70.0 LUFS, true peak -12.8 dBTP; centroid 14639 Hz, flatness 0.009; 0 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** quiet. **Stereo:** Mono. It sits at the center of the sound field and is easy to position as a 3D point source on the turret, so it localizes well in game.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Almost immediate onset, with a sustained plateau of roughly -25 to -31 dB from 0 to about 0.15 s and a few bursty level dips. A sharp cutoff near 0.16 s drops it by about 20 dB or more, then it fades to near silence by 0.26 s. Almost no reverb tail.

These are likely modulation sidebands or codec artifacts rather than real content, and they are barely audible.

Add a very short fade or cutoff at the end. Keep a tiny mid-frequency body so it stays audible on small speakers, since the original is almost entirely very high frequency. Peak around -13 dBFS, with a quiet overall level (RMS about -29 dBFS). Make 8 to 10 variants with slight pitch and pulse differences. The Qwen notes about static and a pitch drop are not supported by the measurements, which show steady pitch.

### `sounds/npcs/turret_gunequip-01.ogg`

**A short burst of sharp metallic clicks and clacks, like a gun being cocked or a mechanism racking, building to a heavier clunk around 0.55 s and then fading quickly.** *(transient, metallic, mechanical, clicky, bright, noisy, layered; confidence medium)*

- **Measured:** 0.81 s, 48000 Hz, 1 ch; peak -3.0 dBFS, RMS -19.4 dBFS, -14.8 LUFS, true peak -3.0 dBTP; centroid 5356 Hz, flatness 0.035; 5 onset(s).
- **Perceived loudness:** moderate. **Stereo:** Mono. It sits centered and positions cleanly with game panning and distance attenuation, so it works as a localized sound from the NPC.. **Pitch:** none. **Loop:** one-shot.
- **Envelope:** Multiple sharp attacks (onsets at about 0.06, 0.18, 0.27, 0.38 and 0.46 s), each decaying within 30-80 ms. The overall peak is near 0.54 s. Decay to -20 dB takes about 0.05 s and to -40 dB about 0.15 s. The tail after 0.6 s is short, and the last ~0.12 s is silent.
- **Frequency profile:** Mid-dominant (about 45% in mid, 35% in presence, 18% in air), with almost no sub or bass energy. The centroid is about 5.4 kHz and the 85% rolloff about 8.5 kHz. The brightest energy is 1-6 kHz, with short low thumps around 100-250 Hz under the bigger clacks. Flatness of 0.035 is low, but the spectrogram shows broadband click bursts rather than steady tones, so the texture is metallic and clicky. There is a faint tonal line near 16 kHz.
- **Role:** The turret NPC equipping or arming its gun: a mechanical cock/rack and deploy sequence. This is inferred from the event name 'turretGunequip'. The measurements support a multi-part mechanism sound. Qwen's 'camera shutter' reading matches the sharp mechanical clicks but not the role.
- **Elements:** 0.00–0.10 s Two small, soft metallic ticks at about 0.01 s and 0.07 s, bright but quiet.; 0.18–0.20 s Isolated light tick after a short gap, a lead-in to the main action.; 0.21–0.38 s Three louder metallic clacks at roughly 0.22, 0.27 and 0.33 s, each with a ringing body at 1-5 kHz and a low thump near 150-250 Hz. The third is the biggest.; 0.38–0.45 s Two small, sharp ticks at about 0.41 and 0.44 s.; 0.46–0.62 s Dense cluster of overlapping clacks building to the loudest, heaviest clunk around 0.54-0.57 s, with a low thump and a bright metallic rattle.; 0.62–0.81 s Fast fade of faint metallic residue into near-silence, with the last ~0.12 s silent.
- **Recreation notes:** Make a roughly 0.8 s one-shot with 5-7 discrete mechanical hits. Layer small metal ticks and latch clicks, such as light servo or ratchet snaps, for the early events. Build to a heavier slide or bolt clack around 0.5-0.55 s, adding a short low thump of about 150-250 Hz. Keep the tail brief and end with a little silence. Target a peak of about -3 dBFS and about -15 LUFS integrated, in mono, with most energy at 1-8 kHz.
